- Dòng dữ liệu: Objective; Observations made; Comments and lessons

### 28 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

learned 1. Clinic set-up and infrastructure • Space adequacy • Client ﬂow • Ventilation • Cleanliness • Available washroom • Linkage to laboratory, pharmacy, etc. • Linkage to ultrasonography services 2. Client–provider interactions • Welcome/reception • Client comfort • Waiting time • Privacy/conﬁdentiality • Interpersonal skills • Partner/support person involvement 3. Care components • Quick check/RAM oﬀered • Client assessment: history, exam and labs • Preventive care: SP/IPT, TT, Fe/Folic

• HIV test/PMTCT • Counselling: birth preparedness planning

4. Documentation • Client clinical record • Clinic registers /logs • Clinic summary indicators • Information charts

5. Emergency care • Emergency drugs • Trolleys • Referral system

## 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Alternate class activity if a site visit is not possible: group work (20 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In this exercise, participants will identify any differences between their respective ANC set-ups and the

ideal ANC clinic set-up and organization described in Session 2.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask participants to think critically about their own clinic setting, its set-up, and the way that antenatal

care is provided. They will have to compare their scenarios to all that they have learned so far about the optimal clinic organization for providing focused antenatal care and make practical suggestions for improving care.

Instructions • Divide the class into 4–5 working groups, each comprising 4–6 members.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assign each group a facilitator to supervise the group’s activity.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Instruct each group to identify a chairperson to facilitate group discussion and appoint a member to

### 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

report the outcome of group work to the class.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assign each group an area of the clinic set-up/organization to review, using the five key areas listed on

the feedback form as a guide.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide them with the feedback form to use in preparing the group feedback.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Allow 30 minutes for group work; encourage active participation by all group members.

All groups will present their findings for 10 minutes.

Debriefing summary and conclusions After each group has presented their respective findings and recommendations, briefly summarize the favourable and unfavourable observations made and highlight the key recommendations.

### 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Facilitate a brainstorming discussion (5–10 minutes) on major common observations. Ask participants to share experiences on how they have overcome challenges in the following areas: • improving clinic infrastructure and set-up • making services more client-friendly • implementing effective workplace and administrative procedures (such as convenient clinic hours, infection prevention and cleaning schedules, record keeping, and staff schedules).

Create a list of shared experiences/best practices and interventions that have worked and encourage other participants to implement these in their settings, where required and feasible. Also encourage participants to document their good practices so they can be shared more broadly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 31

Summary and conclusions

### 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Present slides 21–23 and ask a participant to read out the key messages

Key messages (5 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The organization of an antenatal clinic affects the quality of antenatal care and influences a woman’s decision

on whether to use the facility. It must provide a client-friendly environment and the space, privacy, equipment, supplies and medicines required to ensure a quality ANC visit.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Key considerations in organizing a client-friendly antenatal clinic include:

o providing auditory and visual privacy o respecting and assuring confidentiality o scheduling clinic days to ensure that clients, their partners and the community are able to utilize services maximally o promoting partner/support person participationo ensuring that clients are able to establish rapport with and see the same care provider at subsequent visits.

### 30 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The routinely collated summary data on antenatal care can be classified under three domains: (1) services

utilization; (2) screening; and (3) interventions. These data are analysed regularly to determine the levels of key monitoring indicators of clinic performance.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Antenatal care providers must work with facility managers and stakeholders within the health care facility,

district and community. They should work together to mobilize resources, including community leaders and members, to support a client-friendly antenatal clinic, ensure good quality of care, and encourage pregnant women to come for ANC and childbirth care.

## 32 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

Welcome and introduction to the course

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 or ask a participant to read out the aim and objectives of the session AIM To enable participants to provide quality routine antenatal care

### 32 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Objectives On completion of this session, participants will be able to: 1. describe the steps in the provision of ANC 2. assess the pregnant woman for pregnancy status and screen her for specific problems 3. assist pregnant women to achieve a healthy pregnancy outcome 4. list the risk factors (individual, community and health services) related to the antenatal period and identify avoidable factors.

5. strengthen care provision to women with special needs: adolescents, survivors of gender-based violence, persons with disabilities, and displaced women living in humanitarian settings.

Introduction

Slide 4: Quick check

### 32 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Present slide 4 • Care for a pregnant woman should always begin with a quick check every time she is seen. • The quick check is performed by the first person with responsibility for receiving pregnant women seeking care. • A quick check is a brief assessment of the pregnant woman to quickly identify any signs that indicate serious problems requiring emergency care and taking immediate appropriate action to minimize delays in providing life-saving care.

• Ask participants to name possible danger signs and conditions in pregnancy that need emergency care and write the answers on a flip chart.

Refer participants to ‘Quick check’ in PCPNC B2

- Dòng dữ liệu: anaemia; (C4); visit. In high prevalence settings, haemoglobin

8 million persons infected.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; It presents signiﬁcant morbidity for pregnant

### 34 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

mothers and their babies. Syphilis in pregnancy leads to over 300 000 foetal and neonatal deaths each year and places an additional 215 000 infants at increased risk of early death globally.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The regional target is that at least 95% of pregnant

women are screened for syphilis and 95% of syphilis- seropositive pregnant women are treated with at least one dose of intramuscular benzathine penicillin or another eﬀective regimen.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Every pregnant woman must be tested for syphilis at

the ﬁrst antenatal visit and at each subsequent visit.

## Pcpnc (C6)

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Check for; PCPNC; Provide the following information:

- Dòng dữ liệu: hepatitis; reference; • There are over 60 million people chronically infected

- Dòng dữ liệu: B virus; documents; with HBV in Africa, with up to 350 000 infections in

- Dòng dữ liệu: (HBV); do not; children under 5 years (2015).

- Dòng dữ liệu: infection; contain; • Over 90% of new hepatitis B infections are passed

- Dòng dữ liệu: guidelines; from mothers to their children and in early

- Dòng dữ liệu: for HBV; childhood. The risk of chronic infection is higher in

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 2

these cases. • Every pregnant woman should be asked about her hepatitis B virus infection status at the ﬁrst antenatal visit. If this is not known, the woman is counselled and oﬀered HBV testing. • Pregnant women who test positive should be given prophylactic tenofovir from 28 weeks gestation. • All infants should receive their ﬁrst dose of hepatitis B vaccine as soon as possible after birth, preferably within 24 hours. • The infant may also be given hepatitis B

immunoglobulin (HBIG) at the same time if there is a high risk of infection. • The woman must also be referred and/or evaluated and followed up for her liver function and well-being.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assess; Refer to PCPNC; •; Sexually transmitted infections (STIs) continue to be

- Dòng dữ liệu: for other; (C9); a highly endemic public health challenge in the

- Dòng dữ liệu: STIs; African Region.

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 3

• The total number of new cases for the four curable STIs (chlamydia, gonorrhoea, trichomoniasis and syphilis) in the Region was estimated at 63 million in 2012, representing 18% of the global incidence. • Assessing all pregnant women for abnormal vaginal discharge and ulcers presents an opportunity to identify and treat these curable infections.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Check for; Refer to PCPNC; • The burden of TB infection is high in the sub-Saharan

- Dòng dữ liệu: tuberculosis; (C11); African region and often compounded by HIV

- Dòng dữ liệu: (TB); coinfection.

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 4

• TB infection during pregnancy is associated with poor outcomes for both the mother and her baby, including IUGR, preterm birth and low birth weight for the baby, as well as poor maternal health and death when the disease is not treated. • Maternal care services provide an important platform for screening, detecting and treating TB in pregnant women. • Every pregnant woman attending ANC should be screened for TB using symptom-based screening: (cough lasting >2 weeks, haemoptysis, weight loss,

fever, night sweats).

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assess; Refer to slides; Present and discuss slide 7 on “Assessing nutritional

- Dòng dữ liệu: nutritional; 7 and 8; status” and slide 8 on “Recommended weight gain”

- Dòng dữ liệu: status; during pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 37

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assessment; Reference

Additional comments/information

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: component; learning

materials

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assess for; Refer to; •; Gender-based violence during pregnancy is

- Dòng dữ liệu: gender-based; PCPNC (H4); associated with increased risk of trauma, sexually

- Dòng dữ liệu: violence (GBV); transmitted infections, miscarriage, premature

labour, low birth weight, stillbirth and high maternal and infant mortality.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Women are reluctant to discuss GBV with strangers

and may not readily divulge this information. A high index of suspicion is required by health care providers to identify this risk.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Red ﬂags of GBV include reports or observation of

anxiety, depression or stress, and signs of physical injury.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The care provider must screen clients by

establishing good rapport and asking direct questions about GBV.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Care providers must also be informed about

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 6

psychosocial support and legal services available in their countries for women facing GBV if they are to be able to support and counsel victims.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Alcohol,; Refer to; •; Little is known about the nature and extent of

- Dòng dữ liệu: tobacco and; PCPNC; alcohol and other drug (AOD) use among

- Dòng dữ liệu: substance use; (C10); pregnant women in Africa.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Alcohol and other substance abuse during

pregnancy negatively impacts both mother and child.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The consequences of alcohol abuse during

pregnancy are classiﬁed as foetal alcohol spectrum disorders (FASD).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Illicit drug use in pregnancy has been associated

with preterm delivery, low birth weight infants, placental abruption, neonatal abstinence syndrome, etc.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Routine screening for alcohol, tobacco and

### Pcpnc (C6) — phần 7

substance use should be done at each ANC contact and, where appropriate, pregnant women should be counselled and/or referred for treatment and support at detoxiﬁcation centres.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Health care providers should ask all pregnant

women about their alcohol, tobacco and substance use, including exposure to second-hand smoke, as early as possible in the pregnancy and at every ANC visit.

## 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Assessment; Reference

Additional comments/information

- Dòng dữ liệu: component; learning

materials

- Dòng dữ liệu: Screening for; The PCPNC; •; Hyperglycaemia ﬁrst detected at any time during

- Dòng dữ liệu: gestational; guide does not; pregnancy should be classiﬁed as either gestational

- Dòng dữ liệu: diabetes; have speciﬁc; diabetes mellitus (GDM) or diabetes mellitus in

- Dòng dữ liệu: mellitus; guidance on; pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: screening for; • Women who present with the following: symptoms

- Dòng dữ liệu: GDM; of polyuria, polydipsia, nocturia, a family history of

diabetes, past obstetric history of glucose intolerance or diabetes mellitus, stillbirth, and foetal macrosomia, should all be screened for GDM. • Screening tests include: urine test for glucosuria, and fasting and/or random blood sugar test

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Diabetes mellitus in pregnancy should be diagnosed if one or more of the following criteria are met: • fasting plasma glucose > 7.0 mmol/L (126 mg/dL) • 2-hour plasma glucose > 11.1 mmol/L (200 mg/dL) following a 75 g oral glucose load • random plasma glucose > 11.1 mmol/L (200 mg/dL) in the presence of diabetes symptoms.

Slide 7: Assessing nutritional status Slide 8: Recommended weight gain

Slides 7 and 8 • Ask participants to explain why maternal nutritional status is important during pregnancy. Write their answers down on a flip chart and compare those answers to what is in slide 7 • Assessing maternal nutritional status includes checking for stunted growth and other important micronutrient deficiencies (such as vitamin A, calcium and iron). • Care providers must take the mother’s height during the first visit, take her

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

weight at all visits, check for vitamin A deficiency (check serum retinol - <0.7 mcm / L or ask about night blindness in most recent pregnancy) and enquire about intake of calcium-rich foods. • Discuss the recommended daily requirements for vitamin A and highlight the fact that WHO does not recommend routine micronutrient supplementation during pregnancy. • Using slide 8, discuss the recommended weight gain during pregnancy.

A: Respond to observed signs and volunteered problems

Most pregnant women experience some problems and discomforts (such as: nausea and vomiting, heartburn, leg cramps, low back and pelvic pain, constipation, varicose veins and oedema). These can be managed conservatively and with readily available home remedies (see the WHO 2016 recommendations on ANC for details). Care providers must recognize serious problems and manage them appropriately in a timely manner.

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 39

Slide 9 • Refer participants to Appendix 5 in the participant manual – Management of common discomforts during pregnancy. • Emphasize the importance of differentiating common discomforts from serious and/or life-threatening health conditions requiring medical intervention. • Ask participants to read selected examples from the chart to highlight this fact.

B: Provide preventive measures

Slide 10: Providing preventive measures • The preventive measures to be provided during ANC include: tetanus toxoid (TT) immunization, iron/folate supplementation, de-worming with mebendazole, intermittent preventive treatment for malaria, and use of insecticide-treated bed nets. • These preventive measures are described in PCPNC C12, F2, F3 and F4. • These interventions will be discussed in detail in Session 6.

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 11: Advice and counsel on nutrition and self-care • Counselling the client on healthy eating and active living should be done in relation to her weight gain relative to pre-pregnancy BMI and detectable nutritional deficiencies which require supplementation (for example, if she is vitamin A deficient or anaemic); or to increase balanced energy and protein intake if she’s not gaining weight adequately. • The client should also be counselled on rest, avoidance of heavy lifting,

safer sex, avoidance of non-prescribed medication, the importance of family planning, and on how to care for her newborn, including early and exclusive breastfeeding (refer to PCPNC C13). • Counselling will be discussed in detail in Session 7.

Class activity: Advice and counsel on nutrition Ask the class to answer the following questions:

### 38 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; List local foods that their clients can take to improve their diets and reduce micronutrient deficiencies,

including iron, folate, calcium and vitamin A.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Are there local taboos restricting the diet of pregnant women in the region?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How can care providers overcome such taboos and improve the diet for their clients?

Write their answers on a flip chart and discuss these in detail.

## 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 12: Counselling and preparation for breastfeeding • Emphasize the importance of preparing the client for breastfeeding during the antenatal period. • All pregnant women must have their breast examined to check for conditions that can interfere with breastfeeding, such as inverted nipples or other disease conditions. • Clients must also be counselled on the benefits of breastfeeding not just to their babies but also to themselves.

Planning for the next visit: • A client’s ANC visit must end with the care provider advising the client on when to return for her next contact. o She should return to the clinic or be seen at community level for her next routine antenatal check.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; An eight-contact schedule is recommended for clients with no existing or anticipated problems.

### 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

o If, however, an ANC check-up is needed sooner than that, for example, to monitor a treatment or for additional emotional support, the care provider must tell the pregnant woman when to come back for this follow-up. o As far as possible, the health provider should also call to remind the ANC client to come for such follow-up if she fails to come back.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A care provider must also emphasize to the pregnant woman that she should come back promptly

anytime she is not well or has any danger sign.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The pregnant woman should be encouraged to bring her partner to at least one antenatal visit.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer to the PCPNC guide – Advise on routine and follow-up visits (C17).

### 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 13: Develop and update her birth and emergency plan • The woman (with her support person) is helped by her care provider to develop a birth and emergency plan. • At each subsequent visit, the care provider reviews the plan with the woman and her support person and updates it according to new findings.

Slide 14: Antenatal protocol chart • The routine care components of the eight recommended ANC contacts are summarized in the antenatal care protocol chart in the participant manual (Figure 3.3). • The eight-contact schedule is recommended for healthy clients with no existing or anticipated problems. • amiliarize participants with the care components at each scheduled contact on the protocol by conducting the related class activity below.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 41

### 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 15: Planning client care for a positive pregnancy experience • Explain that the flow chart summarizes the important steps to take in planning client care at the first ANC visit and thereafter. • Findings obtained during these assessments at each contact determine whether the woman needs routine care or additional care, or needs to be referred for special care (see Figure 3.3 below). • When a client has an identified problem, she receives special treatment and care.

• When the problem is resolved, she may re-join the right side of the diagram - routine antenatal care

Class activity: ANC visit protocol

Instructions • Ask participants to turn to the ANC protocol chart.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give participants five minutes to quietly read and study the chart.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Select a participant each to read aloud the steps for the first visit, and subsequent visits by reading

across the chart.

### 40 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask participants to identify the differences between the first visit and subsequent visits in terms of

visit goals, history and examination, laboratory test, health promotion, and actions to be taken.

Check if participants understand the difference between the booking and first scheduled visits. Explain that if the woman comes to you for the first time before 12 weeks gestation, it is considered the booking visit; if she comes for the first time at 12 weeks gestation, it counts as the first scheduled ANC visit. The health care provider should always provide the woman with all the care that is appropriate for the stage of gestation.

At the end of each contact, the care provider determines when the client should come back next. Her next visit will depend on her health status, the presence of existing or anticipated problems, and the care that she needs.

Encourage questions and discussions.

Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 ** Do not give SP to women receiving cotrimoxazole prophylaxis. Note: If the woman has not yet gone into labour at 41 weeks, she should be advised to present herself to the health facility for delivery.

### -- Ram Bp — phần 10

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 46

Factors associated with poor pregnancy outcomes (20 minutes)

Slide 16: Factors associated with poor pregnancy outcomes • There are many factors that can influence the health of a pregnant woman and her unborn child. • Define the avoidable risk factors. Explain that risk factors include individual risk factors, community risk factors and even health service-related risk factors. • Ask participants to give examples of risk factors in the three categories that can influence pregnancy outcomes in their areas and why.

• Write down their answers on a flip chart. Compare their answers with the list in the participant manual and fill in the gaps.

Supporting pregnant women with special needs (25 minutes)

### -- Ram Bp — phần 11

Slide 17: Supporting pregnant women with special needs During antenatal care, some women need additional care, counselling and support to ensure optimum pregnancy outcomes. Such pregnant women include pregnant teenagers; women with poor partner/family support, physical disability and mental health problems; women experiencing intimate partner abuse; and women in humanitarian crisis situations.

Women with special needs are likely to: • delay seeking antenatal care • not attend ANC • not adhere to medications and treatments • experience mental health issues • be less motivated for self-care such as good nutrition. • have generally poor maternal health and/or foetal growth.

Explain that women with special needs require emotional support from the ANC provider and other care providers and support or community groups.

Then ask participants to read aloud the bulleted list in their manual on how to give emotional support

### -- Ram Bp — phần 12

Other sources of support Ask participants to read the paragraph below (silently). Then ask the class to describe the community or support groups in their areas to whom they can refer women for additional support

The care provider needs to link ANC clients with community-based and other support services available. Emphasize that it is important to keep an up-to-date list of support services available to pregnant women in your area and explore additional alternatives for support through: • community groups, women’s groups and community leaders • peer support groups • other health service providers • community counsellors

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 47

Class activity Group work: Providing care for pregnant women with special needs

### -- Ram Bp — phần 13

This group activity is designed to help participants provide support and care for pregnant women with special care needs, including: pregnant teenagers, women with disability, women experiencing gender-based violence, and women in humanitarian crisis situations.

Slide 18: Providing antenatal care for women with special needs

Slide 19: Group work instructions

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slides 18 and 19 to give instructions on the activity. Also refer to the

text below.

Instructions for group work

Break participants into five groups of four or six people Group 1: Pregnant teenager Group 2: Pregnant woman facing gender-based violence/intimate partner abuse Group 3: Pregnant woman with disability Group 4: Pregnant woman in humanitarian crisis situations.

Task each group to identify the care needs of their special group and make recommendations on how to provide antenatal care in their settings. Assign a facilitator to each group.

### -- Ram Bp — phần 14

Instruct participants in each group to:

1. Appoint a group member as chairperson to facilitate group discussion. 2. Appoint a group member to report the outcome of the group work to the class in plenary 3. Identify three individual, three community and three health service-related risk factors associated with pregnant women with special needs in your assigned group. 4. Explain why the risk factors you have identified may be harmful for the client during her pregnancy.

5. Describe the steps to be taken to provide care in order to ensure a positive pregnancy experience for these women with special needs. 6. Use the worksheet(s) provided to record your group work.

Time: Complete the group activity within 20 minutes. Each group will be given 10 minutes to present their findings.
