## 50 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 50 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Key messages

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To summarize, ask participants to read out the key messages on slides 20

to 22. (See text below). Allow clarification questions from participants. (5 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When a pregnant woman visits the clinic, her care should always begin

with a “Quick check” to quickly identify any serious problems and minimize delays.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A routine ANC contact should include these steps:

(a) Assess maternal and foetal status and plan care (b) Check for pre-eclampsia/eclampsia (c) Test for anaemia (d) Test for syphilis (e) Test for HIV status (f) Develop or update the woman’s birth and emergency plan (g) Respond to observed signs and volunteered problems (h) Provide preventive measures (TT, IPTp-SP, ITN, iron and folate, mebendazole) (i) Advise and counsel on nutrition and self-care (j) Advise her when to return for the next ANC follow-up.

### 50 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The ANC checklist describes the standard procedures for providers to

assess a client during her pregnancy. Findings obtained during these assessments will determine whether the woman is eligible for eight routine ANC contacts, needs additional care, or should be referred for special care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Factors associated with poor pregnancy outcomes include individual,

community and health systems challenges.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Pregnant adolescents and pregnant women with disabilities, women who

experience poor partner/family support or intimate partner violence, and women in humanitarian settings have special needs requiring special support.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 51

### 50 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Session 4: Identifying antenatal problems (60 minutes) • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guidelines • National ANC guidelines aligned with the WHO ANC recommendations (2016) • Laptop

Preparation • Gather training materials in advance • Read relevant sections in both the participant and facilitator manuals

Welcome and introduction

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline

Present slides 1 and 2 to welcome participants to the Session and present the outline.

Aims and objectives

Slide 3: Aims and objectives • Present or ask a participant to read the aim and objectives of the Session

Aim To enable participants to identify and manage the main problems that may prevent a healthy pregnancy outcome.

### 50 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Specific objectives On completion of this session, participants will be able to: 1. describe and apply the clinical decision-making steps and benefits; 2. use the PCPNC manual; 3. use the PCPNC Quick check to rapidly assess each pregnant woman and quickly identify any danger signs; and 4. use the RAM charts to determine the emergency treatments to provide to the pregnant woman with danger signs.

## 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Clinical decision-making in antenatal care

Slide 4: Definition of clinical decision-making • Clinical decision-making (CDM) is a purposeful and organized thinking process that a care provider follows to assess a client, make a diagnosis, plan and begin appropriate care, and evaluate the effectiveness of that care. • It links assessment, care and evaluation of the care through a series of logical steps. • Through clinical decision-making, the ANC provider identifies antenatal problems and makes a decision on the best action based on the

information gathered during assessment of the pregnancy and well-being of the pregnant woman.

Slide 5: Steps in routine ANC • Remind participants of the ANC steps discussed in Session 3. • Highlight Step A: Clinical assessment which requires gathering a lot of information on the patient’s health and pregnancy. • These are analysed and used in deciding on the client’s care plan.

### 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Clinical decision-making steps Ask participants to open their participant manual to Figure 4.1 and read in turns the clinical decision-making steps. For each step, ask participants to share their implementation experiences in their respective settings (10 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step 1; Gather; Client history, physical exam, laboratory investigations, and any

- Dòng dữ liệu: information; previous records

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step 2; Interpret; Identify problem(s) or make a diagnosis

information

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step 3; Develop a; • An individualized plan to meet all the needs of the woman (her

- Dòng dữ liệu: care plan; problems, risks, emotional needs, cultural beliefs, socioeconomic

status). • Some women may require special care plans or urgent referral to another facility. • Every plan must be developed with input from the woman

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step 4; Implement; • Give treatments and provide preventive measures; perform

### 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: care plan; additional laboratory investigations if needed; counsel on nutrition

and self-care; and provide counsel on speciﬁc needs or problems. • Record ﬁndings and actions taken. • Give return appointment; refer if necessary.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step 5; Evaluate the; • On next visit, evaluate improvements or change in condition

- Dòng dữ liệu: care plan; through history, examination or repeat lab tests.

• Was care plan implemented as planned? Was it eﬀective? • Does it need change or modiﬁcation?

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 53

Class activity To further deepen the understanding and use of the clinical decision-making steps, ask participants to identify the problem-solving steps undertaken by Fatima’s mother in the case story below.

### 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slides 6 and 7: Fatima’s story Present slides 6 and 7 and provide the instructions for the exercise.

Slide 8: Instructions for class activity 1 In the following text under the heading “Fatima’s abdominal pain”, circle the sentence(s) that show each of the steps outlined below.

Label them with the number of the step that they illustrate. • Step 1 Interviews and examines • Step 2 Determines what the problem is • Step 3 Decides what to do • Step 4 Carries out her plan • Step 5 Follows up to check if her actions were effective

Give 10 minutes for the exercise

### 52 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 9: Problem-solving steps taken by Fatima’s mother Feedback on Fatima’s story • When all participants have completed the exercise, present slide 9 to describe the five key steps in the decision-making cycle that Fatima’s mother used. • Map out the five key steps in the story using the chart below as a guide. • Discuss these answers to ensure that participants have understood the principal steps.

## 54 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 54 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Fatima’s abdominal pain Newly married twenty-year-old Fatima comes back to see her mother in her house. She is holding her lower abdomen and

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1; wincing. Her mother is very concerned and rushes to her and asks,

“What is wrong Fatima?” She answers, “My menses have not come for two months and I am worried. Since this morning, I have noticed some slight cramping on and oﬀ” Is the pain severe and are you bleeding? Her mother anxiously asks. No, she answers. Mama, I am hungry too, she adds. I have not eaten since waking up this morning, because I am nauseous all the time and vomited this morning. Come and lie down my daughter, she smiled and clapped her hands.

### 54 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Thanks be to God… you are pregnant, she announces. I will make you something nice to eat now. Mother helps Fatima to the mat and settles her down. She quickly brings her a pot of delicious local porridge and encourages her to drink it slowly. Later, she brings some soup and Agidi, which Fatima also enjoys.

## 3 Are you feeling better now? Mother asks Fatima two hours later.

### 3 Are you feeling better now? Mother asks Fatima two hours later. — phần 1

Fatima reports back that she is better and that the pain is gone. Mother explains to her that she thinks she is pregnant and that many pregnant women experience nausea and vomiting in the early months. She teaches her about foods she should eat and tells her to rest when she goes back to her home. If the pain should come back, and especially if there is bleeding, Fatima should inform Salifu her husband, to come back and call her so she can accompany Fatima to the clinic, she advises, as she sees

her oﬀ. Mother did not hear from Fatima or her husband for one week , so she paid them a visit in their home the next Saturday. To her relief, Fatima was well and had been following her dietary advice.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 55

Rationale for using clinical decision-making steps during antenatal care

### 3 Are you feeling better now? Mother asks Fatima two hours later. — phần 2

Slide 10: Rationale for following CDM steps in ANC • Explain how the care of pregnant women provides a unique opportunity to use CDM steps repeatedly. • Many clinical decisions are required from the first to the last contact as the clinical situation changes and/or different needs arise.

Slide 11: The five major steps of clinical decision-making The five steps of the clinical decision-making process form a continuous cycle with each step feeding into the next step. During care for a pregnant woman, the cycle is repeated at each visit. Using clinical decision-making steps helps the ANC provider to:

1. collect information in an organized way, therebysaving time and resources; 2. interpret information so that problems or needs are identified correctly, and none are overlooked; 3. give evidence-based care, avoiding unnecessary, inappropriate or excessive treatments; and 4. evaluate the effectiveness of the care provided.

### 3 Are you feeling better now? Mother asks Fatima two hours later. — phần 3

Using the PCPNC manual to strengthen clinical decision- making during care for the pregnant woman

Slides 12 and 13: Using the PCPNC manual for decision-making

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Using slides 12 and 13, explain that the PCPNC manual helps ANC providers

and other care providers to use clinical decision-making skills to identify and manage pregnancy complications.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; It facilitates the collection of relevant information, the classification of

problems, and the identification of evidence-based management.

## 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Quick check and rapid assessment and management (RAM) (10 minutes)

Slide 14: Quick check and RAM • Antenatal care clinics in many low-resource settings are visited by many clients daily and are often busy with long waiting times. • Although most pregnant women visiting these clinics are generally healthy, some may present with life threatening complications requiring prompt and quick attention. • These sick women must be identified and provided with life-saving care as soon as they arrive at the clinic.

Ask participants how they identify and manage sick antenatal clients presenting to their settings? Referring to slide 14, explain Quick check and RAM.

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 15: Using the PCPNC manual • Refer participants to the “Introduction” section of the PCPNC manual – “Structure and presentation”. Select participants to read out this page whilst emphasizing the key points. • Next, refer participants to flow charts B2–B7 in the PCPNC guide. Quick check is on page B2. RAM is on pages B3–B7. Give all participants 10 minutes to browse through these flow charts. • Quick check page – B2. Ask some participants to each read out the first

paragraph, and the respective columns ASK, CHECK RECORD, LOOK, LISTEN, FEEL, SIGNS CLASSIFY and TREAT. • Ask them how long it would take them to do this “Quick check” assessment for a woman who just arrived? • Emphasize the four possible classifications: EMERGENCY FOR WOMAN, LABOUR, EMERGENCY FOR BABY and ROUTINE CARE. • Ask participants to identify which classification means the woman needs RAM, and which one means that she can wait for her turn in the queue?

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Group work activity: Using the PCPNC manual

Case studies: Assessment and management of a pregnant woman with danger symptoms and signs (20 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; In this case study exercise, you will task participants to use the RAM charts to classify a pregnant

client’s complications and determine the type of care needed.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Divide the class into groups and assign a facilitator to each group. Present slide 16 and ask

participants to read and follow the instructions for the case study using the PCPNC charts.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide each group with a case study for classification, along with the activity worksheet. Six case

studies have been provided. You may provide additional case studies if needed.

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 16: Group work case study – Instructions to the class • Appoint a group member as chairperson to facilitate discussions and another to report the outcome of the group activity to the class. • Read and analyse the case studies individually and as a group. When the others in your group have finished reading it, discuss the questions. Identify and use the relevant PCPNC charts to guide management of the case. Agree on and document answers for each question on the worksheet

provided (refer to participant manual).

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 57

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The time for the group activity is 20 minutes. Each group will be given 10 minutes to present their answers.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To end this session, lead a summary discussion to highlight the main lessons from these case studies.

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The answers to the questions in these case studies are presented below in italics.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Where there are significant differences, refer participants to the PCPNC guide. If the national or local

protocol differs, discuss what the correct treatment and advice would be.

Case study 1: Ngozi Ngozi is a 26-year-old nullipara. Her husband carried her into the health centre at 10pm. She is eight months pregnant and complaining of a severe headache, blurred vision and upper abdominal discomfort. She had passed out/fainted briefly on her way to the clinic.

Recognizing these as danger signs and symptoms, you did a rapid assessment and found that her blood pressure was 180/120 mmHg. A dipstick test of her urine recorded 4+ proteinuria. She had not experienced any fit. The foetal heart rate is 150/min and regular.

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B2, B6, B13

Question: What is the diagnosis? Answer: Severe pre-eclampsia/eclampsia

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Transfer the woman to a treatment room, call for help, reassure woman and her husband, insert an IV line, give magnesium sulphate, give antihypertensive, refer her to a hospital. (Chart B13)

Case study 2: Akosua Madam Akosua is pregnant with her fourth baby. She is eight months pregnant and does not attend ANC clinic regularly. You noticed that she looked very worried and uncomfortable when she arrived at the clinic this morning. When you ask her what the problem is, she says that she has been bleeding on and off for two days now. The bleeding has suddenly gotten worse since morning, and she is very worried about her baby. She tells

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

you that she has had to change her pads every two hours and is now using cloth pads instead. She is feeling rather weak and a little dizzy. The baby has not moved very much today.

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B2, B3, B4, B9, B 13, B17

Question: What is the diagnosis? Answer: Antepartum haemorrhage (vaginal bleeding in late pregnancy)

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Transfer the woman to a treatment room for further comprehensive assessment, call for help, reassure woman, perform rapid assessment (ask about quantity of blood loss and presence of abdominal pain; check for pallor; measure blood pressure, pulse, foetal heart rate, abdominal tenderness, presence of contractions). If her blood pressure is low (systolic BP<90) and pulse rate is high (>110), take blood for Hb

### 56 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

test, blood grouping and cross-matching; insert an IV line and start IV infusion with crystalloids; and refer her to a hospital.

## 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Case study 3: Moeshi You are a care provider at the antenatal clinic in Mankanta (a slum area). As you walked into the clinic this morning to start work, you noticed a rather shy-looking teenager sitting in the corner of the antenatal waiting area away from other attendants. She looks rather weak and uncomfortable.

You call her immediately into the consulting cubicle after explaining to other clients that we must give priority care to those who are unwell and need more urgent medical attention.

She tells you that her name is Moeshi; she is 16 years old and she thinks she is about two and a half months pregnant. Her parents are very angry with her and will not speak to her. She has been vomiting since yesterday and has fever, headache and bodily pains. You check her temperature and it is 39 ˚C. Her eyes look sunken and her tongue furred and dry.

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B4, B6 (dangerous fever), B9, B16

Question: What is/are the possible diagnoses? Answer: Severe malaria

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Transfer the woman to a treatment room for further comprehensive assessment (check for fast breathing and stiff neck); call for help; reassure woman; do a rapid test for malaria; set up an IV line and give IV fluids slowly; start parenteral antimalarials (for example, quinine); and refer her to a hospital.

Case study 4: Madam Assi Madam Assi is a 24-year-old client who is eight months pregnant. She is reporting for her fifth antenatal checkup. Today, she is complaining of fever, frequent and painful urination, and flank pain which started three days ago.

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Although she can feel her baby move, she thinks the movements are less than previously felt. You check her temperature and note it is 38 ˚C. Her blood pressure is 120/70 mmHg and her pulse is 100/min. Urine test is positive for protein (+).

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B2, B6, B7, B15, C8

Question: What is the possible diagnosis? Answer: Upper urinary tract infection

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Transfer the woman to a treatment room for further comprehensive assessment (check foetal heart rate), reassure woman, start appropriate antibiotics and refer her to a hospital.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 59

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Case study 5: Naithwati Naithwati is a 21-year-old client who is coming to the clinic for the very first time. At the registration counter, you notice that she looks rather breathless and tired, so you immediately invite her into the consulting cubicle. She reports that she is seven months pregnant but was unable to come to the clinic earlier because she had no money. She complains of tiring easily and feeling breathless even when she exerts herself slightly. She has also

had dizzy spells. She says that she can feel foetal movements.

When you perform a quick check, you notice severe conjunctival pallor. Her temperature is normal; her BP is 130/60 mmHg, and her pulse 110/min.

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B2, B7, C4

Question: What is the possible diagnosis? Answer: Severe anaemia

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Transfer the woman to a treatment room for further comprehensive assessment (check foetal heart rate and haemoglobin level); reassure woman; start her on double dose iron and folate; and refer her to a hospital.

Case study 6: Madam Ceci Madam Ceci is a 29-year-old G2 P1. She is three months pregnant and reporting for her first antenatal clinic visit. She is well but is complaining of abnormal offensive vaginal discharge and vulval itchiness. Her partner is also complaining of urethral discharge. She has no fever, no lower abdominal or flank pain. Her temperature is normal.

Her uterus can be felt just above the pubis (912 weeks). When you inspect her vulva, you notice curd-like slightly offensive vaginal discharge.

Question: Which flow chart(s) in the PCPNC guide is/are relevant for this case study? Answer: B2, C9, F5, G2

### 58 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Question: What is the possible diagnosis? Answer: genital tract infection (possible gonorrhea, chlamydia, candida infection)

Question: What next steps would you take in her care? Answer: Keep her in the waiting area; she does not require RAM. Provide routine ANC. In addition, assess her for well-being of the pregnancy and for other STIs; treat her with appropriate antibiotics and antifungals; and counsel her on safer sex.

## 60 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 60 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Key messages (5 minutes)

Slides 17 and 18: Key messages To summarize and end Session 4, ask participants to read out the key messages or present slides 17 and 18.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clinical decision-making (CDM) comprises five major steps:

o Gather information o Interpret information o Develop a care plan o Implement the care plan o Evaluate the care plan.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Quick check should be used to quickly identify women with danger signs on

arrival at the facility to minimize delays in providing them with emergency care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Women with urgent problems should be transferred to the emergency room

or area, and a skilled care provider should perform rapid assessment and management (RAM) of the urgent problems.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Comprehensive assessment, through history taking, physical

### 60 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

examination, laboratory testing and other special investigations, forms the basis for planning and providing care to a woman during her pregnancy. Allow clarification questions from participants (5 minutes).

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 61

Session 5: Managing antenatal problems Prepare for the Session: Gather all training materials in advance

You will need the following materials: • Flip chart with paper, markers • Facilitator guide, • Participant manual • Copies of case studies for group work activity • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guidelines • National ANC and other safe motherhood protocols • Managing complications in pregnancy and childbirth (MCPC) manual • Recommendations for clinical practice of emergency obstetrical and newborn care (RCP) • Laptop

### 60 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Introduction: Session title, presentation outline, session goals and objectives

Slide 1: Title slide; Slide 2: Session outline Present the course title and welcome participants to the class. Briefly describe the session outline.

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Task participants to read out loud the aim and objectives of the session (5 minutes).

This session focuses on the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); the advice and treatments to be given to a pregnant woman who

presents with common discomforts and concerns of pregnancy; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: (b); the management of specific common conditions and complications

(observed signs and volunteered problems) during antenatal care.

Note: Some select conditions (malaria, anaemia and HIV) in pregnancy are addressed in separate course sessions.

## 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 4: Steps in routine antenatal care • Emphasize that most pregnant women attending antenatal clinics are generally healthy and have no abnormal findings on assessment. • However, a significant proportion may present with concerns and complaints that may be due to the changes of pregnancy or have signs and symptoms suggestive of ill-health and disease complications. • The visit provides the care provider with an opportunity to screen her

client, identify if the client has a problem or complication and provide e vidence-based interventions and treatments to protect her life and that of her unborn child. • Session 5 focuses on Box B: “Respond to observed signs and volunteered problems”.

Managing common discomforts and concerns of pregnancy

### 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 5: Brainstorming discomforts of pregnancy • Ask participants to list some of the physiological changes that pregnancy brings to organ systems in a woman’s body and describe related discomforts and concerns. • List the organ systems on slide 5 to guide the discussion and answers. Record the answers given on a flip chart. • Complete the discussion by referring participants to and reviewing the list of common discomforts and concerns in the chart in Session 5 of the participant manual.

Slide 6: Importance of managing the common discomforts of pregnancy • Using the information listed on the slide, highlight and discuss the importance of proper management of common complaints of pregnancy. • Emphasize the importance of being knowledgeable about existing local practices and treatments that pregnant women use to address some discomforts so the that harmful practices can be discouraged.

### 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 7: Principles for diagnosing and managing common discomforts of pregnancy Present the principles for diagnosing and managing common discomforts. In order to be able to help women manage their discomforts/concerns, the ANC provider must: • distinguish these symptoms from those of life-threatening pregnancy complications; and • provide information to the pregnant woman on the underlying cause(s) of the discomfort and proffer evidence-based measures to relieve them.

• Note: Gaps in knowledge lead to widespread use of many useless or harmful treatments for some common complaints.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 63

### 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 8: Managing the common discomforts of pregnancy • Present slide 8, then • Introduce trainees to the detailed guide on the identification and management of common discomforts and concerns in Appendix 5 of the participant manual. • The guide describes ways to distinguish common discomforts from danger signs. Trainees should be encouraged to refer to this guide when managing their clients. • During the next class group activity on this topic, facilitators should help

participants use the guide by comparing existing management practices to those recommended in the guide.

Group work activity 1: Managing common discomforts of pregnancy This group activity is designed to help students distinguish and manage the common discomforts of pregnancy.

### 62 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 9: Group work – managing common discomforts and concerns • Review the “Instructions for group work” with the class and make sure that students understand what is expected of them. • Divide the class into groups of approximately five. Assign each group one or two organs and body systems as listed in the participant manual. Ask each group to list five discomforts related to their assigned systems. Provide each group with several copies of the “worksheet.”

• Allow 20 minutes for the groups to complete the activity. • Supervise the activity by spending some time with each group or assigning other facilitators to each of them. • Allow 10 minutes for each group to report the outcomes of the group activity. • Use the information in Appendix 5 to fill any knowledge gaps.

Summarize the feedback session by emphasizing once again the principles for managing common discomforts and concerns of pregnancy.

## 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Managing common antenatal disease conditions and complications

Slides 10 and 11: Common antenatal disease conditions and complications • Introduce the topic by asking clients to brainstorm and answer the questions presented on slide 10. • Write down the answers given on a flip chart. • Present slide 11 and compare the answers provided by participants to those listed. • Discuss any differences and reach consensus on the common disease conditions and complications seen by participants in their practice.

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

• Highlight conditions such as substance abuse and gender-related violence which may be under-reported because they may not be screened for, or have stigma attached to them. • Note that whilst some pregnant women with these conditions/ complications may present as emergencies with danger symptoms and signs that are detected during “Quick check” (for example, severe pre-eclampsia), others too may be found during routine screening, such as syphilis and foetal demise.

Slide 12: Managing common antenatal disease conditions and complications • Emphasize the importance of prompt recognition and management of obstetric complications by highlighting their unpredictability and the speed with which they can lead to death or severe disability. • Remind trainees that the “Quick check” must be conducted on all women visiting an antenatal clinic.

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 13: Using evidence-based protocols and guidelines to manage pregnancy complications • Present all reference documents. • PCPNC: Refer clients to the following flow charts in the PCPNC guide: B2, B3, B9, C2 and C3 – to highlight the chart areas for recommended treatments and care: “Treat” “Treatment”, “Advise”, “Treat and Advise” • Select participants to read out loud these sections and ask them to note any differences in content. Highlight the linkages between flow charts using two

examples: circulatory shock (B3 and B9) and anaemia (C4 and F3). • Introduce participants to other recommended treatment guidelines and protocols (MCPC, RCP and national protocols) if these are also available for the course. Briefly describe the layout of these documents to participants and how to use them.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 65

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Stress the importance of applying the clinical decision-making steps in the management of

clients by always linking information gathered (ASK, CHECK RECORD, LOOK, LISTEN, FEEL) to interpret findings (DIAGNOSIS /CLASSIFY) and to the recommended TREATMENT and ADVISE components of the flow chart.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clarify any issues raised and discuss any gaps in participants’ knowledge of how to use these

charts.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The upcoming group work activity on case studies will further enhance participants’

understanding and use of these charts in managing common antenatal disease conditions and complications.

Group work activity 2

CASE STUDIES: Managing observed and volunteered problems and complications (30 minutes)

This group work activity is designed to help participants manage pregnant clients presenting with pregnancy complications using the PCPNC guide and information.

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slides 14 and 15: Group work activity 2 – managing observed and volunteered problems and complications • Present slide 14 and review the “Instructions for group work” with the class and make sure that trainees understand what is expected of them. • Divide the class into groups of approximately five and provide each group with copies of the case studies and worksheets (or refer them to the relevant page in the participant manual). • Assign specific case studies to the groups (using slide 15).

• Allow 20 minutes for the groups to complete the activity. • Supervise the activity by spending some time with each group or assigning each group a facilitator. • Allow 10 minutes for each group to provide feedback on the outcome of the group activity.

### 64 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Feedback and discussion: Refer to the answer key for each case study below while discussing the feedback provided by each group. Clarify any questions that may have arisen during the group activity and address any gaps and/ or inconsistencies in trainees’ knowledge.

## 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Case study 1: Antepartum haemorrhage (bleeding in late pregnancy) Madam Akosua is pregnant with her fourth baby. She is eight months pregnant. She does not attend ANC regularly. You noticed that she looked very worried and uncomfortable when she arrived at the clinic this morning. When you ask her what the problem is, she responds that she has been bleeding on and off for two days now. The bleeding has suddenly gotten worse since morning, and she is very worried about her baby.

She tells you that she is forced to change her pads every two hours because they are soaked, and she must now use cloth pads instead. She is feeling rather weak and a little dizzy. The baby has not moved very much today. You make the diagnosis that Akosua has antepartum haemorrhage (bleeding in late pregnancy).

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe in detail the treatment and other care requirements you will offer Akosua.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: PCPNC flow charts: B2, B4, B9, B3, B17; Other references: _________________

Treatments and other care

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do not do a vaginal examination

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Measure blood pressure and pulse; if in shock (BP< 90mmHg or pulse >110/min):

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Insert an IV line

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Give fluids rapidly if heavy bleeding or shock: infuse 1 litre in 15–20 mins; next, infuse one

litre in 30 mins

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Position her on her left side with legs higher than chest

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Monitor every 15 minutes for: BP, pulse, shortness of breath, urine output

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Record treatments given and time

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Refer her urgently to hospital (give referral note)

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Case study 2: Severe pre-eclampsia Ngozi is a 26-year-old nullipara. Her husband carried her into the health centre at 10pm. She is eight months pregnant and complaining of a severe headache, blurred vision and upper abdominal discomfort. Recognizing these as danger signs and symptoms, you did a rapid assessment and found that her blood pressure was 180/120 mmHg. A dipstick test of her urine recorded 4+ proteinuria. She had not had any fit.

The foetal heart rate is 150/min and regular. Your impression is that Ngozi has severe pre-eclampsia.

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe in detail the treatments and other care requirements you will offer Ngozi.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: Flow charts: B2, B7, B9, B13, B14, B17; Other references: _______________

Treatments and other care

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; (Transfer woman to the treatment room and call for help)

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Check/measure her blood pressure

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Insert an IV line and give fluids slowly – 1 litre in 6–8 hours

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give magnesium sulphate (dosage and regime in chart)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give appropriate antihypertensive drugs (hydralazine 5mg IV slowly) and repeat dose at 30

minutes intervals until diastolic BP is at 90 mmHg. Do not give more than 20 mg in total.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Monitor her every 15 minutes for: BP, respiratory rate, pulse, knee jerk reflex, urine output

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record treatments given and time

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer her urgently to hospital (give referral note)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 67

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Case study 3: Urinary tract infection Madam Assi is a 24-year-old client who is eight months pregnant. She is reporting for her fifth antenatal checkup. She looks ill today and is complaining of fever, vomiting and frequent and painful urination and flank pain which started three days ago. Although she can feel her baby move, she thinks the movements are less than previously felt. You check her temperature and note that it is 39 ˚C. Her blood pressure is 120/70 mmHg and her pulse 100/min.

Urine test was positive for protein (+). You conclude that Assi has urinary tract infection.

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe in detail the treatments and other care requirements you will offer Madam Assi.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: Flow charts: B2, B6, B7, B15, B17; Other reference materials: _________________

Treatments and other care – in treatment area:

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Insert an IV line and give fluids at moderate rate: 1 litre in 2–3 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give appropriate IV/IM antibiotics (ampicillin, gentamicin) before referral; continue for 48 hours after

woman is fever free.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Monitor her for: BP, temperature, respiratory rate, pulse, urine output.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Record treatments given and time.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer her urgently to Hospital (Give referral note)

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

Case study 4: Genital tract infection Madam Ceci is a 29-year-old G2 P1. She is three months pregnant and reporting for her first antenatal contact. She is well but is complaining of abnormal offensive vaginal discharge and vulval itchiness. She reports that her part- ner is also complaining of urethral discharge. She has no fever, lower abdominal or flank pain. Her temperature is normal. Her uterus can be felt just above the pubis (12 weeks). When you inspect her vulva, you notice a curd-like

slightly offensive vaginal discharge. Your impression is genital tract infection (possible gonorrhoea/chlamydia/ trichomonas and candidiasis)

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe in detail the treatments and other care requirements you will offer Ceci.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: Flow charts: C9, F5, G2,; Other references: _________________

Treatments and other care – in treatment area:

### 66 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give appropriate oral antibiotics to the woman (two antibiotics: ceftriaxone and erythromycin)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give appropriate oral antibiotics to her partner (ciprofloxacin, doxycycline)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give antifungal drugs (clotrimazole pessary)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Advise on correct and consistent condom use

## 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Case study 5: Intimate partner abuse

Naithwathi is a 28-year-old client who is coming to the clinic for the very first time. She reports that she is seven months pregnant but was unable to come to the clinic earlier because she had no money and her husband is unwilling to give her any. You notice that she looks sad and has a bruise on her face. After gently prompting, she tells that she has been beaten by her partner. This was not the first time. She has been married to him for three years and

### 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

has one other child. Her husband is blaming her for getting pregnant too quickly even though he refused to let her use contraceptives. Apart from bodily pains, she is otherwise well and can feel her baby move. When you examine her, you notice that she has another bruise on her back that was healing. Her temperature, BP and pulse are all normal. Her SFH corresponds to her gestation, and FHR is present and normal. Your diagnosis is intimate partner abuse.

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe the treatments and other care requirements you will offer Naithwathi.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: Flow charts: C10, H4,; Other references: _________________

Treat and advise • Support the woman (provide privacy; listen to her; help her assess her situation with respect to her own safety and the safety of her children; explore options; identify local sources of support; and refer) • Document abuse

### 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Case study 6: Preterm rupture of membranes You are a care provider at the antenatal clinic in Mankanta (a slum area). As you walked into the clinic this morning to start work, you noticed a rather shy looking teenager sitting in the corner of the waiting area, away from other attendants. She looks rather uncomfortable. You called her immediately into the consulting cubicle after obtaining permission from other waiting clients.

Her name is Moeshi and she is an 18-year-old student. She is about seven and a half months pregnant. She has had drainage of copious clear fluid from her vagina for the last three days. This morning, however, the amount of fluid discharge seems to have increased. She also has very slight lower abdominal pain. She can nonetheless still feel her baby move. You check her temperature, and it is normal. Vaginal inspection confirms that she is draining

### 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

amniotic fluid from her cervix. She does not have uterine contractions on palpation, and FHR is normal. You form an impression of preterm rupture of membranes.

Question: Using the relevant PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines, describe the treatments and other care requirements you will offer Ngozi.

Answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: Flow charts: C7, B15, B17; Other references: _________________

Treat and advise

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give appropriate oral antibiotics (ampicillin or erythromycin)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer her urgently to hospital

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 69

Summary and conclusions: key messages

Slide 16: Key messages • End the session by asking trainees to list some key lessons learned in this session. • Present slide 16 to conclude the session.

Key messages (5 minutes)

### 68 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Common discomforts and concerns of pregnancy must be distinguished

from danger signs and other symptoms. The care provider should provide information to the pregnant woman on the causes of the discomforts and counsel her on how to relieve them. The correct management of discomforts increases the client’s confidence in the care provider.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; About 15% of pregnant women will develop problems and

complications during their pregnancy. Pregnant women reporting to the antenatal clinic must be screened for problems at all visits and any identified problems managed promptly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The PCPNC charts and/or other reference guidelines such as

national protocols and the MCPC and RCP manuals can guide antenatal care providers to use their clinical decision-making skills in identifying and classifying common pregnancy complications, and in providing appropriate, evidence-based treatment and advice.

## 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Session 6: Providing preventive care measures As a facilitator, make sure you have the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; National PMTCT guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; National malaria guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; National ANC guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; 2016 WHO ANC recommendations for a positive pregnancy experience

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; WHO guideline on syphilis screening and treatment for pregnant women (2017)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; National STI guidelines (if available)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; National TB guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Laptop

Preparation

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Gather training materials in advance.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Read through the module – participant and facilitator manuals.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Make sure to cross-check against what is in national guidelines.

Welcome and introductions

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline

### 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slide 1 to welcome participants to the session.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slide 2 to describe the session outline.

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 or task a participant to read the aim and objectives of the session.

Aim: To describe the recommended evidence-based preventive measures that should be provided during antenatal care.

Objectives: On completion of this session, participants will be able to communicate effectively on the following: 1. The preventive care measures for common infections and nutritional problems during pregnancy. 2. The schedule for giving tetanus toxoid immunization, iron/folate supplementation, deworming, and intermittent preventive treatment for malaria during pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 71

Preventive care measures for healthy pregnancy outcomes (25 minutes)

### 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 4: Plenary discussion questions • Ask participants to mention the preventive care measures they provide to pregnant women in their respective settings and write down their answers on the flip chart. • Early identification and management of antenatal problems is obviously important, as outlined in sessions 3 and 4. • Providing preventive measures to all pregnant women is equally important, particularly in Africa, where infections such as malaria, neonatal tetanus,

parasitic infestations (such as amoebiasis, hookworm and schistosomiasis), HIV, viral hepatitis and tuberculosis are significant. Nutritional problems, in- cluding anaemia, are also prevalent in this environment.

### 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 5: Key steps in routine antenatal care • Present slide 5 to highlight the role of preventive care in the provision of routine ANC to clients. • Pregnant women must be assessed and provided these preventive measures as part of routine ANC visits, as shown in Figure 6.1. • They must also be counselled on adherence to medicines and self-care to avoid risk conditions and maintain good health throughout their pregnancy.

Slide 6: Recommended preventive measures (1) The package of evidence-based preventive measures recommended for pregnant women in the sub-Saharan region includes: • provision of tetanus toxoid immunization • iron and folate supplementation • deworming • intermittent preventive treatment for malaria • sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net. • prevention of mother-to-child transmission of HIV, viral hepatitis, and syphilis (further discussed in session 11).

### 70 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Review the participants’ list of interventions on the flip chart and discuss any discrepancies.

Slide 7: Recommended preventive measures (2) • Highlight the fact that all pregnant women must be assessed for and provided with preventive care interventions. • Emphasize that counselling on the interventions and medicines as well as self-care forms an essential component of providing preventive care measures.

## 72 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

referenced chart (refer to the PCPNC charts C12 and F2–F4).

## 1 TT/Td at 14–16 years

### 1 TT/Td at 14–16 years — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 75

Preventive care counselling (20 minutes)

Introduction: • Emphasize that preventive measures require appropriate counselling and support. • During her first antenatal visit and at subsequent visits, the care provider must counsel and support the pregnant woman to practice healthy lifestyles and to take steps to protect her health and her pregnancy.

The key preventive counselling topics are listed in slides 8 and 9.

Slides 8 and 9: Preventive care counselling

### 1 TT/Td at 14–16 years — phần 2

Slide 8: First contact – counselling on: • preventive medicines (TT/TD, IPTp-SP, iron/folate, mebendazole) and their regimens; • good nutrition and anaemia prevention; • common infections (malaria, HIV, hepatitis B, syphilis, TB); • lifestyle (hygiene, stopping smoking and alcohol and drug misuse and abuse, safer sex and correct and consistent use of condoms); • danger signs during pregnancy and what to do if they occur; • developing a birth and emergency plan with client and her support person;

• adherence to ANC contact schedule

Slide 9: All subsequent contacts • Review and update her birth and emergency plan (according to new findings). • Reinforce all of the above key messages.

### 1 TT/Td at 14–16 years — phần 3

Third trimester • Danger signs during labour and childbirth • Family planning • Repeat HIV testing for negative women and reinforce ARV adherence counselling in HIV positive cases • The importance of exclusive breastfeeding • Care of the newborn (early initiation of exclusive breastfeeding, thermal care, clean cord care, danger signs in the infant, and immunization including HBV birth dose within 24 hours).

Session 7 of this training package will describe in more detail counselling steps and skills for antenatal care.

Individual class activity: Providing preventive care interventions to all pregnant women at the antenatal clinic

This class exercise is to help participants think through ways to ensure that the essential preventive measures are available to all pregnant women attending antenatal clinics in their settings.

## 76 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 76 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 10: Individual work – providing preventive care measures in ANC • Present slide 10 to explain the purpose and instructions for this exercise to participants. • Ask participants to work on this exercise individually. • When they have finished, they should raise their hand so that a facilitator can come to them (or vice versa) to discuss their work individually. • They will also be provided an opportunity to share their thoughts with class

members during discussions to summarize and conclude the session.

Instructions for facilitators Giving individual feedback will require the participation of all facilitators. As participants finish this exercise, they should raise a hand to get a facilitator’s attention or come to you. When they do, the facilitator should sit with each participant to review their work.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Sit somewhat away from others who are still working (if possible) and keep your voice low so as not

### 76 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

to disturb others.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; First, check that the participant has written correct answers in the first chart – the information can be

found in Figure 6.2 or PCPNC charts F2, F3, F4. If the participant has an incorrect answer, ask about it. Either she did not refer to Figure 6.2, or perhaps she is describing a standard that is different in her clinic. Make sure that she has a correct understanding of good practice.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Next, review the second chart. Ask the participant about some of the answers to show your interest

in whether providing these preventive measures is a challenge in her clinic and how she plans to overcome these challenges.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If you see that some participants have finished the exercise but have not discussed it with any

facilitator, encourage them to do so.

### 76 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

When all participants have received feedback, the lead facilitator must briefly review and discuss the answers using the answer guides provided below. Compare these with participants’ answers and address any knowledge gaps and concerns.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Conclude the module by presenting the key messages.

Conclude the session by presenting the key messages below.

Key messages (5 minutes)

Present slides 11 and 12: Key messages • In sub-Saharan Africa, environmental factors lead to diseases such as malaria, nutritional anaemia, neonatal tetanus and parasitic infestations (such as amoebiasis, hookworm, and schistosomiasis) which are major contributors to poor pregnancy outcomes. • Special attention must be paid to the prevention of mother-to-child transmission of HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis.

- Dòng dữ liệu: • The; recommended; preventive; care; medicines; to; be

### 78 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

given during antenatal care include tetanus toxoid immunization, iron/ folate supplementation, deworming with mebendazole, and intermittent preventive treatment for malaria. • During each antenatal visit, pregnant women must be counselled on adherence to preventive measures such as taking daily iron and folic acid tablets, sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net, and returning for subsequent ANC contacts so that they can receive all the recommended preventive measures.

Classroom quiz: ANC (sessions 1–6) – answers

### 78 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

The following quiz can be conducted as a classroom question-answer activity. Simply read each question to the class and allow a show of hands for the response. If an incorrect response is given, provide the correct one. Avoid accepting answers from the same trainee(s). Encourage all trainees to participate, as the quiz provides you with an opportunity to assess their level of knowledge and understanding so far (for example, the quiz covers the content of sessions 1 through 5).

Depending on the progress made in this course, time constraints may prove a challenge. Suggested alternatives are: (i) reduce the number of selected questions asked; (ii) use some questions when summarizing the session as sug- gested in the previous session; and (3) hand out the quiz to the trainees as a take-home assignment, to be returned the next day for marking.

Present and discuss the correct answers as listed below.

### 78 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

1. Give five reasons why periodic visits to a health care provider are necessary during pregnancy.

• Early detection and treatment of complications. • Establishment of a supportive relationship between the woman and the health care provider. • Development of a birth and emergency plan with the woman. • Provision of preventive measures. • Provision of advice and counselling.

## 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

2. What is the recommended schedule of antenatal contacts (that is, how many routine visits and at what intervals)?

Ans. Eight contacts at 12, 20, 26, 30, 34, 36, 38 and 40 weeks

3. Mention four things that the health care provider should do during all contacts with the woman to maintain privacy and confidentiality.

Ans. Any four of the following: • ensure a private place for examination and counselling; • ensure that discussions, especially about sensitive subjects, cannot be overheard; • ensure that the woman has given her consent before discussing matters with her partner or family; • never discuss confidential information about clients with other providers, or outside of the health facility; • organize the examination area so that, during examinations, the woman is protected (with a curtain, screen, or

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

wall) from the view of other people; • ensure all records are confidential and kept locked away; and • limit access to logbooks and registers to responsible health care providers only.

4. What are the daily and occasional administrative activities that should be conducted to ensure the proper running of antenatal clinics?

• Records of equipment, supplies, drugs, and vaccines should be updated. • Essential equipment should be checked daily to ensure proper functioning. • Staff lists and schedules should be established. • Periodic reports on births, deaths, and other indicators should be completed according to regulations.

5. Why is it essential for health care providers to follow universal precautions when providing antenatal care?

To protect the woman, her baby, themselves and the community from infection with bacteria and viruses, including HIV.

6. What should be done first when a woman presents at an antenatal clinic?

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

A Quick check should be performed to identify any emergency signs and, if an emergency sign is identified, rapid assessment and management (RAM) should follow.

7. Name five individual risk factors during pregnancy?

Ans. Any five of the following: • adolescent pregnancy • anaemia • burning on urination • complications of previous pregnancy • multiple pregnancy • syphilis • low educational status • low economic status • poor health status • sociocultural and religious beliefs that are harmful during pregnancy • violence against women

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 81

8. Name five community risk factors during pregnancy?

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Ans. Any five of the following: • endemic malaria infection • endemic iodine deficiency • endemic vitamin A deficiency • great distance from a woman’s home to a health facility where the required care is available • lack of transportation between home and a health facility • low socioeconomic status • low educational status • prevailing sociocultural and religious beliefs that are harmful during pregnancy • violence against women.

9. Name five health services risk factors during pregnancy?

Ans. Any five of the following: • antenatal clinics that do not have the basic supplies, equipment and drugs for antenatal care; • staff that are not trained to provide routine and emergency care during the antenatal period; • absence of a functional referral system for the management of complications; • inability of health care providers to manage women with special needs; • negative attitudes of health care providers toward pregnant adolescents;

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

• limited competencies of health care providers to manage women who experience violence; and • poor links between the health facility and the community and traditional providers.

10. When giving emotional support to a woman with special needs, how can the health care provider create a comfortable environment for the woman?

• By being aware of her own attitude; • by being open and approachable; • by using a gentle, reassuring tone of voice.

11. What does the health care provider need to do to help the pregnant adolescent consider her options and make decisions?

### 80 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

• Help the adolescent girl understand the importance of planning to have her baby in a hospital or health centre. • Help her to understand the importance, to herself and to her baby, of STI or HIV/AIDS prevention, including the use of condoms in all sexual relations. • Help her to understand the importance of birth spacing for her own health and that of her baby, including the recommendation of at least 2–3 years between pregnancies.

• Support her in making the best decisions for herself and for her baby.

12. The clinical decision-making process should be continuous and ongoing. True or false?

Ans. TRUE

13. The development of a care plan is based on the findings of the assessment. True or false?

Ans. TRUE

14. The first step in clinical decision-making is identifying the problem(s). True or false?

Ans. FALSE

## 82 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 82 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

15. Clinical decision-making occurs before development of the care plan and does not need to occur again, for that patient or client. True or false?

Ans. FALSE

16. What observations (LOOK, LISTEN, FEEL) does the health care provider need to make when doing a Quick check on an antenatal client?

The person doing the Quick check needs to observe whether the woman is being wheeled or carried into the clinic or whether any of the following are present: vaginal bleeding, convulsions, looks very ill, unconscious, in severe pain, in labour, delivery imminent.

17. What emergency signs are included in rapid assessment and management (RAM) of a pregnant woman?

### 82 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

• Airway and breathing: very difficult breathing or central cyanosis • Circulation and shock: cold and moist skin, or weak and fast pulse • Vaginal bleeding in early pregnancy: uterus not above the umbilicus • Vaginal bleeding in late pregnancy: uterus above the umbilicus • Convulsions or unconsciousness: convulsing (now or recently), or unconscious • Severe abdominal pain: not normal labour

• Dangerous fever: temperature >38 ˚C and any of the following: very fast breathing, stiff neck, lethargy, very weak/not able to stand.

18. At which antenatal visit should the woman be checked for anaemia?

At every visit, check for pallor.

19. When checking for anaemia, what does the health care provider need to ask the pregnant woman?

If the woman tires easily and if she is breathless (short of breath) during routine chores or work.

20. At which antenatal contact should the woman’s haemoglobin be measured?

### 82 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Check HB at contact 1, 3 and 6 if the woman has no complaints.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 83

Session 7: Counselling during antenatal care • Pregnancy, childbirth postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • Learning guide for antenatal counselling • Laptop • One table and three chairs

Preparation • Gather training materials in advance. • Review the module in the participant and facilitator manuals. • Identify suitable participants to perform the role plays and brief them so that they can prepare in advance.

Introductions

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slides 1 and 2 to introduce the session and provide the course

outlines.

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 or ask a participant to read out the aim and objectives of the session (5 Minutes).

### 82 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

AIM: To help participants understand the importance of good communication during antenatal care while improving their skills in counselling pregnant women and support persons.

Objectives On completion of this session, participants will be able to:

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); demonstrate five good communication practices during ANC;

- Dòng dữ liệu: (b); outline the steps involved in the counselling process;

- Dòng dữ liệu: (c); discuss the advantages of group counselling, couple counselling and

individual counselling; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: (d); apply counselling principles and skills in antenatal care.

## 84 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 84 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 4: Women want a positive pregnancy experience • Ask participants what they understand by a positive pregnancy experience; write down their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 4. • Ask participants to share some of their positive and negative experiences during their interactions with antenatal clients. • Ask about the effect of these experiences on ANC uptake and quality.

Slides 5, 6 and 7: What do women say about their ANC experience?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Many factors affect the uptake and quality of ANC for women.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present these slides to share some positive and negative comments received

from antenatal clients on their pregnancy care experiences.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; These comments were obtained during qualitative surveys

conducted by WHO for the purpose of developing guidelines to ensure a positive pregnancy experience.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Use; these; together; with; the; shared; experience; of

### 84 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

participants to emphasize the importance of good client-provider interaction and communication during antenatal care.

Defining counselling in ANC (5 minutes)

Slide 8: Counselling during ANC Slide 9: Key steps in routine ANC

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To introduce the topic, ask participants what counselling in antenatal care is.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Write down their answers on the flip chart.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slide 8 and provide the definition as follows:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; ANC counselling is an interactive process between the health care

provider and her client during which information is exchanged and support is provided to enable the woman and her family to make decisions, develop a plan, and take actions to ensure the health of herself and her baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Using Slide 9, highlight the importance of ANC counselling in routine care

provision.

### 84 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 85

Slide 10: Good counselling is critical for a positive pregnancy experience • A positive pregnancy experience hinges on good antenatal counselling. • Use this slide to describe and emphasize the client-provider interactions and communications that support positive pregnancy outcomes for the client.

Counselling principles, steps and skills

Slide 11: Guiding principles for counselling • Present slide 11 to list the guiding principles of counselling.

Slides 12, 13, 14 and 15: Guiding principles for counselling • Refer participants to the participant manual and/or present slides 12–15. • Ask participants to take turns reading the paragraphs describing each of the principles in detail. • Explain each principle by giving relevant examples. You may use the “examples” provided below if necessary.

### 84 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 12: Self-reflection, respect and empathy Self-reflection • Being a staunch Christian, the ANC care provider always insists that her Muslim clients also say the “Our Father” prayer prior to start of the clinic (or vice versa). This upsets them.

Respect and empathy • The local cultural practice requires that all pregnant women wear large beaded amulets around their hip to protect themselves and their babies from pregnancy complications. • During group counselling on danger signs, explain the importance of danger signs in detecting pregnancy complications early, and that pregnant women should adhere to your advice whilst also wearing the charms. They do not have to remove them before coming to the clinic because of their fear of

your disapproval.

## 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 13: Confidentiality • Emphasize the need for clinic settings that promote visual and auditory privacy (for example, consulting cubicles with closed doors or curtains; not conducting consultations with other clients in the room). • Use HIV as an example of a sensitive topic to highlight challenges that could occur if a care provider shared a client’s positive test results with her best friend who unknowingly happens to be the client’s sister-in-law.

• Discuss how ANC clients’ records are kept to ensure confidentiality.

Slide 14: Encouraging interaction and building on current knowledge Encouraging interaction • Demonstrate verbal and nonverbal ways of promoting two-way interactions: • “Tell me about yourself and how this problem started.” • “Did you say that your husband refuses to give you money to come to this clinic?” • Holding the client’s hand if culturally acceptable.

### 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Building on current knowledge or skills • Explaining to an anaemic client who already knows that iron and folate must be taken during pregnancy why she must now take a double dose of her iron medication.

Slide 15: Shared problem solving, tailoring to specific needs Shared problem solving • A pregnant woman facing spousal abuse: discuss all available options with her – move to stay with her mother; report her spouse to the police; stay at home with her husband but take measures to protect herself from injuries during physical assaults/attacks. • State that you will support her, whatever her decision: she decides for the sake of her young children that she will continue to stay at home with her

spouse and use protective measures.

### 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Tailoring to specific needs • Your client is an unmarried teenager, whose boyfriend abandoned her when she became pregnant. She wants to give up her baby for adoption and asks for your advice and help in this regard. • You readily consent and provide her with all the information and support she needs.

Key steps in the counselling process

Slide 16: Key steps in the counselling process

Present slide 16 to lists the key steps in a counselling process.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); Assess the situation.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (b); Define the problem, needs or information gaps.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (c); Generate alternative solutions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (d); Prioritize the solutions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (e); Develop a plan.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (f); Review and evaluate.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 87

### 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; As you go through each step, ask a participant to read it out loud and explain the paragraphs in the participant

manual that describe each step more fully. Correct any discrepancies (20 minutes).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Note that the guiding principles outlined above support these six steps in the counselling process.

Counselling skills (15 minutes)

Slide 17: Counselling skills • Present and discuss the counselling skills listed in the slide. • Emphasize that these skills are based on the guiding principles and the six steps in the counselling process described above. • As you go through each counselling skill, ask participants in turn to read out loud and explain to the rest of the class the paragraphs in the participant manual that describe the skills in detail. • Correct any misunderstanding.

### 86 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 18: Counselling skills – communication Slide 19: The counselling context • Present slides 18 and 19 and clarify any issues that arise.

Class activities 1 and 2

During these class activities, assist participants to learn the counselling skills through:

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; individual work (activity 1); and

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.; observing a demonstration of the correct ways of performing these skills (activity 2).

Prepare for this activity as described below.

## 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024
