### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 20: Class activity 1 – individual work Applying counselling principles and skills (20 minutes) • Refer participants to the relevant section in their manual. • Explain the purpose of the exercise. • Read out and explain the instructions.

Instructions • Ask participants to work individually on this exercise. They should read the question and write their answers in the space provided. • When they have finished, the facilitator will lead a feedback discussion so that participants can share their answers and comments.

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Activity 1: Feedback and discussion • When all participants have completed the exercise, ask for volunteers to read their answers to each question. Then ask if anyone has another possible answer. • If an answer does not demonstrate the counselling skill as it should, ask the other participants for another answer. • Refer to the answer sheet provided to give feedback and to conduct discussions to identify the best answers.

Activity 1: Applying counselling principles and skills – answer sheet

Question 1: Write a response to each statement below that shows respect and empathy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); “I’ve been crying over something practically every day. It’s crazy.”

Answer: You’re crying almost every day and you feel like that’s crazy !

(b) “Men in my family do not come to the health centre with their wives.” Answer: In your family, it is not expected that your husband will come to the clinic with you.

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Question 2: Write a better response than the one below to encourage interaction.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); “You don’t have to tell me about your mother-in-law. Believe me, I’ve heard it all before.”

Answer: Tell me more about your mother-in-law and how you are feeling.

Question 3: How should the health workers described below demonstrate active listening?

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); Nurse Lila is leaning back in her chair and looking out the window while the woman is talking.

Answer: She should sit forward in her chair, look at the woman while she talks, and nod or smile (as appropriate) at what she is saying.

(b) Sister Shepherd is sitting behind a desk, and the door to the noisy hallway is open. Answer: She should sit in a chair on the same side of the desk as the woman. She should shut the door to make it quieter and more private.

Question 4: Write better questions than those below to find out about a woman who has anaemia.

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); “Your haemoglobin has gone down. Have you been skipping your iron pills?”

Answer: Are you able to take your iron pills each day, or are you having any difficulties with them?

- Dòng dữ liệu: (b); “I presume you are eating well?”

Answer: Can you describe to me what you have been eating?

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 89

Question 5: Rewrite the following statements to better provide this information to a pregnant woman:

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

(a) “Malaria infection can lead to severe anaemia, severe placental parasitation and even foetal death!” Answer: If you get malaria while you are pregnant, it is dangerous. You are likely to become sicker than you would otherwise be, and it could even kill your baby. (b) “You must bring your husband for HIV testing and counselling, as well as consultation about making plans for institutional birth and other contingencies.”

Answer: It would be very helpful if you could bring your husband with you on your next contact – if possible. I would like to talk to him about HIV testing and counselling and if he agrees, he can be tested also. We could also all talk together about plans for the baby’s birth and what you could do in case of an emergency.

Class activity 2: Role play – demonstration of counselling skills

Topic: Counselling a client on pregnancy care by a skilled attendant (30 minutes)

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Preparations for this activity: • Prepare for this role play activity when preparing for the session by: • identifying and orientating role play actors as follows: • choose a facilitator to play the role of the health provider and another two volunteers (participants) to play the role of Jeanine the patient and her mother; and • orientate the actors about their roles and allow them to practice these roles until perfect.

Actor roles

Health care provider: The provider is an experienced community midwife at a primary health care centre who has good communication skills.

Jeanine: Jeanine is a 28-year-old woman; she has four living children and is now four months pregnant. One of her babies died shortly after birth, and her sister died in childbirth last year.

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

Jeanine’s mother: Jeanine’s mother is 52 years old. She has eight living children; she had two stillbirths and one child died at the age of 1 month. One of her daughters died in childbirth last year. Participants will act as the audience and observers. They should take notes on how the health provider applied coun- selling principles and skills.

Case scenario and background information • Jeanine, a 28-year-old woman, has come to the health centre with her mother. She has four living children and is now four months pregnant. One of her babies died shortly after birth. Her mother and grandmother have helped her to deliver all her babies at home. • Jeanine’s mother is 52 years old. She has eight living children; she has had two stillbirths, with one other child dying at the age of 1 month.

### 88 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

• Jeanine has been to the health centre once before, when she brought her 5-year-old son who had pneumonia last year. The two women are interested in learning more about the care available at the health centre because a relative delivered her baby there six months ago. Jeanine is nervous about her current pregnancy because her sister died in childbirth at home last year.

## 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Focus of the role play The focus of the role play is the interaction between the midwife, Jeanine, and her mother as they discuss Jeanine’s desires as an expectant mother – keeping in mind her rights as a human being. The midwife should demonstrate the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; be friendly and reassuring;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; discuss safe motherhood principles and a woman’s right to expect safe, respectful health care;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; assess Jeanine’s knowledge about the role of the midwife and the services available for women;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; describe the role of the midwife to the women;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; briefly explain what services are available and how Jeanine can be involved in the decisions about her care;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; discuss Jeanine’s human rights as a childbearing woman, generally (such as her right to be free from ill treatment

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

and to be assured privacy and confidentiality) and specifically (such as her right to have a companion during doctor-patient contacts throughout her pregnancy and during childbirth); and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; encourage the women to ask questions, and address the questions they ask.

Jeanine and her mother should ask questions and express their concerns until the midwife has provided them with enough information about the role of the midwife, their rights, and the care available at the health centre.

Slide 21: Demonstration of counselling skills • Present the instructions for this demonstration role play. • Instruct participants to closely observe the role play and to document their observations as per the guidance provided under “Focus of the role play”. • They will discuss their observations in the feedback session at end of the role play.

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Feedback and discussion At the end of the role play, the facilitator should use the questions below to facilitate discussions: • How did the midwife prepare her room for the counselling activity? • How did the midwife approach Jeanine and her mother? • Did the midwife give Jeanine and her mother enough information about the role of the midwife? About the health centre? About her right to safe motherhood? About her right to a birth companion?

• How did Jeanine and her mother respond to the midwife? • What did the midwife do to demonstrate emotional support and reassurance? • Were the midwife’s explanations and reassurance effective? Why or why not?

Allow participants to comment on these questions extensively whilst pointing out the good counselling skills and practices.

Counselling approaches (20 minutes)

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 22: Counselling approaches • Introduce the topic by asking participants to describe the various ways/ approaches they use to provide counselling in their settings. What factors influence their choices for the different approaches they use? • Record their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 22 to describe the three counselling approaches used in antenatal care. • Compare these with participants’ answers and fill any gaps.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 91

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Slides 23–26 explain the advantages and disadvantages of each of the three counselling approaches

(group, couple and individual).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; First, ask participants to list the advantages and disadvantages of each of the approaches; write down their

answers on the flipchart.

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Then, present slides 23–26 to describe the advantages and disadvantages of each counselling approach.

Compare these with participants’ answers and fill any gaps.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; For each slide, highlight the following important considerations:

Slide 23: Group counselling – advantages and disadvantages • During this interaction, the health provider counsels and delivers health messages to several people at the same time. • This can be employed in busy clinics for non-sensitive messages; and in settings where group ANC is being conducted. • This does not eliminate the need for individual counselling for sensitive and private issues.

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slides 24 and 25: Couple counselling – advantages and disadvantages • Couple counselling should be voluntary for both partners and should not be mandatory or coerced. • The antenatal clinic set-up must be couple- or male-friendly, with adequate seating provided for the pregnant woman and her partner. • During couple counselling, while joint decision-making is encouraged, both partners must feel free to make independent decisions.

• An agreement should be reached with the couple at the start of the counselling activity about sharing confidential information.

Slide 26: Individual counselling – advantages and disadvantages • The individual counselling approach is best suited to discuss sensitive matters, especially those related to sexuality.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Conclude this subject by emphasizing that the approaches complement each other.

### 90 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The choice of which approach to use in addressing a health topic is influenced by clinic situations such as

workload and time, the sensitive nature of the topic, and the complexity and relevance of the message.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give this example: a group counselling approach may be used to introduce PMTCT and explain what HIV

is and its modes of infection. Couple counselling on the same topic can be employed prior to testing of the pregnant woman and her partner whilst individual counselling can be used during post-test counselling.

## 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Individualizing antenatal health messages

Slide 27: Individualizing ANC messages Introduce the subject by explaining the following: • The antenatal period is a nine-month long period during which the pregnant woman experiences many changes with different health needs and concerns along the way. • Different pregnant women have different needs during their pregnancies. • It is therefore important to individualize and prioritize health messages for each individual client. This means selecting topics to discuss at different

times in relation to the client’s gestational age and clinical situation. • Prioritization is also important because of the limited time available at each contact for client-provider interaction. • Present slide 27.

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 28: Recommended counselling topics for the first trimester • Ask participants to list the health messages that are best delivered during the first trimester, and the second and third trimesters. • Refer them to table 7.1 in the participant manual. • Record their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 28 and compare their answers to those on the first trimester list.

Slide 29: Recommended counselling topics for the second and third trimesters • Present the information on slide 29 and compare it with those provided by participants. Fill in any gaps. • Refer participants to the PCPNC guide, specifically charts M1 and M2, which contain information and counselling messages in formats that can also be shared with the woman to take home.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 93

Class activity 3: Role play – ANC counselling (45 minutes)

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

The purpose of this group activity is for participants to practice their skills in conducting individual or couple counselling with the aid of learning guides and checklists. • Divide the class into groups of three and assign each group a facilitator. • Present the instructions for the group role play activity on slides 30 and 31 below. • Provide each group with a role play scenario and background information on their respective role plays.

Each participant must play the role of either client, midwife or observer. • Refer participants to the learning guides/checklist in the participant manual. A brief plenary discussion will be held at end of the activity.

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slides 30 and 31: Class activity – role play • Using slides 30 and 31, read out the instructions and clarify any issues. • When playing the role of client, participants must refer to the “Client background information”. • When acting as midwife, participants must refer to the “Counsellor background information” and use the ANC learning guide. • When acting as an observer, participants must use the ANC learning guide as a checklist to record their observations for discussion with the group during

the feedback session.

Background information for role plays

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Role play 1: scenario – Mrs A Mrs. A is a 23-year-old lady reporting to the ANC clinic for her first visit; she is accompanied by her mother-in-law. She is four months pregnant. She lives with her husband and her mother-in-law in the village. Her mother-in-law has been advising her not to eat too much meat and not to take those “bad medicines” supplied at the clinic as they make delivery difficult and harm the babies.

Client information • You are 23 years old. • This is your first pregnancy and your first visit to the antenatal clinic. • The midwife has completed her assessment of you and has given you some iron tablets. • You have never taken iron tablets and are worried that they might harm your baby. • You tell the midwife that your mother-in-law will not allow you to take the iron tablets. • You also tell the midwife that your mother-in-law will not allow you to eat red meat during your pregnancy.

### 92 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Counsellor information • You have completed your assessment of Mrs A and no problems were detected. • Her haemoglobin is 12.5g/dl. • You have given her a three-month supply of iron/folate tablets.

You now need to counsel Mrs A about nutrition and her compliance in taking the iron/folate tablets.

## 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Role play 1: scenario – Mrs B Mrs B is a regular ANC attendant. She is six months pregnant and is at the clinic for her third visit. She looks well. All her examination findings are normal. Her only concern is that she does not want to have any more children. She thinks that five children are enough.

Client information • You are 30 years old and have five children. • This is your sixth pregnancy and you do not want to become pregnant again. • You are in the third trimester of pregnancy and want to talk to the midwife about not becoming pregnant again. • You have not used a family planning method in the past but have heard other women talk about the methods they use. • You have not yet talked to your husband about not wanting any more children.

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Counsellor information • Mrs B is well known to you and is a regular attendant. She is now six months pregnant. • Mrs B has confided in you that she does not want to become pregnant again. • She is in the third trimester of pregnancy and is well. • You need to provide Mrs B with advice and counselling about family planning.

Role play 1: scenario – Mrs C Mrs C is a new attendant reporting for the first time to the antenatal clinic. You notice that she had been quite interactive during the group counselling session on danger signs and asked a lot of questions. She mentioned that a doctor had told her to deliver her baby in a hospital. She has entered the cubicle for her routine check-up. She had no new health complaints. All her examination findings were normal.

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Client information • You are 25 years old. This is your third pregnancy and your first clinic visit for this pregnancy. Your first and second babies were born at home and the births were attended by your mother-in-law. • You bled a lot following the birth of your second baby and by the time your neighbours got you to the hospital, you were very ill and had to be given two units of blood transfusions.

• The attending doctors and nurses told you how lucky you were then to still be alive. They said you should deliver in the hospital in your next pregnancy. You are concerned since your village is far from the hospital. A client has informed you that the midwife can be of help in addressing this concern.

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Counsellor information • Mrs C is 25 years old. This is her third pregnancy and her first clinic visit for this pregnancy She has never had an antenatal check-up. She is five months pregnant. • Her previous deliveries were all at home and the births were attended by her mother-in-law. She survived PPH after the birth of her second child and has been advised to deliver her babies in a hospital.

• She lives in a village about 15 mm from the hospital. You are to counsel and help Mrs. C prepare a birth and emergency plan.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 95

Figure 7.3: Learning guide/checklist for counsellors and observers Use the guides below to remind you of the counselling skills. Use the “Remarks” column to record observations or problem areas about the interaction with the client.

Checklist for antenatal counselling

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: Skills; 1; 2; Remarks

Use two-way communication • Provide the woman ample opportunity to talk about and clarify issues and problems. • Provide the required information to the woman and encourage her to talk about the information provided.

Build rapport • Apply the guiding principles of counselling to establish a trusting and caring environment for the woman. • Put the woman at ease to talk about her situation and to discuss sensitive topics.

Demonstrate active listening • Close windows and doors to reduce distractions. • Make sure that the woman is seated comfortably. • Look at the woman as she talks with you. • Use a warm, friendly tone of voice. • Use gestures and body language such as nodding and smiling. • Use verbal aﬃrmations such as “yes” and “I see”. • Ask questions about what the woman has said. • Paraphrase what the woman has said, when appropriate. Ask questions • Use open-ended questions.

### 94 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

• Ask questions in a non-judgmental way. Provide information • Provide clear and understandable information that relates to the woman’s needs. • Encourage the woman to ask questions and clarify anything she has not understood. • Ask the woman to repeat the information in her own words, if necessary.

Facilitate • Help the woman to ﬁnd appropriate solutions by asking her to list possible solutions and consider the advantages and disadvantages of each. • Keep summarizing the discussion to help keep the discussion on track and move the woman toward making a decision.

Checklist for antenatal counselling process

- Dòng dữ liệu: Steps; 1; 2; Remarks

1. Assess the situation.

2. Deﬁne the problem, needs or information gaps

3. Generate alternative solutions

3. Prioritize the solutions

4. Develop a plan. 5. Review and evaluate.

## 96 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 96 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Feedback and discussion When each participant has had a chance to play all three roles and group feedback discussions have been completed, bring participants together in a brief plenary discussion to conclude the activity. Use the following questions to facilitate discussions:

- Dòng dữ liệu: (a); Ask participants what they felt went well during the counselling practice.

- Dòng dữ liệu: (b); What were some of the skill challenges they encountered?

- Dòng dữ liệu: (c); How will they overcome challenges at their respective ANC clinics when conducting similar

counselling for their clients?

Allow participants to comment on these questions extensively; then summarize lessons learned to conclude the activity.

Remind participants that they will have the opportunity to further practice their counselling skills in other training sessions that address specific complications, such as Malaria, anaemia and HIV/

## Pmtct.

### Pmtct. — phần 1

Key messages (5 minutes)

Slide 32: Key messages End session 7 by presenting these key messages: • Antenatal counselling is an interactive process between the health care provider and a pregnant woman and her support persons. It is an important component of ANC. • Communication involves the exchange of information and is most productive and effective when it is a two-way process in which each pers on can discuss and clarify issues and problems.

Key messages

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; Key steps in the counselling process include:

(a) assessing the situation; (b) defining the problem, needs or information gaps; (c) generating alternative solutions; (d) prioritizing the solutions; (e) developing a plan; and (f) reviewing and evaluating.

### Pmtct. — phần 2

2. Individual, couple and group counselling during ANC contacts each have their advantages. Group counselling saves time for the provider. Couple counselling may help couples cope better with their pregnancy, plan together for antenatal care and birth, and strengthen their communications relating to that pregnancy. In individual counselling, the care provider can identify the client’s individual needs and target counselling messages

to address those needs. The client is also more likely to discuss sensitive issues.

3. With good communication skills, the care provider can establish good rapport, counsel effectively, and provide quality care to the client.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 97

### Pmtct. — phần 3

Session 8: Developing birth and emergency plan • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • Learning guide for ANC counselling • Laptop • One table and three chairs

Preparation • Gather training materials in advance.

Introduction

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline Present slides 1 and 2 to welcome and introduce participants to the session.

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 or ask a participant to read the aim and objectives of the session (5 Minutes).

Aim: To enable participants to support pregnant women and families to prepare birth and emergency plans.

Objectives On completion of this session, participants will be able to: 1. explain to the pregnant woman the importance of preparing a birth and emergency plan; and 2. assist the pregnant woman and her family to prepare their own birth and emergency plan.

## 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Rationale for birth preparedness and complication readiness

Slide 4: Why should women deliver in a health facility? Slide 5: Barriers to skilled attendance at birth • Ask participants “what percentage of women in their communities/ countries deliver with skilled birth attendants or in health facilities” • Ask “why is it important for pregnant women to deliver with skilled birth attendants and in health facilities?”. • Note their answers.

Present slides 4 and 5 whilst also highlighting the following additional points: • Approximately 16–33% of maternal deaths could be averted if pregnant women received care from skilled attendants. • ANC serves as the entry point into the continuum of maternal care services. • It therefore provides an opportunity for helping women prepare for and use subsequent maternity care services through careful planning, education and increasing their level of satisfaction with services.

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 6: Key steps in routine antenatal care • Developing a birth and emergency plan is a key component of routine ANC. • Present slide 6 (figure 8.1) to show its importance within the scheme of care. • This session focuses on how to develop this plan. • Ask participants if the development of a birth and emergency plan is part of the ANC they currently provide. • Listen to their answers, and then continue.

Defining birth preparedness and complication readiness (BP/CR)

Slide 7: Defining birth preparedness and complication readiness (BP/CR) • Present slide 7 and task a participant to read the definitions on the slide for birth preparedness and complication readiness. • Clarify any questions participants may have on these definitions.

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

The birth preparedness and complication readiness plan (also known as “birth and emergency plan”) is the document that lets the care provider and support persons of the pregnant woman know her wishes and preferences for how things should be done to manage her when labour or complications occur.

Slide 8: Why is a birth and emergency plan important? Present slide 8 to highlight the importance of the BP/CR plan, emphasizing the following facts: • It is an important global strategy to increase universal access to skilled attendance at birth. • It is a key component of routine ANC. • It increases the likelihood that needed resources are available and that the woman and her baby will receive appropriate and timely care at the time of birth or complications.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 99

Developing the birth and emergency plan

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 9: Developing a birth and emergency plan • Ask participants to answer the following two questions: 1. At what time during the pregnancy should a BP/CR plan be developed? 2. Who should be involved in its development?” • Present slide 9 and explain that the plan is developed during ANC counselling and must be initiated from the first visit. • It is individualized to reflect the pregnant woman’s situation and resources.

• Also highlight the importance of including family members/support persons. • Task a participant each to read out the stated reasons for including family members in the BP/CR planning as outlined in the participant manual.

Help families develop a birth and emergency plan (20 minutes)

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 10: Helping families prepare the birth and emergency plan • Explain that the care provider must first understand the client and her family/support persons’ attitudes and situations to be able to assist them to come up with the best BP/CR plan. • She must ask a number of questions to get relevant information. • he then provides advice and additional information as needed to help the families and client make their decisions about what they would do.

Slide 11: Preparing the birth plan • Ask participants to list some questions they would ask clients to better understand their attitudes and situations regarding delivery at a health facility. • Note their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 11 and compare the questions listed to those provided by participants. Fill in any gaps. • Refer participants to the page on preparing the birth plan in their manual (Birth preparedness).

### 98 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

• Task a participant each to read one question and its related advice. • Discuss and clarify any concerns that arise.

Slide 12: Developing the emergency plan • Complications are not always predictable and can occur at any time. • Use the information on slide 12 to list and explain the relevant questions to ask to determine and plan a client’s emergency preparedness. • Emphasize the importance of reminding clients that complications can occur on several occasions during the nine-month-long pregnancy period. They must therefore have extra funding and plans for such occasions.

## 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 13: Danger signs during pregnancy • For a client and her family to implement a birth and emergency plan, they would first have to recognize these situations. An essential component of preparing a plan is to educated clients about the signs and symptoms of labour and danger signs. • First, ask participants to list all danger sign/symptoms they know. • Write these on a flip chart. Then ask them to sort these signs into two groups:

“Go to hospital immediately” or “Go to hospital as soon as possible”. • Present slide 13 and discuss the answers.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Understanding the sociocultural environments from which clients come makes it easier for care

providers to include additional relevant questions and advice in their BP/CR planning.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; For example, in a culture where men are not allowed into labour wards but must make decisions

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

for cesarean section, the health care provider may advise the client to have an additional support person including the husband.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Table 8.3 in the participant manual provides some possible advice and solutions to address

common challenges that clients and their families experience in the development of BP/CR plans.

Slide 14: Document and review the birth and emergency plan • When a plan has been reached with the client and family, it is important that this is documented and referred to at each antenatal visit, for review and revision. • The record also serves as a reminder to the client and family of what to do when the anticipated situation arises. • Refer participants to the sample form in their manual. Ask if they have such forms in their settings and how they differ from this sample.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Note that the best-laid plans do not always go according to plan. Remind clients to always check if

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

their agreed plan is implementable, for example, village transport is still available and functional.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Having alternate plans may be necessary for some key issues such as: what to do if the first choice of

transport fails, or what to do at home if the baby is delivered before the clinic is reached, etc.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; These scenarios must all be considered during review and further development of the plan.

Class activity: Role play – preparing a birth plan (45 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The objective of this activity is to practice developing a birth and emergency plan, taking into

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; account local and cultural beliefs and practices.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present the instructions and provide the role play scenarios to the groups (or refer them to the relevant

page in their manual).

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 101

Slide 15: Class activity 3 – role play Assign participants into groups of five. There are four different roles in the role play, giving each group member an opportunity to act as a client, a support person, the midwife, and an observer. • Client must use the information below under the heading “Client and support persons: background information”. • Support person must use the information below under “support person in “Client and support persons: background Information”.

• Midwife must use the information in the box labelled “Counsellor background information. The midwife must use the sample BPCR form to record the agreed plan (refer to the participant manual or relevant appendix)

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Refer to PCPNC chart M3 or the counselling guide provided in Box 8.5. • Observers should use the counselling guide as a checklist. They must observe how the midwife applies the counselling principles and steps to interact with the client and support persons to prepare and reach the agreed plan.

Slide 17: Role play scenario (45 minutes) • This allows 10 minutes for each role play (including preparation time), plus five minutes for the observer to provide feedback about the counselling session. • The feedback should cover the points listed in the counselling guide and counselling skills.

Information for role plays – actors

Role play scenario • An ANC provider (midwife) is undertaking counselling on birth preparedness and complication readiness with a client and support person at the clinic.

Counsellor background information You are an experienced midwife at the antenatal clinic. You will require the following:

### 100 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; a sample birth and emergency plan form

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.; a counselling guide for BPCR ( see PCPNC M2) or use the guide in the box below:

## 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Figure 8.5: Counselling guide for developing a birth and emergency plan (for use by counsellor and observers)

In order to help a woman and her family develop their individualized birth and emergency plan, ask questions first; then listen to their answers to understand their attitudes and situation. Provide advice based on their situation and the answers they give, and document the agreed plan.

When planning for facility birth – consider the following: • Where will you go to deliver? • How will you get there? • How will you pay for transport to get there? • How much will it cost to deliver at the facility and how will you pay for this? • Can you start saving for these costs now? • Who will go with you and support you during labour and delivery? • Who will help take care of your home and other children while you are away?

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Gather and bring along the following items to the facility: • home-based maternal record • supplies for delivery (provide the list) • clean clothes for you and the baby. • food and water for you and the support person.

To plan for an emergency, consider: • What are the danger signs? • Where should you go if a danger sign occurs? • How will you get there? • Will you have to pay for transport to get there and how much will it cost? • What costs will you have to pay at the health centre and how will you pay for these costs? • Can you start saving for these possible costs now? • Who will go with you to the health centre? • Who will help to care for your home and other children while you are away?

Client and support persons: background information (Select participants to play client and support roles in each of the scenarios)

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Client 1: Madam A She is 17 years old, with her first pregnancy. This is her first ANC visit. She is single and unemployed and lives in a slum area in same town as the clinic. Support person: her mother

Client 2: Mrs B She is 35 years old and a farmer. She has six children. She lives with her mother-in-law and her husband about 25 km away. This is her seventh pregnancy. She delivered all her babies at home and says she prefers home births. Support person: her mother-in-law

Client 3: Mrs C She is 35 years old and a teacher. She is pregnant with her third child. Her previous deliveries were all in this hospital. However, she was unhappy with the way she was treated when she requested to be allowed to have a squatting position for delivery at her last delivery. Support person: her husband.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 103

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Role play feedback and discussion (10 min) • When all participants have had a chance to play the role of counsellor and group feedback discussions have been completed, bring participants together in a brief plenary discussion to conclude the activity. • Use the following questions to facilitate discussions. 1. Ask participants what they felt went well during the counselling practice 2. What were some of the skill challenges they encountered?

3. How will they overcome challenges at their respective ANC clinics when conducting similar counselling for their clients? • Allow participants to comment on these questions extensively. • Then summarize lessons learned to conclude the activity.

Establishing links with the community to support birth and emergency plans

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 18: Establishing links with the community • Ask participants to list groups and organizations within their communities that work with and support women, especially during pregnancy. How have they engaged these groups? • Emphasize that establishing links with the community is essential to support birth and emergency planning. • Using slide 18, give examples of how communities can support women’s BP/ CR plans. • Challenge ANC care providers to explore other innovative ways to

encourage communities to play their role in the BP/CR strategy.

Key messages (5 minutes)

### 102 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slide 19: Key messages • Developing a birth and emergency plan is a key component of ANC. • Having a birth plan can reduce confusion at the time of birth or emergency and increase the chance that the woman and her baby will receive appropriate and timely care. • In preparing for birth, it may be important to include the woman’s family members, her partner, or other decision-makers in her life. Helping the family prepare their own birth and emergency plan involves ongoing

discussion with the pregnant woman and her family to help them decide where to give birth, organize the resources they need for the birth, and decide what they will do in an emergency situation.

## 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Session 9: Malaria prevention and control in pregnancy Before teaching this session, you should familiarize yourself with the national policy/protocols for prevention and control of malaria in pregnancy. Check that the guidelines for malaria in pregnancy prevention and control in this session are consistent with the national guidelines, and if not, revise the text and slides as needed.

• Pregnancy, childbirth postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • WHO technical brief – Implementing malaria in pregnancy programmes in the context of World Health Organization recommendations on antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience, April 2017 • National malaria guidelines and protocols (up to date) • National antenatal care guidelines (up to date) • Laptop • One table and three chairs

### 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Preparation • Gather training materials in advance • Review the module in both the facilitator and participant manuals • Review the WHO technical brief and national malaria protocols

Introduction

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline Present slides 1 and 2 to introduce participants to this session.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 105

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 or ask a participant to read the aim and objectives of the session (5 minutes).

Aim: To help participants understand the importance of malaria in pregnancy and develop skills for malaria prevention and case management in pregnancy.

### 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Objectives: At the end of the session, participants will be able to: 1. describe the burden and consequences of malaria in the pregnant woman and her baby; 2. counsel pregnant women on ways to prevent malaria in pregnancy; 3. identify and correctly manage and/or refer cases of malaria in pregnancy; and 4. describe the severe consequences of malaria in pregnant women when associated with HIV, sickle cell disease or nutritional anaemia.

Burden of malaria in pregnancy

Introduce this topic by asking how many participants have ever managed a client with malaria in pregnancy; and how many clients with this condition (MiP) they see in a month in their facilities. Note their answers.

### 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slides 4 and 5: Burden of malaria in pregnancy Present slides 4 and 5. Highlight the following: • Malaria infection during pregnancy is an enormous public health problem, with substantial risks for the mother, her foetus and the neonate. • Women are four times as likely to get sick from malaria if they are pregnant and twice as likely to die from the disease. • Malaria and pregnancy are mutually aggravating conditions. The physiological

changes of pregnancy and the pathological changes due to malaria have a synergistic effect on the course of each other, thus making life difficult for the mother, the child and the treating physician. • In Africa, 30 million women living in malaria-endemic areas become pregnant each year. For these women, malaria is a threat both to themselves and to their babies, with up to 200 000 newborn deaths.

Malaria and its transmission (15 minutes)

### 104 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Ask participants how malaria is transmitted and listen to their answers.

## 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slides 6–8: Malaria transmission • Present slides 6–8 and explain the following facts (if not well understood). • Malaria is an infection caused by a protozoan of the Plasmodium family. The most common (responsible for over 90% of infections) and most dangerous in sub-Saharan Africa is the P. falciparum. • The parasite is transmitted through the bite of an infected female Anopheles mosquito. When the infected female mosquito bites its victim,

it injects the Plasmodium parasites into the victim’s blood vessels. These parasites enter red blood cells (RBCs) and the liver and multiply. When they infect and later burst out of the red blood cells, they damage these cells. New parasites released go on to attack newer red blood cells in a vicious cycle or are picked up by another mosquito, to be transported to another victim. • During pregnancy, the placenta becomes an additional infection site for the

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Plasmodium parasite, leading to consequences for the pregnancy too.

Present the transmission cycle using slide 8 • Depending on the intensity of disease transmission, areas are described as being endemic or non-endemic for malaria . Many areas in sub- Saharan Africa are endemic for malaria. In stable endemic areas, malaria transmission occurs all year round, with individuals continually exposed to the parasite. • Repeated exposure develops immunity, which results in less severe clinical malaria, particularly in adults. Only the very young (children under 5

years of age), pregnant women and visitors to the area are at risk. However, in non-endemic areas, people have lower immunities and epidemics may occur with devastating consequences for all.

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 9: Mechanisms leading to severe malaria and its complications (optional) • The continuing breakdown of red blood cells leads to anaemia. • You may present slide 9 to explain further the pathological changes that occur with the infection of RBCs by the Plasmodium parasite and how these lead to the complications that ensue.

Why are pregnant women more susceptible to malaria?

Slide 10: Why are pregnant women more susceptible to malaria? • Present slide 10. Ask participants to read the listed reasons why pregnant women are more susceptible to malaria infection. • Explain these points further and highlight the fact that malaria infection in pregnancy results in more frequent and severe clinical illness in the mother because of her increased vulnerability. • Note that this vulnerability is particularly greatest during first pregnancies.

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

• Women with subsequent pregnancies can develop and mount improved immune responses against malaria.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 107

The consequences of malaria in pregnancy (10 minutes)

Ask participants to list the consequences of malaria in pregnancy. Write down their answers on the flip chart.

Slide 11: Consequences of malaria in pregnancy • Present slide 11. • During pregnancy, malaria infection often leads to debilitating anaemia and severe complications such as cerebral malaria and even death. The pregnancy may end in miscarriage and/or the foetus may suffer growth re- tardation or stillbirth. • Complications in the newborn include prematurity, low birth weight and congenital malaria. • Explain that these complications are due to the higher rates of parasitaemia

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

(parasite loads) due to weakened resistance, additional sites for parasite development in the placenta, and the disruption of placenta function. • For women with pre-existing conditions such as HIV, sickle cell anaemia or pre-existing malarial anaemia leads to even graver outcomes.

Slide 12: Adverse consequences of malaria in pregnancy • Present slide 12 and refer participants to Table 9.2 in the participant manual. • Compare the list on the slide to the one provided by participants on the flip chart and fill in any gaps.

Strategies to reduce the burden of malaria in pregnancy (60 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Preventing and controlling malaria in pregnancy is a major global priority.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; All health care providers caring for pregnant women in sub-Saharan Africa must be proficient in the

prevention and management of malaria in pregnancy.

### 106 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask participants to share their experiences on strategies to reduce the burden of malaria in pregnancy.

Slide 13: Strategies to reduce the burden of malaria in pregnancy • Present slide 13. • Refer to Figure 9.3 in the participant manual. • A three-pronged approach is currently recommended to reduce the burden of malaria infection among pregnant women: 1. use of insecticide-treated nets (ITN); 2. intermittent preventive treatment in pregnancy (IPTp); and 3. case management of malaria illness.

Slide 14: Use of insecticide-treated nets (ITN) • Sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net is probably the most effective method for preventing mosquito bites at night when the pregnant woman is asleep. • ITNs prevent mosquito bites by repelling mosquitoes or killing them if they land on the net. All pregnant women should be counselled to obtain and sleep under an ITN.

## 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slides 15–18: Intermittent preventive treatment in pregnancy • Present slide 15. • The IPTp strategy assumes that every pregnant woman living in areas of high malaria transmission has malaria parasites in her blood or placenta, whether she has symptoms of malaria or not. • Pregnant women with malaria infection but no symptoms may still suffer health consequences such as anaemia, low birth weight and foetal death.

Slide 16: IPTp-SP dosing • Emphasize that IPTp-SP and SP can be safely administered from the beginning of the second trimester until delivery. • Refer to Table 9.3 in the participant manual and give five minutes for participants to read. • Explain that pregnant women should receive their first dose of IPTp-SP as early as possible at the beginning of the second trimester, defined as 13 weeks gestation. • The period between 13 and 20 weeks is critical for irreversible

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

negative consequences of MiP, when parasite densities are highest and major benefits can be achieved from malaria prevention. • Pregnant women should receive an additional dose of IPTp-SP at each contact with a health care worker trained to deliver IPTp-SP until the time of delivery, ensuring that doses of IPTp-SP are administered at least one month apart.

Slide 18: Precautions for SP administration • Although studies have reported no major adverse reactions to SP in mother or baby, potential complications are still possible. • It is important to screen all mothers prior to administering each SP dose. • If LMP is unknown, ensure that the client is in the second or third trimester by enquiring if quickening has occurred. • Screen the pregnant woman for history of allergy to sulphur. Ask the

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

pregnant woman if she has been recently treated with SP or another sulphur-containing medicine (within the past month). If so, wait until one month has passed before giving SP. • Check for G6PD defect, unexplained recurrent jaundice, and severe liver disease. If present, do not give IPTp-SP.

Slide 19: Other protective measures • When a woman cannot take SP, it is even more important for her to avoid mosquito bites that could transmit malaria. • Advise her on her increased risk from malaria and on other measures to prevent mosquito bites. • Discuss the additional measures to prevent malaria infection listed on the slide.

Case management of malaria illness in pregnancy

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Early diagnosis and treatment of malaria reduces disease and prevents deaths. It also contributes to

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

reducing malaria transmission. Despite preventive measures, some pregnant women will still become infected with malaria and present with clinical symptoms.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; These women must be promptly and adequately treated to prevent the development of severe malaria

with life-threatening consequences, including maternal and/or foetal death.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 109

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slides 20 and 21: Malaria case management and diagnosis • Malaria is identified by its signs and symptoms and through laboratory investigation. In areas where malaria is endemic, many people know the symptoms of malaria and may diagnose the problem and even attempt to treat themselves before reporting to the clinic. • Using clinical decision-making skills, care providers can diagnose malaria by gathering information through history, examination and confirmatory laboratory testing where possible.

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Malaria is classified into clinical categories: • uncomplicated and complicated (severe) malaria. • Uncomplicated malaria is defined as symptomatic malaria without signs of vital organ involvement. The signs and symptoms of uncomplicated malaria are non-specific. Present slide 21 and task a participant to read out the clinical signs and symptoms of uncomplicated malaria (Table 9.4). • Severe (or complicated) malaria is defined as symptomatic malaria with signs

of vital organ dysfunction, either clinical or laboratory.

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

Slide 22: Complicated or severe malaria • Present slide 22 and task another participant to read out the signs and symptoms of complicated/severe malaria. (table 9.4) • Ask participants to highlight the differences between the two categories. Point out that the main differences are that the signs of severe malaria are those indicating vital organ involvement, whereas the signs of uncomplicated malaria are non-specific. • Emphasize that when a midwife sees a woman who has malaria, he/she

must distinguish between uncomplicated malaria and severe malaria, to ensure that the woman receives the correct treatment.

### 108 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

Slide 23: Laboratory investigation • Ask participants which laboratory test they perform to confirm diagnosis of malaria. Do they always perform the test prior to treatment? • Present slide 23. • Emphasize that all cases of suspected malaria should be confirmed using parasite-based diagnostic testing. • However, the absence of laboratory tests should not prevent the clinical diagnosis and treatment of malaria.

Slide 24: Excluding other causes of fever during pregnancy • Ask participants to list other common conditions that they see which may be mistaken for malaria and considered as differential diagnosis. Write down their answers on the flip chart. • Present slide 24 and compare the answers to those provided on the flip chart. Fill in any gaps. • Ask participants to explain how they will distinguish these conditions from malaria. Refer them to the PCPNC guide (see PCPNC Chart C8).

## 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Treating uncomplicated malaria during pregnancy

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; After the health care provider has diagnosed malaria and determined whether it is uncomplicated or

severe, he/she must provide care promptly.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the diagnosis is uncomplicated malaria, the objective of the treatment is to cure the infection as rapidly

as possible. This prevents progression to severe malaria.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Prompt cure of the infection also achieves the public health goal of reducing transmission of malaria

infection. Refer participants to Table 9.5 in their manual (see below), which deals with steps to treat uncomplicated malaria.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask participants to take turns reading out each care component (treat and advise and related

instructions) in this table. Allow participants to ask questions and clarify any challenges.

Table 9.5: Management of uncomplicated malaria during pregnancy

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Treat and advise; Instructions

- Dòng dữ liệu: Give medicines; •; Refer to national guidelines for the recommended medicines and

- Dòng dữ liệu: to treat malaria; dosing regimens for treatment of malaria in pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Usually, any client diagnosed with malaria during any trimester of

pregnancy can be treated with quinine.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Artemisinin-based combination therapy, such as artesunate-

amodiaquine or artesunate-lumefantrine, is recommended for use only in the second or third trimesters of pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give the pregnant woman the recommended medicine and

number of doses for a complete treatment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask her to take the ﬁrst dose while you are watching.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Give an; •; Such as paracetamol to lower fever: 1 gram (2 tablets) every 6 hours,

- Dòng dữ liệu: antipyretic; if needed.

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: Advise the; Teach the woman how and when to take the antimalarial medicines.

- Dòng dữ liệu: pregnant woman; Advise the woman to:

− follow the instructions to take all the antimalarial medicines; − take paracetamol to lower the body temperature, if needed; − continue taking iron/folate tablets and consume iron-rich foods to prevent or treat malarial anaemia; and − sleep under an ITN and use other measures to prevent mosquito bites. Answer any questions that she may have.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Ask her to come; •; Ensure that the woman knows the danger signs during

- Dòng dữ liệu: for a follow-up; pregnancy. Advise her to return to the facility for a follow-up visit

- Dòng dữ liệu: visit; after two days, or sooner if she observes any danger signs or her

condition worsens.

- Dòng dữ liệu: At the follow-up; •; If her symptoms have improved, tell her when to return for her next

- Dòng dữ liệu: visit, check that; ANC visit.

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: her symptoms; •; If her symptoms have not improved or have worsened, or if she has

- Dòng dữ liệu: have improved; any symptoms that suggest severe malaria, stabilize and refer the

woman to hospital immediately.

- Dòng dữ liệu: One month after •; Give the ﬁrst dose of IPTp with SP at the next visit if she has not

- Dòng dữ liệu: completion of the; previously received it. Follow the IPTp schedule to give the next dose

- Dòng dữ liệu: malaria; if IPTp was started previously.

treatment, give or resume IPTp

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 111

Management of severe malaria in pregnancy

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The objective of antimalarial treatment in severe malaria is to prevent maternal death.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The risk of death from severe malaria is greatest in the first 24 hours. It is therefore recommended that the

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

affected patient be stabilized and referred immediately to a hospital. o Ask participants if they refer all such patients to hospital. Note their answers.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain that the treatment of a pregnant woman with malaria in a hospital is important as it will include

checking for and managing life-threatening complications relating to organ dysfunction such as severe anaemia, hypoglycaemia, and kidney and pulmonary malfunction.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Appendix 6 provides information on medicines and procedures for the treatment of severe malaria in a hospital.

Parenteral antimalarials are required for the treatment of severe malaria. Give participants five minutes to review Appendix 6 in their manual.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; However, the woman should be managed according to national treatment protocols in place. If possible, pre-

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

referral treatment should include the first dose of quinine and paracetamol if she can swallow tablets. Remember that a woman in the first trimester of pregnancy should not be given artemether. A care provider must also accompany the sick woman to the hospital.

Slide 25: Treating malaria during pregnancy • Present slide 25 to summarize the management and treatment guidelines for uncomplicated and severe malaria highlighting the differences.

Class activity 1: case studies – malaria diagnosis and treatment (30 minutes)

Slide 26: Case studies and class activity instructions • Present slide 26 and inform the class that in this individual class exercise, they will use information provided in case studies to diagnose malaria and specify the appropriate treatment for the case. • Present instructions for the activity and ensure that all participants understand the assignment. • Refer them to case studies 1 and 2 on pages …. of their manual.

### 110 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

• Optional – present slides 27, 28, 29 and 30 (read out the case studies and related questions). Allow the class to work on the assignment for 30 minutes. • Feedback: When all participants have completed the exercise, ask them to share their answers, then present the correct answers using the answer sheets below. Allow the class to discuss the answers and clarify any discrepancies.

## 112 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 112 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Answer sheets for activity 1: case studies

Case study 1: Madam Fati

## Part A:

### Part A: — phần 1

Fati is 30 years old. She is approximately 20 weeks pregnant with her second baby. She has come to the antenatal clinic for her first ANC visit complaining of severe headache and fever. A “Quick check” was performed and with no emergency signs found. Fati and her family moved to the area six months ago. She has never suffered from malaria.

Questions

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; What will you include in your initial assessment of Fati?

Answer The assessment should include all the recommended steps regarding the first antenatal visit, including: • history (personal information, menstrual and contraceptive history, present pregnancy, obstetric history, medical and medication history, daily habits and lifestyle); • physical examination (including general well-being and BP, visual breast examination, abdominal and genital examination); and

### Part A: — phần 2

• testing (including haemoglobin, syphilis screening, HIV testing after VCT and consent, blood group and Rh if available, urine for protein, urine for glucose if indicated).

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.; What aspects of Fati’s physical examination will help you identify her problems and needs?

Answer Fati’s general appearance, her blood pressure, temperature, respiration, pulse, pallor in eyelids (to check for anaemia), signs of dehydration (loose dry skin, sunken eyes) and abdominal examination (for fundal height, position and lie, and foetal heart sounds) will help identify her problems and needs.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 3.; What screening procedures and laboratory tests will you include (if available) in your assessment of

Fati?

Answer Check: haemoglobin for anaemia; malaria test; urine for protein (if blood pressure is greater than 140/90 mmHg, to rule out pre-eclampsia).

## Part B:

### Part B: — phần 1

After assessing Fati, your main findings include the following:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; History: Fati states that she has been well during this her pregnancy and only developed fever and headache

yesterday morning. She says that she does not have any other symptoms such as cough, painful urination, abdominal pain, or leaking of fluid. She has not had convulsions or loss of consciousness. She has not taken any medication. She does not have a bed net.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Physical examination: Fati’s temperature is 38.7 oC; her blood pressure is 122/68 mmHg; her pulse rate is

80 beats per minute; and her respiration rate is 18 breaths per minute. Fati is not pale. Her fundal height is 23 cm (which is compatible with the dates of her last menstrual period) and foetal heart tones are 140 beats per minute.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Laboratory testing: Her haemoglobin is 10.5 g/dl. The thick blood film test for malaria is positive.

### Part B: — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 113

Case study 1: Fati (continued)

Questions

- Dòng dữ liệu: 4.; Based on these findings, what are Fati’s problems and needs?

Answer

She has uncomplicated malaria and anaemia, both of which need to be treated. Since she recently moved into the area, she needs education on malaria prevention, and a bed net. She has just begun her second trimester of pregnancy and needs ANC.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 5.; What is your plan of care for Fati?

Answer

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Treatment for malaria with the recommended antimalarial medicine and number of doses for a complete

treatment. Also, paracetamol to lower the fever.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Advice on how and when to take the antimalarial medicines and paracetamol and to return for follow-up after

two days or sooner if she gets worse.

### Part B: — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Treatment for anaemia with iron and folic acid twice a day.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; ANC, including developing a birth and emergency plan with the woman, and after screening, tetanus

immunization and deworming with mebendazole.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counselling on pertinent health issues, including malaria prevention, nutrition, self-care, safer sex, preventive

medicines, quitting smoking and alcohol, and taking only medicines given by a health care provider.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; At her next ANC visit that occurs one month or more after malaria treatment has been completed, give IPTp-SP.

Case study 2: Victoria

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Victoria is 24 years old. She reported at the antenatal clinic looking very ill and weak. During “Quick check”, she

### Part B: — phần 4

told the care provider at the antenatal clinic that she was three months pregnant and had been ill with fever, headaches, body aches, dark urine and vomiting for the past four days. She had taken some paracetamol tablets but saw no improvement. She has no cough, neck pains or painful urination. Her stools are normal, and she has no abdominal pain.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Upon rapid assessment, you note the following: she has slight yellowish discoloration of the eyes; she is pale;

and her temperature is 39 oC. Her tongue is dry, and her eyes are slightly sunken. Her blood pressure is 100/60 mmHg, her pulse 100/min, and volume weak. Her uterus is felt just above the pubis.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; You suspect she has severe malaria since her address indicates that she lives in the slum area of the city where

malaria is a problem, and her signs indicate vital organ involvement. You perform a rapid diagnostic test for malaria, and it is positive.

### Part B: — phần 5

Questions

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; Which other important laboratory tests would you carry out?

Answer

Haemoglobin to determine if she is anaemic. HIV test if she has not tested in last three months and she consents.

## 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Case study 2: Victoria (continued)

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.; How will you treat her malaria?

Answer

She has severe malaria, so she needs to be stabilized and referred promptly to a hospital. If she can swallow, she will be given a first dose of oral quinine and paracetamol.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 3.; What other treatments will you provide?

Answer

IV line with dextrose 5% solution and quinine if she is vomiting.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 4.; What advice would you give to Victoria after treatment and recovery from malaria?

Answer

Will educate her on the causes of malaria and how to prevent it by sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net and avoiding mosquito bites. Will also advise her to return for IPTp-SP after one month; take one iron and folic acid tablet daily, or if she is anaemic, twice daily; and to eat foods high in iron (describe what those foods are).

### 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: 5.; What measures would you take to ensure that Victoria recovers completely and remains healthy?

Answer

Have her come for regular ANC visits (including preventive medicines); continue refilling her iron and folic acid tablets; encourage her to eat a healthy diet as advised and to practice safer sex; and prepare a birth and emergency plan with her and her family.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 115

### 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Preventing and managing malaria in pregnant women with special high-risk conditions (20 minutes) Slide 31: Malaria in pregnant women with special high-risk conditions • Some women in the sub-region have additional health conditions that make them even more susceptible to malaria infection during pregnancy. This further aggravates the situation with severer consequences for the mother and her baby. • Three such conditions include: 1. HIV 2. sickle cell disease 3. pre-existing nutritional anaemia.

Reasons why women with these conditions remain at high risk • Ask participants why they think the conditions in slide 31 are special risks for malaria in pregnancy. • Listen to their answers and present slides 32 and 33 to explain this further.

### 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slides 34, 35 and 36: Malaria-related care for women with special risk conditions Closer vigilance is required during antenatal care for women with the above three high-risk conditions for malaria in pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; When seen at all antenatal contacts, the provider must probe for symptoms

suggestive of malaria and assess carefully for the presence of fever, anaemia and the well-being of the baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel the woman and her family about her increased risk for severe

malaria.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Perform a blood film or rapid test for malaria parasites at the first visit and

at all later visits if possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Give her information on other protective measures against mosquito bites.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel her on the additional danger signs of malaria in pregnancy for

women with her pre-existing condition (for example, signs of sickling crises and severe anaemia)

### 114 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel her to report early to the health facility if she has any symptoms of

malaria. She should avoid self-treatment at home.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Emphasize that if malaria develops, the care provider must quickly confirm

the diagnosis – if possible, initiate treatments and refer her promptly to a hospital for further management.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Note that in these women, malaria can rapidly progress from uncomplicated

to severe with life-threatening effects within a few hours.

## 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Class activity 2: role play – malaria prevention counselling for a client with a special risk condition (30 minutes)

Slide 37: Class activity 2 – counselling women with special risk conditions for malaria in pregnancy • The purpose of this role play is to demonstrate and observe and practice counselling a client who has a special risk condition about preventing malaria. • You may choose to conduct this role play as a “play” performed by three participants for the entire class, as described below, or as a practice session in which everyone participates.

• If you choose the latter, organize participants into groups of three and ask them to do the role play at the same time. If time allows, ask them to repeat it two or three times, playing different roles each time. • Present slide 37 to give instructions to participants.

### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Instructions for demonstration and practice • Select three participants to perform the following roles: 1. health care provider (midwife) 2. antenatal client with special need (sickle cell anaemia in pregnancy) 3. Support person (for example, family member) • Task the three participants taking part in the role play to read about their roles in the participant manual and then take a few minutes to prepare. Also see charts below.

• The rest of the participants will be observers. Observers should also prepare by reviewing the Learning guide for antenatal counselling, the instructions on preventing malaria in women with special risk conditions, and other information about malaria prevention. • The role play should last up to 10 minutes. • After the demonstration, the class will discuss the counselling process and messages.
