### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Case scenario: read out the following scenario to introduce the role play The client is 17 years old, unmarried, and pregnant for the first time. She has come to the facility for the first ANC visit, presenting at six months’ gestation. The midwife took her history and did a physical examination. The client’s mother said that her daughter has sickle cell anaemia. She has had malaria a few times and

sometimes had to go to the hospital because of it. She lives in the city with relatives during the school year but is living at home in this rural area with her mother now. From her history and examination, the midwife finds no other problems. However, her haemoglobin is 8.0g/dL. A malaria test is negative. The midwife will now counsel this woman (and her mother) about the danger of malaria for a person with sickle cell anaemia and how to prevent it.

### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 117

Participant roles

Health care provider You are an experienced midwife who has good counselling skills. Refer to Appendix 7 and the information pro- vided on malaria prevention in this session to prepare yourself to counsel this young woman about preventing malaria. You may also want to review the Learning guide on antenatal counselling.

Client You are 17 years old, unmarried, and pregnant for the first time. Your mother has brought you to the facility for the first ANC visit. You are about six months pregnant. You have sickle cell anaemia. You have had malaria a few times in your life and you were admitted into the hospital a couple of times.

Support person You are the mother of the client. You care for your daughter, but you are obviously angry with her for becoming pregnant out of wedlock.

### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Observers The observers should note the following during the counselling process. If the midwife has:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; assessed the woman’s situation;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; defined the problem and the information needs of the client;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Generated appropriate messages about preventing malaria for the client in line with her special condition;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; checked that the client and support person understand what is being discussed; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; helped the client plan how she would protect herself from malaria.

Feedback session for role play: At the end of the role play, ask for comments from the observers using the following points as a guide to facilitate the discussions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How did the midwife prepare her room for the counselling activity?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How did the midwife approach the client and her support person?

### 116 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Did the midwife give the client and her support person enough information about why she is at special

risk for malaria?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How did the midwife check for the client and support person’s understanding of their high risk for malaria?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; What did the midwife do to demonstrate emotional support and reassurance?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Did the midwife provide enough information on how the client can protect herself against malaria in

pregnancy and did she help the client reach a decision on what to do?

Allow participants to comment on these questions extensively whilst pointing out the good counselling skills and practices.

## 118 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 118 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Key messages (10 minutes)

Slides 38 and 39: Key messages End Session 9 by presenting the following key messages 1. WHO-recommended strategies for controlling malaria in pregnancy include: (a) sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net; (b) intermittent preventive treatment with SP (at each scheduled ANC visit during second and third trimesters of pregnancy); and (c) prompt case management of malaria illness. 2. IPTp-SP should be administered under directly observed therapy (DOT),

starting as early as possible in the second trimester (13 to 16 weeks), with doses given at least one month apart until the time of delivery. 3. Recommended antimalarial medicines in pregnancy include quinine and artemisinin-based combination therapies (ACTs). 4. Treat malaria according to your national guidelines. 5. Pregnant women with special risk conditions such as HIV infection, sickle cell anaemia and pre-existing anaemia due to other causes suffer

### 118 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

severe consequences when they get malaria infection during pregnancy. If they develop malaria, refer the woman promptly to a hospital for treatment.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 119

Session 10: Prevention and management of anaemia in pregnancy • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • Up-to-date national antenatal care guidelines in line with WHO recommendations on antenatal care for a posi- tive pregnancy experience (2016) • WHO’s Managing complications in pregnancy and childbirth • Relevant national nutrition guidelines • Laptop • A list of all available haematinics (blood tonics) and their composition (iron folate content)

Introduction

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 3: Session outline Welcome and introduce participants to the session by presenting slides 1 and 2

### 118 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Anaemia is a condition in which there is a reduced number of red blood cells or the haemoglobin

concentration within the red blood cells is lower than normal.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask participants why it is important to prevent and manage anemia in pregnancy and write their answers

on a flip chart.

Slide 3: Aim • Present slide 3 or ask a participant to read the aim of the session.

AIM To enable participants to understand the burden of the problem of anaemia in pregnancy, its common causes, and prevention strategies; and to develop skills for diagnosis and management of anaemia.

## 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 4: Session objectives • Present slide 4 or ask participants to read in turn the objectives of the session.

Objectives: At the end of the session, participants will be able to:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; describe the burden of the problem of anaemia in pregnancy and its effects

on the pregnant woman and her baby;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; define the common causes of anaemia in sub-Saharan Africa;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; describe strategies for preventing anaemia in pregnancy;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel a pregnant woman about prevention of anaemia in pregnancy;

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; correctly manage and/or refer cases of anaemia in pregnancy; and

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; explain why pregnant women with HIV or sickle cell disease face additional

risks and complications from anaemia during pregnancy.

The burden and consequences of anaemia in pregnancy

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 5: The burden of anaemia in pregnancy •• Anaemia is a global public health problem affecting both developing and developed countries at all ages. During pregnancy, it is an important cause of morbidity and mortality. It is estimated that one out of 10 maternal deaths can be attributed to iron deficiency anaemia. • Ask participants about the prevalence of anaemia in pregnancy in their settings. • Present slide 5 (10 minutes) to describe the global and regional burdens of

anaemia in pregnancy. • According to recent estimates, about forty-two per cent (41.8%) of pregnant women in the world are anaemic. • In sub-Saharan Africa, the prevalence of anaemia in pregnancy is higher, about 50%.

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 6: Effects of anaemia in pregnancy Highlight the following facts: • Anaemia in pregnancy is a major contributor to poor maternal and newborn outcomes. • It is associated with increased risks of low-birth-weight newborns, premature birth and perinatal mortality. • For the mother, anaemia is associated with poor maternal well-being, increased susceptibility to infections, and increased risk of death from major obstetric complications.

In fact, the risk for maternal death has been shown to decrease by 20% for every 1 g/dL increase in haemoglobin level.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 121

Defining anaemia in pregnancy

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 7: WHO definition of anaemia in pregnancy • According to the World Health Organization (WHO), anaemia is defined as haemoglobin (Hb) levels <12.0 g/dL in non-pregnant women and <13.0 g/ dL in men. • During pregnancy, anaemia is diagnosed when a woman’s haemoglobin concentration is found to be less than 11.0 g/dL during the first trimester or lower than 10.5 g/dL during the second trimester. o Moderate anaemia: Hb >7 g/dL and <11 g/dL o Severe anaemia: Hb <7 g/dL

• Ask participants why lower Hb levels are required for diagnosing anaemia in pregnancy. • Explain the physiological basis if this is not known by the class.

Common causes of anaemia during pregnancy in sub-Saharan Africa

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 8: Common causes of anaemia in pregnancy in sub-Saharan Africa • Ask participants to list the common causes of anaemia in pregnancy in their settings. Note their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 8 and compare the answers provided to those listed on the slide and fill in the knowledge gaps. • Explain how prevalent these conditions are in the sub-region and how they cause anaemia. • Remind participants that a pregnant woman may have more than one cause of anaemia.

Preventing anaemia in pregnancy: WHO-recommended strategies and related ANC plan (30 minutes)

### 120 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slide 9: Preventing anaemia in pregnancy • Ask participants to list and/or describe the strategies in their respective settings to prevent anaemia in pregnancy. Record these on a flip chart. • Encourage a discussion on how well these strategies are being implemented, including the challenges. • Present slide 9 to review the WHO strategies and determine if all these strategies are known and implemented in participants’ settings.

• Refer to the care plan for prevention of anaemia in pregnancy in the participant manual). • Task a participant each to read out a component of the plan and emphasize the importance of each component.

## 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Class activity: Homework

Slide 10: Class activity – homework • A variety of locally available foods exist that are rich in iron and folate. It is important that care providers know about these foods so that they can advise their clients. • Several medicines (blood tonics) sold over the counter in most countries are purported to be ideal for pregnant women. • Very often these medicines do not all contain the WHO-recommended vvdosage levels for iron and folate to meet the needs of pregnancy.

• It is important for care providers to know about such medicines so they can advise clients and prescribe the right ones. o Task participants to find out about the aforementioned foods and medications in a homework activity. o Present slide 11 and explain the instructions for this activity.

### 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Class activity 1: Feedback discussion • It is recommended that participants are given adequate time to research and obtain answers to the assignment. This will require at least 24 hours. The feedback discussion can be held one day later or during the skills practice discussions on day 5. • Ask participants to list the iron/folate-rich foods and provide evidence to support the selection of these foods. Record their answers on a flip chart.

• Ask them to also list the medicines/products that they reviewed and the iron and folic acid content of each product. Record them on a flip chart. • For each product, ask participants whether the daily intake will meet the daily dose of iron and folic acid needed for supplementation during pregnancy. • Ask whether the iron content is sufficient for the treatment of anaemia during pregnancy. • Caution that health workers need to be very careful about recommending or endorsing such products

### 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

unless they are sure of what they provide. • Usually, the iron and folic acid tablets prescribed at the health facility are better than these more expensive products.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 123

Identifying and treating pregnant women with anaemia (30 minutes)

Slide 11: Identifying pregnant women with anaemia • At the antenatal clinic, all pregnant women should be screened for anaemia at every antenatal visit. • Refer to PCPNC Chart C4 to classify the signs of anaemia. Have participants take turns reading aloud until you have reviewed the entire chart. • Present slide 11 to emphasize these symptoms. • Note that during client assessment, it is also important to determine the underlying cause of anaemia.

### 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

• Ask participants to list questions they could ask to help identify the cause. • The diagnosis of anaemia and its causes and severity must always be confirmed through laboratory tests. This is important for monitoring treatment response.

Slide 12: Treating the pregnant woman with anaemia • Key factors that influence the care plan for the pregnant woman with anaemia include: the cause(s) of anaemia, its level of severity, and the woman’s gestational age at the time of diagnosis. • Ask a participant each to read out the explanations given in their manual on how these key factors influence the treatment options and care plan. • Give additional explanations if needed.

### 122 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 13: Managing anaemia in pregnancy • Refer participants to Figure 10.3 below (Flow chart on management of anaemia in pregnancy) – and present slide 12. • Using Figure 10.2, highlight how the severity of anaemia, potential causes and gestational age have all influenced the care plan outlined. • Emphasize that any pregnant woman whose haemoglobin is less than 11 g/ dL has anaemia and should be treated with iron and folic acid tablets, one tablet two times a day.

Review the chart in Figure 10.2 with participants to be sure that they understand how to read it and how it may be used.

## 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Figure 10.2 Flow chart for managing anaemia in pregnancy

Screen all pregnant women for anaemia. Check haemoglobin levels at booking and at all visits (if possible)

Haemoglobin is <11 g/dL = anaemia in pregnancy Do a comprehensive assessment for underlying cause of anaemia

Haemoglobin is <7 g/dL (severe anaemia) Haemoglobin is >7 and <11 g/dL and/or woman is symptomatic for anaemia at (moderate anaemia) any gestation

- Dòng dữ liệu: Refer urgently to a; Gestation; Gestation

- Dòng dữ liệu: hospital with; >28 weeks; < 28 weeks

- Dòng dữ liệu: blood transfusion; Refer for

- Dòng dữ liệu: facility for; delivery in

- Dòng dữ liệu: admission and; hospital

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

appropriate care* • Give one iron and folic acid tablet twice daily (60 mg iron and 400 mcg folic acid per tablet) and/or • Give IPTp-SP – ﬁrst dose as early as possible in the second trimester and at each scheduled ANC visit during the second and third trimesters • Counsel on nutrition • Deworm once after ﬁrst trimester • Monitor haemoglobin at all visits until normal • Monitor foetal growth closely • Identify and treat other causes of anaemia

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; See “Treat and Advise” column in Chart C4 and Chart F3 in the

PCPNC guide – refer to anaemia guidelines

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 125

Monitoring response to iron/folate treatment

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 14: Monitoring response to treatment with iron and folate • Effective treatment of anaemia involves regular follow-up of the patient to check on treatment response. • In many patients with iron deficiency anaemia, haemoglobin levels generally respond quickly to treatment with oral iron therapy. • However, in some patients, the effectiveness of the treatment can become compromised by poor absorption of the iron, gastrointestinal discomforts

(nausea, vomiting, constipation and abdominal discomfort) and non- adherence to treatment. • Expected Hb rise (if treatment is effective) is 1.0 g/dL two weeks after initiation of treatment, and thereafter about 0.5 g//dL weekly.

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Describe and discuss steps that must be taken to monitor treatment response. All pregnant women with anaemia started on oral iron therapy should be seen and have their haemoglobin levels checked again two weeks after initiation of iron treatment. The expected Hb rise (if treatment is effective) is 1.0 g/dL.

If haemoglobin rise is adequate and there are no challenges: • Review woman again at two to four weekly intervals (depending on severity of anaemia and gestational age). • Check haemoglobin levels at all subsequent visits to determine if response to treatment is still satisfactory.

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

If haemoglobin rise is unsatisfactory and//or there are challenges: • The care provider must find out why response to treatment is unsatisfactory. Ask questions such as: o Has the client procured the prescribed iron and folate medication? o Is she taking them twice a day as ordered? o Does she have any discomforts or concerns associated with taking her medication such us nausea, vomiting, bloating, constipation or diarrhoea?

• If the client has challenges due to intolerance and GI upsets, the provider can suggest the following: o Take medication with meals and with vitamin C-rich foods such as oranges or mangoes o Change to other iron preparations such as iron fumarate and iron gluconate • The client must be reviewed again in two weeks for response. If response is still unsatisfactory refer the woman to a hospital for other treatment options, such as intravenous iron.

• Note: No pregnant woman should enter into labour with anaemia.

### 124 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Emphasize the correct dosage for treatment of anaemia in pregnancy and the criticality of educating

the client on the importance of taking iron as prescribed, on common side-effects, and how they can be managed.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Emphasize that, in a hospital setting, trained medical doctors or experienced midwives may give

parenteral iron and blood transfusion for treatment of anaemia. These clients must be monitored very closely during treatment.

## 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Managing anaemia in special risk groups: HIV infection, haemolytic anaemia (30 minutes) (refer to slide 13)

Slide 15: Managing anaemia in special risk groups • Pregnant women with HIV infection or haemolytic anaemia, such as sickle cell anaemia or G6PD, are special high-risk anaemia patients. • Ask participants why these women have especially high risk for severe anaemia and its complications? Note and correct the answers given. • Women presenting with these risk conditions and with anaemia should be managed at hospital level and by doctors with the relevant skills.

• Refer participants to Figure 10.3 in the participant manual. Task participants to read out sections of the chart. • Discuss and explain the various care plans, emphasizing how they differ from routine care.

Class activity 2: Case studies

### 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 16: Class activity 2 – case studies • The purpose of class activity 2 is to enable participants to use their clinical decision-making skills and the care planning guides provided in the session to manage cases of anaemia in pregnancy. • Present slide 16 to provide instructions for this activity. Note that this is an individual exercise.

Slide 17: Case study – Jemila Slide 18: Case study – Madam Bie • Refer participants to the case studies in the participant manual. • Present slides 17 and 18 or have participants read out the case studies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 127

### 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Feedback and discussion • Task co-facilitators to supervise the class activity and individually review participants’ answers when they have completed the assignment. • When all participants have completed the assignment, the lead facilitator will give feedback to the entire group. Encourage participants to share their answers and compare them to the possible answers provided below in italics.

CASE STUDY 1: Jemila Ms Jemila is 23 years old, She lives in the poor part of town and has had three children. Her last child is 15 months old. She is now three months pregnant and is reporting to you for her first ANC visit. She is feeling well and has no health complaints. Her uterine size corresponds to 12 gestation weeks. You examine her and she looks pale. Her Hb is 8.5 g/dL. Her sickling test is negative. Her urine and stools are negative. Blood test is negative for malaria parasites.

Question 1: What treatments will you give Jemila?

### 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Start her on iron and folate tablets twice per day

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask her to return after two weeks’ gestation, at which time you will give her one dose of mebendazole and

start her on IPTp-SP

Question 2: What counselling messages related to anaemia will you give her?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain the causes of anaemia and the importance of taking iron and folate tablets to treat or prevent

anaemia.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Advise her to: take iron tablets with foods containing vitamin C, which improves the absorption of the iron;

and take the iron tablets with food. Gastrointestinal side-effects that are likely to occur if iron is taken on an empty stomach include nausea, vomiting, constipation and diarrhoea.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Advise her to: eat foods rich in iron, folate, vitamin C, B vitamins and protein-rich foods. Avoid foods that

### 126 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

inhibit iron absorption, including tea, coffee, cola, bran, and other unhealthy “foods” such as clay.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Provide examples of iron-rich foods: red meat (especially liver), poultry, fish, whole grains, dark green leafy

vegetables, shellfish, dried fruits. Examples of folate-rich foods are beans, dark green leafy vegetables, spinach, citrus fruits, mangos, avocados.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain how to prevent malaria: sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net, avoiding mosquito bites,

attending ANC visits to receive IPT.

Question 3: When will you ask her to return to the clinic for follow-up?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; She should return in two weeks for follow-up: to check whether her haemoglobin level is improving, to

receive mebendazole, and to start IPTp with SP.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; She can come sooner if she has any concerns.

## 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Case study 2: Madam Bie Madam Bie is eight months pregnant. This is her second antenatal visit to your clinic. She came for her first visit when she was six months pregnant. At her first visit two months ago, her haemoglobin level was 7.5 g/dL. You gave her a prescription for tabs ferrous sulphate to take one tablet (60 mg elemental iron) twice daily and tabs folic acid 400 mcg once daily. You also started her on the required dose of IPTp-SP. You asked her to report back for a check-up in

two weeks, but she did not come back until now. At this second visit, her haemoglobin is 8.5 g/dL.

Question 1: Is the improvement in her haemoglobin level satisfactory? Explain your answer.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; No, the improvement in her Hb level is NOT satisfactory. Her haemoglobin would have been normal around 11.5

g/dL if she was taking her treatment as planned.

Question 2: What questions will you ask her?

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Why she did not come for her follow-up check-up at two weeks

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If she obtained and was still taking her medications

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If she has had any challenges with taking her iron/folate medications

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If she has signs and symptoms suggestive of worsening anaemia

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If she has noticed any jaundice, bloody urine, worms in her stool or bleeding from vagina

Question 3: What additional actions and treatments will you undertake now?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel her on how to take the iron pills; counsel her on recommended iron-rich foods and malaria

prevention; provide her with iron and folate tablets, two per day (morning and evening); and provide IPTp-SP and mebendazole.

Question 4: What messages will you address in counselling?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; She has anaemia and it is extremely important that she takes her iron pills to treat the anaemia and improve the

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

haemoglobin level as much as possible before childbirth. The dose of iron tablets should be double – one with the morning meal and one with the evening meal, preferably taking the tablet with citrus to improve absorption.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Eat foods rich in iron, folate, vitamin C, B vitamins and protein.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoid foods that inhibit iron absorption, including tea, coffee, cola, bran, and other unhealthy “foods” such as

clay.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Examples of iron-rich foods are red meat (especially liver), poultry, fish, whole grains, dark green leafy

vegetables, shellfish, dried fruits. Examples of folate-rich foods are beans, dark green leafy vegetables, spinach, citrus fruits, mangos, avocados.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Because of her anaemia, she has an increased chance of complications. She should revise her birth plan to give

birth at a hospital with blood transfusion facilities. She should identify blood donors.

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Counsel her on danger signs in pregnancy and complication readiness

Question 5: When will you tell her to return to the health centre?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; She should return to the clinic every week for monitoring until childbirth.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 129

Key messages (10 minutes)

Slides 19–21: Key messages Present slides 19–21 to conclude the session and summarize the key messages. • Anaemia during pregnancy is associated with significant health consequences for both the mother and her baby. • WHO strategies for controlling anaemia in pregnancy include: iron/folate supplementation to all pregnant women; control of malaria in pregnancy by IPTp with SP and sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net; control

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

of hookworm infestation by deworming; nutrition counselling; and optimal birth spacing. • Whether they are symptomatic or not, all pregnant women should be screened for anaemia at every antenatal visit. • Any pregnant woman whose haemoglobin is less than 11 g/dL has anaemia and should be treated with iron and folic acid tablets, one tablet twice a day. • Important considerations for developing a care plan for clients with anaemia

in pregnancy include the severity of the anaemia, gestational age at the time of diagnosis, and the underlying cause(s) of the anaemia. • The general principle is that “No pregnant woman should enter labour with anaemia”. • The expected haemoglobin rise (if treatment is effective) is 1g/dL after two weeks following initiation of iron therapy. Thereafter, Hb levels increase at rates of 0.5–1.0 g/dL. • Pregnant women with anaemia who have HIV infection or haemolytic

### 128 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

anaemia, such as sickle cell anaemia or G6PD, are special high-risk anaemia patients. They should be managed at hospital level and by doctors with the relevant skills.

## 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Session 11: Integrated approach to the prevention of mother-to-child transmission of human immunodeficiency virus (HIV), hepatitis B, syphilis and tuberculosis • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • National HIV prevention and control guidelines • National strategy for the elimination/prevention of mother-to-child transmission (PMTCT or eMTCT) • National STI prevention and control guidelines

• Guidelines for the prevention, care and treatment of chronic hepatitis B infections • WHO guideline on syphilis screening and treatment for pregnant women • WHO policy advice on dual elimination and triple elimination • Laptop • Table and chairs for role play

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Before teaching this session, familiarize yourself with all the above-mentioned reference materials,

especially with the national policy/protocols, including algorithms for testing and treatment in your country.

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Check that the training content is consistent with existing national protocols and, if not, revise the text and

slides as needed.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; If the national guidelines and protocols are not up to date, use the training materials and WHO guidelines

indicated above for reference.

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline • Present slides 1 and 2 to welcome participants and introduce the session (2 minutes)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 131

Introduction (5 minutes)

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 3: Aim and objectives Present slide 3 to describe the aim and objectives of the session: Aim: To enable participants to implement integrated counselling, testing and treatment of HIV, hepatitis B, (HBV), syphilis and tuberculosis (TB) during antenatal care. Objectives • Provide key information on HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB infections. • Describe the key strategies for preventing mother-to-child transmission of HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB.

• Provide counselling and testing for HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB. • Provide care and treatment for pregnant women with HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB.

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 4: Overview of HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB • Ask participants to reflect on the four diseases mentioned in the aims and objectives. • Ask if they see pregnant women with these conditions in their settings. • Ask participants if they know why these four infectious diseases have been prioritized for intervention. • Present slide 4 to highlight and explain the reasons listed. • Emphasize that babies can also get these infections from their mothers

during the pregnancy, and/or soon after birth when they are breast feeding.

Slide 5: Preventing mother-to-child transmission of HIV, HBV, TB and syphilis • Ask participants to describe ways through which we can prevent babies from getting these infections from their mothers. Note their answers. • Present the information on the slide and compare the strategies listed to the answers given and fill in the gaps.

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Highlight the following points: • Preventing MTCT of infectious diseases during pregnancy and providing appropriate treatment for the infected pregnant woman are a key objective of ANC. • WHO recommends providing an integrated care package that links ANC to relevant clinical services for these infections, such as HIV testing services (HTS), TB screening, SRH, and other harm reduction programmes. • The primary purpose of such integration is to make care more convenient

for pregnant women coming for ANC and presenting with these conditions. • Integrating ANC with these services also creates the opportunity to promote testing uptake, diagnose coinfections and start care and treatment services for all co-existing conditions at the same time and in the same place.

Key information on HIV, hepatitis B, syphilis and tuberculosis infection

### 130 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Begin by asking participants to share what they know about the three diseases, if they have any experience

with a patient, a friend or family member affected.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ask about the causes, modes of transmission, signs and symptoms and prevalence/burden.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Note their answers on a flip chart. Refer participants to their manual for more information.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Highlight the key features of the four infections and correct any knowledge gaps during the presentation.

Explain their similarities and the rationale for elimination.

## 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

HIV infection and its burden in pregnancy

Slides 6–7: HIV infection and burden in pregnancy Present slides 6 and 7 and explain the following: • In 2016, there were 25 500 000 HIV-infected people in sub-Saharan Africa, with 116 000 new HIV infections in children. • Briefly discuss some of the misconceptions and challenges associated with the management of the disease.

Note for example that: • HIV cannot be transmitted through hugging or mosquito bites. • Most persons with infection are asymptomatic. • Women in their reproductive age are at higher risk. • The stigma associated with HIV infection is a barrier to diagnosis and care. • PMTCT remains a key control strategy. • Persons with HIV infection require lifelong treatment and support.

Tuberculosis and its burden in pregnancy

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slides 8–11: Tuberculosis in pregnancy • Present slides 8–11 to provide background information on TB in pregnancy. You may also enrich the presentation with the additional information provided below. • TB is believed to be nearly as old as human history. The human host serves as a natural reservoir for M. tuberculosis. • TB infection is caused by inhalation of viable bacilli, which may persist in an inactive state (known as latent TB infection [LTBI]) or progress to active TB

disease. Individuals with LTBI are asymptomatic and not contagious. Latent TB bacilli remain viable and may reactivate, causing active symptomatic TB disease, which can be transmitted via airborne spread. • Tuberculosis (TB) was declared a public health emergency by WHO in 2005 in the Africa Region. • Worldwide, the burden of tuberculosis (TB) disease in pregnant women is substantial. • The exact global extent of the problem of maternal mortality caused by TB

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

is unknown – particularly in the context of HIV and stigma. But studies from sub-Saharan Africa and India report TB as the cause of 6–15% of all-cause mortality and 15–34% of indirect maternal mortality. (Khan M et al., AIDS 2001; 15:1857-63)

Slides 10–11: Tuberculosis in pregnancy (continued) • Congenital TB is very rare. It occurs by trans-placental transmission through umbilical veins to the foetal liver and lungs or by aspiration and swallowing of infected amniotic fluid in utero or intrapartum, causing primary infection of foetal lungs and gut. • Transmission of tuberculosis through breast milk does not occur. Horizontal transmission in the postpartum period by droplets from an infected mother

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

or undiagnosed family member is the more common route of transmission to the infant. • TB during pregnancy is associated with a two-fold increase of mother-to- child transmission of HIV. This, because TB infection of the placenta results in increased placental HIV viral load. • Antenatal care presents a unique opportunity for evaluation and management of latent and active TB among individuals with risk of TB who may not otherwise present for medical care.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 133

Syphilis and its burden in pregnancy

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 12: Syphilis and its burden in pregnancy • Present slide 12 on general information on syphilis: • It is a bacterial STI caused by Treponema pallidum, which causes substantial morbidity and mortality. • In 2012, an estimated 5.6 million new cases of syphilis occurred among 15– 49-year-olds worldwide. There are an estimated 18 million prevalent cases of syphilis. • Ask participants if they have seen cases of syphilis. Let them describe the clinical presentations if they have.

• Refer participants to their manual for additional information if necessary.

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slide 13: Syphilis and its burden in pregnancy (2) • Untreated primary and secondary syphilis infections in pregnancy result in severe adverse pregnancy outcomes. • Latent (asymptomatic) syphilis infections in pregnancy also cause serious adverse pregnancy outcomes in more than half of cases. • Mother-to-child transmission of syphilis (congenital syphilis) is usually devastating to the foetus. • The foetus can be easily cured with treatment, and the risk of adverse

outcomes to the foetus is minimal if the mother receives adequate treatment during early pregnancy – ideally before the second trimester.

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

Slide 14 – 15: Hepatitis B infection and its burden in pregnancy • Present the information on the slide and supplement it with the additional information provided below if needed. • Viruses are the most common causes of hepatitis in the world, but other infections, toxic substances (such as alcohol and certain drugs), and autoimmune diseases also cause hepatitis. • There are five known types of the family of viruses (Hepadnaviridae) with

affinity for the liver which affect humans (A, B, C, D, E). • Two types (B and C) can cause severe liver damage and lead to chronic disease and liver cancer. Hepatitis B virus (HBV) is the commonest type of viral hepatitis in Africa. • Briefly describe the clinical presentations of the infection and occurrence of fulminant infections during pregnancy. • Symptomatic acute disease occurs in about 1% of perinatally infected

### 132 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

individuals, in 10% of children infected between 1 and 5 years of age, and in about 30% of individuals infected after the age of 5 years. • Highlight the risks of MTCT and the long-term health outcomes in the baby. • A vaccine is available to protect the newborn and medications exist to treat women with chronic infection and high viral loads.

Conclude the presentations on the various infections by summarizing their similarities and the impact of coinfections with these germs on the pregnant woman and her baby. • HIV, hepatitis B and syphilis can be sexually transmitted. • Very often, coinfections with these diseases occurs in the same individual. • The three infections and TB can be screened for or identified using rapid diagnostic test kits at point of care. • Treatments are also available to prevent mother-to-child transmission.

## 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slides 16 and 17: Provider-initiated counselling and testing Present the information on the slides 16 and 17 to describe the PITC approach and its benefits. • The woman knows her status and can take appropriate measures. • She can protect her partner and family from infection in case of serodiscordance. • She gets support from her partner and her family. • She can encourage her partner to be tested. • She can get treatment as soon as possible in case of a positive result and prevent MTCT.

Highlight its role in the continuum of maternal and child care and in reinforcing couple involvement.

Counselling and testing/screening for HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The introduction of provider-initiated testing and counselling (PITC) of HIV as a routine component of

ANC has led to a massive decline in MTCT of HIV.

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Many countries have therefore prioritized PITC in ANC as a key component of their effort to eliminate

mother-to-child transmission of HIV (eMTCT).

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; HIV testing is also being effectively combined with screening for syphilis, HBV and TB, and this has had

considerable public health benefits.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The PITC approach is therefore strongly recommended for settings with a high prevalence of the four

diseases.

Slide 18: Guiding principles of PITC • Present the 5Cs (guiding principles) for PITC. • Refer participants to the relevant section in their manual. • Task a participant each to read out loud and explain the text describing each principle. Clarify any points if necessary. • The five key guiding principles (5Cs) for PITC are described in the text box below.

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

1. Consent: the woman/couple is informed of the process for testing and counselling and of their right to decline testing. In most settings, verbal consent for HIV testing is considered sufficient. 2. Confidentiality: what the provider and the client discuss will not be disclosed to anyone else without the expressed consent of the person being tested. Confidentiality applies not only to the test results but also

to any personal information, such as information concerning sexual behaviour and the use of illegal drugs. 3. Counselling: this includes pre- and post-test counselling. Pre-test information can be provided in a group counselling setting, but clients/couples can also ask questions in private individual or couple counselling. However, testing must always be followed by appropriate high-quality post-test counselling for all clients, based on the specific test results.

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

4. Correct test results: quality assurance mechanisms must exist to ensure that people receive the correct diagnosis. For HIV, all people who receive a positive diagnosis are retested to verify their diagnosis before initiation of HIV care or treatment. 5. Connection to care and treatment: linkages to prevention, treatment and care services should exist, and this must include appropriate follow-up, including long-term prevention and treatment support. Without

linkage to treatment and care, testing and learning about one’s positive status for the infections will have limited value. Pregnant women who test negative for infections also require linkages to prevention care services, if they continue to be at high risk for infection.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 135

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 19: Providing PITC for HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB • Refer participants to the learning guide Table 11.1 in their manual. • Explain the purpose of the guide and task seven or eight participants to each read out a section of the guide and explain their understanding of the steps described in the text. • Clarify any knowledge gaps or misunderstandings.

End discussions on the counselling approach by asking participants to share their experiences on counselling women for HIV, syphilis and hepatitis B testing.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Do women more often consent or decline?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Did they use the steps described?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; How have they overcome difficulties?

Recommended HIV, HBV, syphilis and tuberculosis testing and screening approaches for pregnant women and their newborns

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; As part of preparations for this session, familiarize yourself with the national protocols for HIV, HBV,

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

syphilis and TB testing.

Slide 20: Recommended testing/screening approaches • Ask participants about the kits they use in their facilities to test for HIV, HBV and syphilis. • Do they screen all clients for TB and how is this done? Note their answers. • Present slide 20 to highlight the general WHO recommendations for selecting test kits. • Rapid diagnostic tests (RDTs) are so called as they produce a test result quickly, usually in less than 30 minutes. • List the benefits of RDTs at points of care (POC).

• Emphasize that participants must always adhere to national testing protocols as these have often been carefully selected to meet national settings.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slides 21 and 22 below to describe the recommended tests and protocols for pregnant women

and their newborns. You may also use the additional information provided below to enrich the presenta- tions. Also refer to Figure 11.2 in the participant manual.

### 134 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Inform participants that they will have an opportunity to see and practice using these tests in Session 14

if they have no prior experience.

## 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 21: Recommended testing/screening approaches HIV: • HIV RDTs detect HIV 1 and 2 antibodies and antigen (HIV-1 p24) in the blood. Most persons (about 90%) will develop enough HIV antibodies to be detected three to four weeks after infection. RDTs are just as accurate as a standard HIV test. • If the test is negative at ANC booking, it is advised that it is repeated at 36 weeks. If the initial test is positive, then further supplementary tests

are done to confirm the diagnosis. A combination of RDTs, simple and enzyme immunoassays are recommended as second-and third-line assays to confirm an initial reactive test result before lifelong treatment is started. • Molecular testing techniques that detect the presence of HIV viral nucleic acid, such as RNA or DNA, are recommended for early infant diagnosis (EID) in infants under 18 months of age. This is because passively

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

transferred HIV antibodies from the mother to the baby can give false positive results if antibody detecting RDTs are used.

HBV: • A positive HBsAg (hepatitis B surface antigen) test result means that the patient is infected and can spread the hepatitis B virus to others through their blood. • Anti-HBs or HBsAb (hepatitis B surface antibody) – a “positive” or “reactive” anti-HBs (or HBsAb) test result indicates that a person is protected against the hepatitis B virus.

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 22: Recommended testing/screening approaches Syphilis: • Traditionally, a variety of screening/diagnostic tests were available for detecting syphilis. They included VDRL, TPHA, RPR and TPPA. Each had limitations, and interpreting their results can be challenging for less experienced care providers. • Recently, POC rapid syphilis tests (RSTs) for treponemal antibodies have been developed and approved for use. • RSTs provide results in 10–15 minutes and can be performed on-site in

any setting, since they do not require refrigerated storage or laboratory equipment. • RSTs use T. pallidum antigens to detect treponema-specific antibodies. However, a positive test result does not distinguish between active and previously treated infections.

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Tuberculosis • Traditionally, TB diagnosis was confirmed by sputum microscopy and culture. Results could take weeks to obtain. Negative culture results did not conclusively mean that the patient did not have TB, especially for HIV coinfected persons. • Recent advances are changing the picture, however. Simpler and quicker tests for TB are being developed. • A test recently approved (2011) for use is the Xpert MTB/RIF assay. It is

a nucleic acid amplification test that uses disposable cartridges with a tabletop GeneXpert instrument. • Sputum samples from patients with suspected TB are mixed with the reagent provided for the assay, and a cartridge containing this mixture is placed into the GeneXpert machine. All processing from this point on is fully automated and test results are available in 2 hours.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 137

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Class activity: Role play – counselling on HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB Inform participants that the purpose of this role play activity is to enable participants to learn and practice their skills in counselling a pregnant woman about HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB.

Slide 23: Class activity – role plays • Using slide 23, present the instructions for the role play activity, making sure participants understand their roles and responsibilities. • Read out the three role play scenarios (slides 24–26). • Divide the class into three to six groups (of five to six individuals per group). Assign each group a role play scenario and a supervisor/co-facilitator. • Ask them to carefully read the background information for the respective

### 136 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

roles: client, counsellor, observer – as described in their manual, before they proceed to act/or observe. • Each group must ensure that members practice the role of a counsellor by rotating the roles). • Observers will give feedback at the end of each role play practice.

Role plays:

Instructions for co-facilitators • As participants perform the role plays, group facilitators should listen and observe in order to assess the participants’ counselling skills as well as messages given to the client. • They should permit feedback and comments from peer observers before giving their own feedback. • They should also monitor time to ensure that all participants have practiced. • For large groups (comprising more than six participants), they could subdivide the groups into three and

allow simultaneous role play practice by the subgroups. • Care must be taken to ensure that group activities do not interfere with each other.

## 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

End of activity feedback session: (15 minutes)

Bring the class back together after the role play activity is over. Start the feedback session by asking how many role plays were scored 5 by their peers.

Ask participants to share their feelings, experiences and lessons learned when they acted as client and counsellor. You may raise the following questions to get the discussions going. • Did the counsellors provide the necessary information about the disease? • Did the counsellors put the clients at ease to discuss the topic? • Was confidentiality ensured? • Was the information provided clear and understandable?

• Did the counsellors help the clients plan how to go about resolving any problems or meeting their needs regarding the infection? • Do participants feel comfortable about counselling clients about the four infections discussed?

Summarize the discussions and address any concerns to end the role play activity.

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 27: Care, treatment and support for pregnant woman with HIV, HBV, syphilis and TB • Linkage to treatment and support is a key ingredient of the PITC strategy. • Ask participants to list some of the challenges that persons with HIV, TB and STIs face in their communities, and to describe how these affect a pregnant woman’s ability to access care. • Write down their answers on a flip chart. • Present slide 27 and task a participant to read out the information on the slide.

• Ask participants if they know of any community-based support groups for HIV, TB and the other diseases.

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 28: Care and treatment for HIV, HBV, syphilis or TB • Emphasize that the number and scheduling of antenatal visits may need to be modified to enable the care provider to meet the health needs of the client who has tested positive for these infectious diseases. • Care must also be client-centred. Women with HIV and HBV, for example, require long-term treatment and follow-up. • ANC care providers must therefore involve other relevant experts and

coordinate care with these team members right from when the diagnosis is made.

Note that many countries have protocols in place to manage these infections. It is best for participants to learn and use existing national protocols for case management. Where necessary, adapt slides 29–30 accordingly to reflect existing national protocols.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 139

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 29: Care and treatment for mothers and newborns – HIV Present the slides or ask a participant to read the information. Highlight the following for HIV infection in pregnancy: • Antiretroviral therapy (ART) or highly active antiretroviral therapy (HAART) using a combination of medicines is the most effective treatment. • Care providers must monitor for adherence to these medicines and check the patient for side-effects such as anaemia or poor response to treatment.

Immunosuppression with AIDS occurs if the woman’s CD4 cell count falls

- Dòng dữ liệu: *TDF: tenofovir, 3TC: lamivudine,; below 200, leaving her more susceptible to infections.

- Dòng dữ liệu: FTC: emtricitabine, EFV: efavirenz •; HBV: universal HBV birth dose recommended for all newborns

irrespective of maternal HBV status.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remind participants that each country decides on its treatment protocols based on several factors,

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

including cost considerations and prevalence of HIV.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The information below on ARVs is provided for the facilitator. Do not present it to the participants (it is not in

the participant manual). However, it may be a useful reference if you need to answer questions about ARV treatment options.

Some facts on antiretroviral drugs • Recommended antiretroviral treatment regimens are based on worldwide evidence from ART programmes. • Factors considered include the following: effectiveness of the drug, pill burden, dosing in relation to food toxicity, dosing frequency, nutritional requirements, convenience and drug interaction profiles, resistance to ARV, availability and cost. • Triple drug therapy (treatment combining three drugs) is presently the recommended treatment regimen for

all persons needing ARV therapy. Mono therapy or dual therapy (treatment with one or two drugs only) is contraindicated.

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Recommended triple therapy regimens consist of: • two nucleoside/nucleotide reverse transcriptase inhibitors (NRTIs) and one non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor (NNRTI) or two NRTIs and one boosted protease inhibitor (PI)

Classification of ARVs

- Dòng dữ liệu: Nucleoside revers; Nucleotide reverse; Non-nucleoside; Protease inhibitors (PI)

- Dòng dữ liệu: transcriptase; transcriptase; reverse transcriptase

- Dòng dữ liệu: inhibitors (NRTI); inhibitor (NRTI); inhibitors (NNRTI)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Zidovudine; Tenofovir (TDF); Nevirapine (NVP); Ritonavir-boosted lopinavir

- Dòng dữ liệu: (AZT/ZDV); (LPV/r)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Lamivudine (3TC); Efavirenz (EFV); Ritonavir-boosted atazanavir

(ATV/r) Abacavir (ABC) Emtricitabine (FTC)

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A first-line regimen is the first option for treatment of all patients who fit the treatment criteria.

### 138 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A second-line regimen is used when there is evidence of treatment failure with the first-line regimen, and this

must be confirmed preferably by CD4 and viral load monitoring where available.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; A third-line or salvage therapy is recommended for heavily treatment-experienced patients and those who

have failed second-line treatment.

## 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 30: Care and treatment for mothers and newborns – syphilis • Present slide 30 and provide the following additional information on syphilis. • When benzathine or procaine penicillin cannot be used (for example due to penicillin allergy) or are not available (due to stock-outs), the WHO STI guidelines suggest using, with caution, erythromycin 500 mg orally four times daily for 14 days; or ceftriaxone 1 g intramuscularly once daily for 10–14 days; or azithromycin 2 g once orally.

• Although erythromycin and azithromycin treat the pregnant woman, they do not cross the placental barrier completely and, as a result, the foetus is not treated. It is therefore necessary to treat the newborn infant soon after delivery.

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 31: Care and treatment for mothers and newborns – TB • Present slide 31 and also highlight the following: • TB disease can be treated by taking several drugs for 6–12 months. • It is very important that people who have TB disease finish the medicines and take the drugs exactly as prescribed. • Mention the difficulty of multidrug-resistant TB, especially among HIV coinfected patients. • The mother must be monitored for signs of clinical deterioration at each visit.

• Her baby must preferably be referred for paediatrician assessment after birth and before BCG is administered. • BCG vaccine should also not be used in children who are known to be HIV coinfected.

End the presentations on treatment and care by asking participants to share any best practices they might have that increase access to care and treatment for pregnant women diagnosed with TB, HIV, HBV and syphilis.

Key messages

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slides 32 and 33: Key messages To end Session 11, ask a few participants to state one key message or highlight of the session for them.

Next, present slides 32 and 33 to summarize and conclude the session.

Key messages

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; HIV, hepatitis B, syphilis and TB testing/screening and counselling constitute an important task for all health

workers caring for pregnant women. It is an entry point to provide prevention and appropriate care and support to the pregnant woman and her baby.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Test-positive women should be provided appropriate medicine(s) as soon as possible during pregnancy, and be

linked to the appropriate care centres for long-term follow-up.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; HIV-positive women should receive a single daily dose of TDF + 3TC (FTC) + EFV for life, and should be advised

that early infant diagnosis for HIV is offered after birth.

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Pregnant women should be tested for syphilis during the first trimester and those positive should be given a

single dose of 2 400 000 IU of benzathine benzylpenicillin as soon as possible.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Pregnant woman with hepatitis B should be given TDF or entecavir where indicated.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Universal hepatitis B pentavalent vaccines are recommended for all newborns at birth.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Women with TB, especially those with HIV coinfection, should be referred for directly observed therapy using

short-course (DOTS) combination therapy of four drugs for at least six months.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 141

Session 12: Ultrasonography in antenatal care As part of preparations for this session, ensure that the supplies and materials required for the session are available and ready for use.

• Laptop

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Some acronyms used in sonography

- Dòng dữ liệu: AC; abdominal circumference

- Dòng dữ liệu: AFI; amniotic fluid index

BPD biparietal diameter

- Dòng dữ liệu: CRL; crown-rump length

- Dòng dữ liệu: FL; femur length

- Dòng dữ liệu: HC; head circumference

MSD mean sac diameter SDP single deepest pocket measurement, largest vertical pocket of amniotic fluid

Slide 1 Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Session outline

Present slides 1 and 2 to welcome and introduce participants to the session. Slide 2

Session aim and objectives

### 140 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slide 3: Session aim and objectives Present or task a participant to read the aim and objectives of the session (5 minutes). Aim: To enable participants to understand the benefits and common indications of ultrasound for effective patient care during pregnancy. Objectives: On completion of this session, participants will be able to: 1. explain what ultrasound and ultrasonography are; 2. understand the benefits and indications of ultrasonography in antenatal care;

3. recognize the limitations of ultrasound findings in clinical decision-making in caring for the pregnant woman; 4. identify the correct timings for ultrasonography scans for different pregnancy conditions or indications; and 5. describe how to prepare a client for the obstetric ultrasound procedure and its outcomes.

## 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Introduction

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; To begin, ask participants how many of them have witnessed ultrasonography or requested this

investigation for management of the pregnant woman. Do they provide ultrasonography in their settings? Encouage them to list some of the challenges they have encountered in its use.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer to this list during the training session to address participants’ concerns and challenges.

Slide 4: What is ultrasonography? • Present slide 4. Explain that all ultrasound machines use a tool called a transducer that uses sound waves and echoes to create pictures of internal organs and the baby on a computer. • The most common kinds of ultrasound during pregnancy are: o transabdominal ultrasound: the transducer is placed and moved over the abdomen to create the pictures; o transvaginal ultrasound: the ultrasound is done through the

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

vagina. A thin transducer shaped like a wand is passed into the vagina; and o doppler ultrasound: here the transducer converts the ultrasound waves into audible waves. This approach is used to listen to the baby’s heartbeat and to measure the blood flow in the umbilical cord and in some of the baby’s blood vessels.

Slide 5: Obstetric ultrasound • Present slide 5. • Although the general perception is that ultrasound imaging has no adverse effects on the mother or the foetus, evidence collated from laboratory studies has shown effects of potential clinical significance. • Explain that the ALARA principle is therefore advocated to minimize exposure to the barest minimum necessary.

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Additional information: other types of available ultrasound scan (a) Three-dimensional (3D) ultrasound: a 3D ultrasound takes thousands of pictures at once. It makes a 3D image that is almost as clear as a photograph. Some providers use this kind of ultrasound to check if the baby’s organs are growing and developing normally. It can also check for abnormalities in a baby’s face. A 3D ultrasound can also be used to check for problems in the uterus.

(b) Four-dimensional (4D) ultrasound: this is like a 3D ultrasound, but it also shows the baby’s movements in a video format.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 143

Benefits and uses of ultrasound in pregnancy

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Slide 6: Advantages of ultrasound use in pregnancy Slide 7: Use of ultrasound in pregnancy • Ask participants, “what are the benefits and uses of ultrasound in pregnancy care?” • Then ask participants to list the reasons for which ultrasound scans have been requested in their workplaces. Present slides 6 and 7. • Highlight the fact that ultrasonography has been used safely during pregnancy for over 40 years. • It has become an invaluable third eye and is an essential and useful tool for

screening, diagnosis and surveillance of maternal and foetal health. • WHO recommends the use of ultrasound to monitor foetal development and well-being.

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slides 8 and 9: Routine vs indicated ultrasound • During pregnancy, ultrasound scans may be done for all women routinely or for a specific reason (indicated). Ask participants about what the practice is in their settings. • Present slide 8. • Routine scans are performed at specified gestation periods during the pregnancy. • WHO recommends one ultrasound scan before 24 weeks of gestation (early ultrasound) to estimate gestational age, improve detection of

foetal anomalies and multiple pregnancies, reduce induction of labour for post-term pregnancy, and improve a woman’s pregnancy experience. Ask participants to discuss how they can ensure that all clients have this routine scan done easily.

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Slide 9 • Present slide 9 and ask participants to list other reasons for which they would request an ultrasound scan during antenatal care. • Refer them to Table 12.1 on “Common indications of ultrasound scanning during pregnancy” in the participant manual. • Ask a participant to read out the information in the table. • Encourage participants to identify the indications for ultrasound in each of the three trimesters.

Limitations of ultrasonography during pregnancy

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Despite being a tool of considerable significance in pregnancy surveillance, there are limitations on the use of

ultrasound that must be appreciated by both the care provider and the antenatal client.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Risks associated with the use of ultrasound in pregnancy (as with all other medical diagnostic tools) can be

categorized into diagnostic errors and possible biological side-effects.

### 142 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Fully understanding the limitations and benefits of ultrasonography is important to minimize errors in clinical

decisions for patient care and reduce abuse of the procedure.

## 144 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 144 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 10: Limitations of obstetric ultrasound • Present slide 10. • Using the information on the slide, discuss the key considerations when requesting an ultrasound during pregnancy. • Use examples to highlight the limiting factors below. o Mild pre-eclampsia – no abnormalities will be noted in the baby o The older the pregnancy, the less accurate the calculated EDD using size measurements o The more obese the mother is, the poorer the image quality,

which in turn negatively affects the detection rate of foetal anomalies.

Slide 11: Key considerations when reviewing ultrasound (US) reports • Present the slide and explain that it is important for ANC care providers to appreciate the limitations of ultrasounds when reviewing the reports they receive. • Moreover, they must always make care decisions for the client with due consideration of the clinical presentations and observations also made.

### 144 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 145

Slide 12–16: Common obstetric measurements and their limitations

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present slides 12–16 to describe the common foetal measurements and

related calculations with regard to gestational age, expected date of delivery, and foetal weight and well-being.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Highlight the margins of error listed for the various measurements and

discuss how these might affect clinical decisions such as planned delivery by caesarean section or inductions.

Slide 12–16 (continued): Common obstetric measurements and their limitations • Ask participants to share any experiences that they may have had where not recognizing the limitations of scan measurements had led to poor decision-making?

Preparing clients for obstetric ultrasound

### 144 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 17: Preparing a client for obstetric ultrasound • Ask participants to describe how they prepare their clients for ultrasound. • Note their responses and present slide 17. • Emphasize the importance of the preparation steps listed in optimizing the outcomes of the procedure. • Ultrasound scans must only be requested for medical reasons. • Clients must understand the benefits and risks of the scan – including the possibility of misdiagnosis.

• In settings where scans are only available in private facilities, it is important to direct clients to certified sonographers to minimize the challenge of misdiagnosis and abuse.

## 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Educating/counselling clients on ultrasound findings

Slides 18 and 19: Educating and counselling on ultrasound • Ask participants to describe any experiences they have had breaking news of scan reports to a client/ couple. • Explain that, generally, pregnant women and their partners put a very high value on obstetric ultrasound examinations. They often believe that an ultrasound examination will provide clear-cut answers on the exact expected date of delivery and accurately tell whether something is wrong

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

with their babies or not. They often do not always understand the limitations and potential disadvantages of pregnancy ultrasounds. Therefore, pre-scan counselling is necessary to dispel such myths. • Ultrasound findings, if not properly communicated, can contribute to unnecessary anxiety for the expectant mother and her family. This is particularly important where findings indicate bad news such as foetal abnormality, demise or presence of pregnancy complications. The worry and

anxiety caused by poorly communicated ultrasound examination findings can have long-lasting effects. • Present the information on slides 18 and 19. Highlight key steps for breaking bad news to clients. Note that these steps follow basic counselling principles.

Class activity: Debate – “is routine/multiple ultrasound scan justified in all pregnancies?”

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 20 – 21: Class activity – debate • Present the topic for the debate activity and explain its purpose. • Review all instructions on the slide with participants so that everyone understands how the debate will work and how to prepare. • Divide the class into the three groups. • Choose three people from the audience group to be the judges. The facilitator will act as moderator. • Provide the debating teams with their reference materials (see text below).

• Encourage them to conduct additional searches to enrich their arguments. Allow each group 10 minutes to prepare for the debate whilst you set up the room. Also educate the three judges on their scoring system whilst the groups prepare. • Total time for the debate is 30 minutes. • After the debate is finished and the winning side determined, summarize the points made in this debate and record them on a flip chart.

Examples of arguments for and against ultrasound are provided below

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4
