- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 147

Information for debating teams

Arguments for ultrasound in pregnancy

A: It is safe • Ultrasound is, arguably, the most commonly used diagnostic procedure in obstetrics. • Its advantages are: it is non-invasive, convenient, painless, and yields immediate and extensive results. • While the ultrasound scan is not 100% accurate, it is widely considered to be safe for both mother and unborn baby. • According to the American College of Obstetricians and Gynaecologists, there have been no documented

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

reports of negative effects on the foetus from diagnostic ultrasound procedures. • A review of over 50 medical studies shows that ultrasounds do not pose any danger to mothers or foetuses. They do not cause birth defects, childhood developmental or intellectual problems, or cancer. “It is unlikely that the routine ultrasounds done during pregnancy – which last 15 minutes or less – could cause harm.”

• Foetuses can hear ultrasound examinations. Ultrasound examinations during pregnancy expose the foetus to a sound as loud as that made by a subway train coming into a station, say US researchers. But doctors do not think the experience causes a baby any lasting harm. ... The sound was similar to the highest notes on a piano.

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

B: It improves diagnosis and care provision • Ultrasound is used during pregnancy to check the baby’s development, the presence of a multiple pregnancy, and to help pick up any abnormalities. o It can screen for birth defects, like spina bifida or heart defects. o It can help with other prenatal tests, like chorionic villus sampling (also called CVS) or amniocentesis (also called amnio). CVS involves taking cells from the placenta for testing.

The placenta is a tissue that provides nutrients for the baby. Amnio is a test where amniotic fluid and cells are taken from the sac around the baby. o It can check for pregnancy complications, such as ectopic pregnancy, molar pregnancy, and miscarriage. • Ultrasound is therefore an essential and useful tool for screening, diagnosis and surveillance of maternal and foetal health.

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

C: It enhances the positive pregnancy experience for the couple and increases bonding • Ultrasound provides parents with the first glimpses of their developing baby and enhances bonding. Ultrasound screening enables expectant parents to experience the pregnancy as more real and gives partners, especially, the opportunity to experience and see the pregnancy. The expectant father meets and connects with the foetus.

Arguments against

A: Safety concerns • There is to date insufficient evidence about the absolute safety of pregnancy ultrasounds. • Although the general perception is that ultrasound imaging has no adverse effects on the mother or the foetus, evidence collated from laboratory studies has shown effects of potential clinical significance. Potential bioeffects of ultrasound can be either thermal or mechanical.

### 146 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 8

• More studies around safety are warranted [46, 48], especially since experimental studies on foetal mice have shown adverse effects. • Although previous systematic reviews regarding safety have been unable to find evidence of significant adverse maternal, perinatal or childhood outcomes [6, 46], an association between exposure to ultrasound in pregnancy and non-right handedness has been found.

• Pregnant physiotherapists who provided ultrasound treatments for more than 20 hours per week were found to have an increased risk for spontaneous abortions. • There are some concerns that children who have been exposed to ultrasound while in their mothers’ uterus have a greater chance of suffering from dyslexia and other speech and learning problems. • The uncertainties regarding safety are evident in the recommendations of prudent use and adherence to the

ALARA (as low as reasonably achievable) principle.

## 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

B: It is costly and frequently inaccessible for persons in low-resource settings

C: Misdiagnosis and interference with the positive pregnancy experience

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ultrasound is an invaluable third eye, but it cannot see everything.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Sometimes ultrasounds create unnecessary stress. There are situations where the use of ultrasound had caused

more harm than good, as deviations picked up that were not clear, creating a lot of anxiety and stress for the pregnant woman and her family. Many examples exist of false positive findings that caused distress or even ruined the experience of pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Uncertain answers arising from difficult to interpret ultrasound findings were described as hard to deal with for

expectant parents.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Ultrasounds in the second and third trimesters are not perfect. A 2014 study from France found that, out of 526

### 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

pregnancy ultrasounds performed during the said periods, 8.8% of cases showed false positive results for foetal anomaly and 9.2% showed misclassification of potential anomalies.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The worry and anxiety caused by the ultrasound examination can have long-lasting effects; the belief and anxiety

about something being wrong with the child sometimes lasts throughout the child’s upbringing. D: Effect on obstetric skills and practice

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Reliance on ultrasound is now sometimes outweighing clinical experience. There are concerns that some clinical

skills are lost because of the increased use of obstetric ultrasound.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The liberal use/abuse of obstetric ultrasound is occurring for reasons including avoidance of blame by colleagues

or expectant parents if major problems were not detected or anything subsequently went wrong.

Arguments against (continued)

### 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Care providers sometimes find it particularly difficult to communicate complex information from ultrasound

findings to people with lower levels of education, or where there are language barriers and counselling occurrs via interpreters. This create stress for care providers and clients. For example, You know your baby has this, it has a 20% chance of being very severely disabled, a 30% chance of mild disability, and a 50% chance of being normal.

E: Non-medical use/abuse of scans and ethical challenges • There is a growing phenomenon of non-medical or so-called ‘entertainment’ ultrasound which is raising concern among pregnancy care providers. • Non-medical use of obstetric ultrasound can be defined as situations where the purpose of the examination is solely to view the foetus, take its picture, or determine its sex without medical indication.

### 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

• An ethical analysis of non-medical foetal ultrasound concludes that obstetric ultrasound practice is ethically justifiable only if the indication for its use is based on medical evidence. Furthermore, the practice of non-medical or ‘entertainment’ ultrasound is discouraged by governments and professional bodies. • In some settings, non-desired babies (sex, down syndrome) are aborted in preference for desired babies because of routine ultrasound.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 149

Feedback after debate

After the debate is over and the winning side determined, summarize the points made in this debate by presenting slides 22–27.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Slide 22; Slide 23; Slide 24

- Dòng dữ liệu: Slide 25; Slide 26; Slide 27

Conclusion and key messages

Slide 28: Conclusion to the debate

### 148 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Conclude the debate by presenting slide 28.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Clarify any concerns.

Side 29: Key messages Conclude the session by presenting slide 29 on key messages. • Ultrasonography is an important tool during ANC for early detection of complications and monitoring the progress of pregnancy. • WHO recommends one ultrasound scan for all pregnant women before 24 weeks of gestation to: o establish gestational age; o improve detection of foetal anomalies and multiple pregnancies; o reduce induction of labour for post-term pregnancy; and o improve pregnancy outcomes.

• Requirements when requesting ultrasonography include clearly stating the reason for the scan and providing vital information such as LMP, if known. • Care must be taken when giving scan reports in order to reduce stress and protect the positive pregnancy experience. • Ultrasonography reports must be interpreted alongside other clinical findings.

## 150 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 150 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Classroom quiz 2: Answer sheet – sessions 7–12

1. What are the steps in the counselling process?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Assess the situation

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Define the problem, needs or information gaps

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Generate alternative solutions

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Prioritize the solutions

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Develop a plan

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Review and evaluate.

2. List five aspects of self-care that the pregnant woman should be counselled on.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Good nutrition

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Taking iron and folic acid tablets

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Rest and avoidance of heavy lifting

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Malaria prevention: sleeping under an insecticide-treated bed net

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Safer sex: correct and consistent use of condoms, if at risk for STIs or HIV

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoidance of alcohol and smoking during pregnancy

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Avoidance of medication that is not prescribed at a health facility

### 150 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Danger signs in pregnancy.

3. List five danger signs for which the pregnant woman should be advised to go to hospital immediately.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Vaginal bleeding

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Convulsions

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Severe headache with blurred vision

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Fever and too weak to get out of bed

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Severe abdominal pain

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Fast or difficult breathing.

4. What preparations for birth and emergency should be discussed with the pregnant woman during antenatal care?

For birth • Where she will give birth • How she will get there • To start saving money for transport and the baby’s birth • Supplies she should gather and take for the birth • Who will go with her for support during labour and birth • Who will care for her home and children while she is away

### 150 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 151

In case of emergency • Danger signs during pregnancy • Where the woman will go if an emergency occurs • How she will get there • How much it will cost for transport and services • If she can start saving money straight away • Who will go with her for support in an emergency • Who will take care of her home and children in an emergency • Who can donate blood for her – if needed.

5. List the three WHO-recommended strategies for the prevention and control of malaria in pregnancy.

Use of ITNs, IPTp-SP, and case management of malaria illness.

6. Malaria is less severe in women during their first or second pregnancies than it is in subsequent pregnancies. True or false?

False

7. IPTp-SP should not be given in the first 16 weeks of pregnancy. True or false?

True

### 150 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

8. Parenteral (IV or IM) quinine is a drug of choice for a pregnant woman with severe malaria. True or false?

True

9. Anaemia in pregnancy is associated with an increased risk of obstetric complications such as low birth weight, premature birth, and PPH. True or false?

True

10. At what haemoglobin level is a pregnant woman considered to have anaemia?

Below 11 g/dL

11. What dosages of iron and folate are given for the prevention of anaemia in a pregnant woman?

1 tablet = iron 60 mg + folic acid 400 mcg daily (note: 400 mcg equals 0.4 mg).

## 152 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 152 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

12. What dosage of iron and folate is given to treat anaemia in a pregnant woman and for how long should it be given?

Two tablets (120 mg iron and 400 mcg folic acid) every day until haemoglobin is normal and then continued for three months.

13. What are the advantages of HIV, HBV and syphilis testing for a pregnant woman?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Woman will know her status and take steps to protect herself and her family

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Woman will receive treatment if tested positive

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Woman can be given ARVs to prevent mother-to-child transmission of the disease

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Baby can be protected after birth by immunization (for example against HBV)

14. A pregnant woman with TB must be treated using multiple anti-TB medicines given as directly observed treatments (DOTs). True or false?

### 152 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

True – she is treated with a combination of four drugs (RIPE): isoniazid, ethambutol, rifampicin and pyrazinamide for at least six months, given as DOTs.

15. Name one drug that is recommended to treat a syphilis-positive pregnant woman?

Pregnant women should be tested for syphilis during the first trimester and those positive should be given, as soon as possible, a single dose of 2 400 000 IU of benzathine benzylpenicillin.

16. Name three antiretroviral medicines given to prevent mother-to-child transmission of HIV.

HIV-positive women should receive a single daily dose of TDF + 3TC (FTC) + EFV for life, and should be advised that the baby will also be given antiretrovirals (NVP or AZT) after birth and whilst breastfeeding. They will also be offered early infant diagnosis for HIV.

17. A baby born to an HBV-positive mother must be given hepatis B immunoglobulin and hepatitis B vaccine at birth. True or false?

### 152 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

True – all babies must now receive HB vaccine to protect them from infection irrespective of the mother’s status. Those with infected mothers additionally receive immunoglobulins to mop up any maternally transmitted infections.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 153

18. List five uses of ultrasonography in pregnancy.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Estimation of gestational age

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Identification of whether the foetus is alive or not

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determination of the lie and position of the foetus

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Detection of multiple foetuses

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Identifying the location of the placenta

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Determination of adequacy of liquor volume

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Identification of uterine tumours that may obstruct childbirth.

### 152 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

19. At which gestation should routine ultrasound be performed for all pregnant women according to WHO recommendations?

One ultrasound scan before 24 weeks of gestation (early ultrasound, best around 22–24 weeks) is recommended for pregnant women, to estimate gestational age, improve detection of foetal anomalies and multiple pregnancies, reduce induction of labour for post-term pregnancy, and improve the woman’s pregnancy experience.

20. Ultrasound can be used to date the pregnancy most accurately and predict the expected date of childbirth when performed in the third trimester. True or false?

False

## 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Session 13: Skills practice – selected clinical skills

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The skills to be taught in this session and skills stations to be supervised by each facilitator should have been

carefully planned.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Prior to this session, the lead facilitator should brief all assisting facilitators about the skills stations and their

assignments.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; All facilitators should check that the necessary test kits, clinical specimens and other supplies are ready.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; They should prepare to introduce the skill, demonstrate and supervise participants as they practice it.

Prepare the following materials for this session: • Pregnancy, childbirth, postpartum and newborn care (PCPNC) guide • Laptop • Supplies (refer to the list of supplies for each clinical skills station)

Welcome and introductions; aim and objectives

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Slide 1: Title slide Slide 2: Aim Slide 3: Objectives Show slide 1 to welcome participants to the session and introduce all facilitators for the session. Present slides 2 and 3 to introduce the aim and objectives of the session.

Aim: To enable participants to enhance their knowledge and practical skills in selected ANC components described during this training.

Objectives: On completion of this session, participants will be knowledgeable and able to demonstrate how to perform the following relevant practical skills: • rapid diagnostic test for HIV; • rapid diagnostic test for malaria; • rapid test for syphilis; • dipstick urine testing for protein; • measuring blood pressure using correct techniques; • calculating gestational age and estimated date of childbirth (EDC) using a pregnancy wheel;

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

• determining the symphysiofundal height using tape or palpation; and • performing simple analysis of antenatal clinic summary data in order to monitor clinic performance and inform management decisions.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 155

Slide 4: Introduction Slide 5: Rights of the antenatal care patient

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Present these slides to introduce and discuss the basic considerations

regarding antenatal procedures and tests and the rights of the client.

Highlight the following issues: • Infection prevention practices must be observed when handling body fluids and conducting tests for infections. • The rights of the client, especially with regard to obtaining her consent and respecting privacy and confidentiality, should be observed.

Introduction for co-facilitators

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Preparing for each skills station • Each co-facilitator will be assigned to a specific skills station and remain there. Participants will rotate from station to station. • Use the checklist provided to check if the necessary test kits, clinical specimens and other test supplies and instruction sheets are available and ready. • Ensure that infection prevention supplies (gloves, disposable bins, decontamination supplies, soaps and hand washing facilities) are available.

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Demonstrating the skill • Read out loud the background information for the skill, where the relevant steps are described. • Demonstrate the skill, explaining what you are doing as you show each step. • Allow participants to ask questions about the demonstration and clarify any concerns. • Then ask a participant to demonstrate while another reads the steps aloud and you supervise the demonstration. • All participants must practice all the skills selected until they acquire the correct steps.

• Supervise and give guidance as needed as each participant practices the skill. • When all participants at the station have completed the skill, they will rotate to the next station and you will teach the next group that comes to your station.

Skill 1: Urine dipstick testing for protein (20 minutes)

### 154 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Materials and supplies Dipsticks (one per participant), fresh urine samples (including positive and negative samples), disposable gloves (one box) and five copies of instruction sheets (slide 6) and learning guides.

Slide 6: Instructions for skill 1 – checking urine for protein • Review the checklist provided above to ensure the materials are ready. • Read and familiarize yourself with the manufacturer’s instructions for use of the dipstick. • Welcome participants to the station and present slide 6. • Give participants five minutes to familiarize themselves with the learning guide and test materials. • Describe the performance steps as you demonstrate the skill.

• Allow participants to practice the skill using the learning guide provided. • Supervise the practice and answer any questions they may have.

## 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Learning guide for urine dipstick testing for protein

- Dòng dữ liệu: Steps; Yes; No; Remarks

Supplies and equipment • Prepare supplies and equipment Client preparation (in clinical setting) • Label a clean container with the woman’s name • Give the woman the labelled container and explain where she can urinate • Use language and terms that the woman understands to teach her how to collect a clean- catch, midstream urine sample.

Steps in the dipstick method • Wash your hands thoroughly and wear examination gloves on both hands • Dip the coated end of dipstick in the urine sample, completely immersing reagent areas, and remove it immediately • Compare reagent areas on dipstick with colour chart on the bottle label • Read the result accurately • Record the result.

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

End of procedure • Discard the rest of the urine sample following recommended infection prevention steps for liquid waste disposal. • Wash your hands thoroughly when ﬁnished. • Record the results on the woman’s chart and facility-based records. • Explain your ﬁnding to the client and the next steps, if any.

Skill 2: Rapid diagnostic tests (malaria, syphilis, hepatitis B, and HIV) (120 minutes)

Materials and supplies

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; RDT kits for malaria, syphilis, hepatitis B, and HIV (one each per participant) with relevant

instruction sheets

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Positive and negative blood samples for HIV and HBV

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Lancets to take finger-prick samples

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Pipettes

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Disposable gloves (one box)

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Learning guides

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Disposable bins

NB: Set up four sub-stations – one for each RDT; one co-facilitator for each sub-station.

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 7: Skill 2 – RDTs for HIV, HBV, syphilis and malaria • Check that all materials needed to conduct your RDT tests are available. Review and familiarize yourself with the manufacturer’s instructions for the RDT. • Welcome participants to the station and present slide 7. • Present briefly the background information on RDTs (see below and/or refer participants to the information in the participant manual.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 157

Rapid HIV test The rapid test indicates whether HIV-1 or HIV-2 antibodies are present. Results are available within 20 minutes. If one line appears on the strip, it means that the person is not infected with HIV (with 99.8% accuracy). If two lines appear, the person is likely infected (99.3% accuracy).

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

Rapid malaria test The RDT is used to determine if a person with malaria-like symptoms actually has malaria. The malaria parasites in the blood of an infected individual produce chemicals (proteins) called antigens. The RDT detects these malaria antigens in a person’s blood.

Rapid syphilis test The rapid syphilis tests currently available are treponemal tests. Their only disadvantage is that they cannot distinguish between active and past treated infections as the antibodies to treponemal antigens are retained for years. The treatment of all test-positive women will therefore result in overtreatment. Given the serious consequences of missed treatment, the benefits of treatment far outweigh the harm of overtreatment.

Rapid hepatitis B test Hepatitis B rapid screening test is a test for the qualitative detection of the hepatitis B surface antigen (HBsAg) in the whole blood.

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Although the specific instructions for processing each RDT may differ, most tests are performed with only three or four steps and often include the following: 1. remove the test kit from its wrapper and place on a flat surface; 2. add a specified amount of patient sample (whole blood, plasma or serum) to the sample well; 3. add a specified amount of diluent buffer (or reagent) to the sample well; and 4. read the results after a specified time (usually 15–20 minutes).

### 156 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Key steps • Introduce participants to the learning guide below and highlight the differences in steps and procedures for the various test kits. • Demonstrate the steps for each RDT whilst reading out the steps. Answer any concerns and questions. • Form teams of two: one partner will read the steps aloud while the other performs the test. • Allow participants to practice the test by following the steps in the guide. • Supervise the practice and answer any questions they may have.

## 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Learning guide for rapid diagnostic tests (RDTs)

- Dòng dữ liệu: Step; Yes; No; Remarks

Supplies and equipment • Refer to manufacturer’s instructions for the test. • Prepare the supplies and equipment needed for the speciﬁc test.

Client and specimen preparation • Explain the procedure to the woman, using language and terms that the woman understands. • Seek the woman’s consent to proceed. • Ensure privacy and conﬁdentiality for the woman. • Wash your hands thoroughly and put on examination gloves (on both hands). • If indicated, use aseptic technique to perform venepuncture to obtain 2–5 ml of blood from a vein as indicated in the speciﬁc test guide. • Dispose of needle and syringe in the sharps container.

### 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Steps in the test procedure • Read the instructions for each test device kit carefully and follow the steps. • Interpret the test results as directed. • Follow standard precautions to conduct the procedure and dispose of sharps and contaminated supplies safely. Wash your hands thoroughly.

Post-test procedure • Record results on the woman’s chart and facility- based records. • Explain the test results and give appropriate post test counselling.

Skill 3: Measuring blood pressure accurately

Materials and supplies

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Sphygmomanometer (3) with different size cuffs

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Pregnant clients (if available)

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Comfortable chair with back support and table

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Examination couch

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Learning guides

### 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 8: Skill 3: Measuring blood pressure Present the rationale for this station: • The accurate measurement of BP using the standard mercury sphygmomanometer with the Korotkoff sound technique depends heavily on the skill level of the person doing the reading, or the “observer.” • Other critical factors are: use of an accurate, well-maintained device; correct selection and positioning of the cuff; appropriate positioning of the

patient; and recognition of factors that may skew measurement. (Details in the participant manual) • Present slide 7 to explain the instructions for the station.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 159

Demonstrate BP measurement on a participant whilst describing out loud the following key steps.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; Select and use the proper cuff size.

### 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

2. Place the cuff over the bare arm. 3. Place the artery marker over the brachial artery. 4. Apply the cuff snugly, allowing room for no more than two fingers underneath. 5. Have the patient sit quietly for a few (5) minutes. 6. Do not talk to the patient whilst measuring her BP. 7. Support the patient’s back and feet and keep her legs uncrossed. 8. Keep the upper arm at the heart level with the lower arm passively supported (for example, resting on the lap).

9. Keep the arm still during BP measurement. 10. Inflated the cuff to at least 30 mmHg above the point at which the radial pulse disappears. Deflate the cuff slowly at a rate of 2 to 3 mmHg per second. (Deflation rates greater than 2 mmHg per second can cause the systolic pressure to appear lower and the diastolic pressure to appear higher). The first and last audible sounds should be recorded as systolic and diastolic pressure. In pregnant women, the fifth

### 158 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Korotkoff sound has been recommended as the diastolic measurement. 11. Deflate and remove the cuff when readings have been taken. 12. Document your BP measurement and educate the client on the results.

Divide participants into groups of two and task them to practice blood pressure measurement on each other in supine and sitting positions using the steps outlined above.

Ask them to compare the measurements obtained from the two positions.

Supervise the practice and answer any questions they may have.

## 160 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 160 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Skill 4: Calculating the gestational age and expected date of childbirth (EDC) using the pregnancy wheel (20 minutes)

Materials and supplies

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Pregnancy wheel (one each per participant)

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Learning guides

- Dòng dữ liệu: o; Case studies

Slide 9: Skill 4 – calculating the gestational age and expected date of child- birth (EDC) • Present slide 8. • Present the background information on the various methods for calculating gestational age and EDD. • Demonstrate how to use the pregnancy wheel using the first case in the exercise box in the participant manual. • Ask participants to find the EDC working along with you. Then ask participants to determine the EDC for the other cases in the exercise box.

### 160 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

• When all participants have had a minute to determine the EDC, ask one to tell the answer. If anyone does not understand how to use the wheel, the facilitator should go to that participant and help them individually. • Continue with the exercises. Make up additional exercises as needed until all participants have learned to use the wheel to calculate EDC. • Also demonstrate the other methods and ask participants to do some of the exercises using those methods.

Using the pregnancy wheel The pregnancy wheel is made up of two wheels: An outer bigger wheel with a calendar year outline at its rim. Dates are at weekly intervals.

An inner smaller wheel with pregnancy duration at weekly intervals, from week 0 to week 42. It has a mark/arrow indicating the LMP, which begins the dating (gestation week 0); and another indicating EDC (40 weeks gestation).

### 160 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

To determine EDC, position the mark for LMP on the inner wheel to correspond to the given LMP date on the outer wheel calendar. Locate the EDC date on the outer wheel by looking for the date that corresponds to the EDC marker on the inner wheel.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 161

Exercises and answers 1. Mrs A comes to the antenatal clinic on 3 January. She tells you that her last normal menstrual period started on 15 October last year. What is her EDC? (Answer: 22 July) What is her gestational age? (Answer: 11+ weeks)

2. Mrs B comes to the antenatal clinic on 15 May this year. She tells you that her last normal menstrual period started on 10 March. What is her EDC? (Answer: 17 December) What is her gestational age? (Answer: 9 weeks)

3. Mrs C comes to the antenatal clinic today. She tells you that her last normal menstrual period started on

## 10 March. What is her gestational age? (Answer: depends on today’s date)

### 10 March. What is her gestational age? (Answer: depends on today’s date) — phần 1

4. Mrs D comes to the antenatal clinic on 15 May this year. She tells you that her last normal menstrual period started on 1 January. What is her EDC? (Answer: 8 October)

5. Mrs E comes to the antenatal clinic today. She tells you that her last normal menstrual period started on

## 15 December. What is her gestational age? (Answer: depends on today’s date)

### 15 December. What is her gestational age? (Answer: depends on today’s date) — phần 1

## 162 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 162 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Slide 11: Skill 6 – data analysis to monitor ANC clinic performance • Present slide 11 to review the instructions for the station. • Present the background information and introduce participants to the summary data sheet and related questions (see below). • Also ask them to refresh their knowledge on the topic by reviewing the

## section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2.

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 1

Background information

The Lorgoligi clinic is located in Lorgoligi town, in the Zangara district of the northwest region of country A. The main occupation of the area is farming. The expected number of new pregnancies at the clinic in 2019 was 4850. Total live births recorded during the period was 3499.

Antenatal clinic at the facility is held from Mondays to Fridays. The clinic is manned by three midwives and a doctor who sees referrals. They have two consulting cubicles and a large open waiting area where general health talks/group counselling sessions are held. Women visiting the clinic frequently complain that they spend too much time at the clinic.

The clinic routinely offers IPTp-SP, iron/folate supplementation and screening for anaemia and infectious diseases. Unfortunately, they sometimes face stock-outs that affect these services. Using the information and data provided below, answer the following questions.

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 2

Questions 1. What is the ANC coverage for the six-month period of January to June 2019 (first and fourth visits)? 2. What proportion of women attending the clinic from January to June were teenagers? 3. Is teenage pregnancy a problem in Lorgoligi town? 4. What proportion of women booked for antenatal care during their first trimester? 5. List three care services that will be most affected if pregnant women in Lorgoligi do not book early in their pregnancy for antenatal care.

6. What proportion of women were anaemic at 36 weeks gestation (January to June)? 7. Has the strategy for the prevention and treatment of anaemia in pregnancy been successful in ensuring that women are not anaemic before delivery? Explain your answer? 8. What proportion of women attending the clinic were tested for syphilis over the six-month period? 9. What possible reasons can be given for the low positive-test rates for syphilis at the facility?

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 3

10. Do you think the clinic requires additional consulting cubicles and staff? Explain.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 163

Answer sheet: Review participants’ answers and supplement as needed with the following answers

- Dòng dữ liệu: 1.; What is the ANC coverage for the six-month period of January to June 2019 (first and fourth visits)?

Answers: first visit: 1866/3499 x 100 = 53.3%; fourth visit: 1551/3499 x 100 = 44.3%

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 6

- Dòng dữ liệu: 2.; What proportion of women attending the clinic from January to June were teenagers?

Answer: 433/1866 x 100 = 23.2%

- Dòng dữ liệu: 3.; Is teenage pregnancy a problem in Lorgoligi town?

Answer: Yes, one out of every five pregnant women attending the clinic is less than 18 years of age.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 4.; What proportion of women booked for antenatal care during their first trimester?

Answer: 488/1866 x 100 = 26.1%

- Dòng dữ liệu: 5.; List three care services that will be most affected if pregnant women in Lorgoligi do not book early in their

pregnancy for antenatal care?

Answers: 1. Screening and treating syphilis-positive mothers to ensure PMTCT 2. Correcting anaemia 3. PMTCT of HIV 4. Malaria prevention, including IPTp – 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: 6.; What proportion of women were anaemic at 36 weeks gestation (January to June)

Answer: 156/746 x 100 = 20.9%

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 7

- Dòng dữ liệu: 7.; Has the strategy for anaemia prevention and treatment in pregnancy been successful in ensuring that

women are not anaemic before delivery? Explain your answer?

Not in Lorgoligi. About 29% of mothers were anaemic at booking. If the principle is that no woman should enter labour with Hb < 11 g/dL, then care providers should have achieved a 0% anaemia prevalence rate at 36 weeks. The decrease in anaemia prevalence rates from booking was unsatisfactory.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 8.; What proportion of women attending the clinic were tested for syphilis over the six-month period?

Answer: 7/1866 x 100 = 0.37%

- Dòng dữ liệu: 9.; What possible reasons can be given for the low test-positive rates for syphilis at the facility?

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 8

Even though 1834 women were counselled, only seven tests were performed, with four (42.9%) positive. It appears that the facility experienced stock-outs of test kits as none was available or used from March to June.

- Dòng dữ liệu: 10.; Do you think the clinic requires additional consulting cubicles and staff? Explain.

Yes, the clinic requires additional staff and cubicles. The clinic attended to 10 845 client visits in the six- month period, averaging about 90 clients per day (10 845/20 working days x 6 months). With only two consulting cubicles, a midwife manning each cubicle attends to about 45 clients a day. If 10 minutes are spent with each client, then it will take seven hours 30 minutes for each midwife to attend to all 45 clients.

This is a very heavy workload, which explains the long client waiting times and dissatisfaction with services. It could also lead to staff burnout and poor-quality care.

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 9

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 165

Feedback at the end of the practice

Slide 12 • Slide 12: End-of-practice feedback session • Bring the class together for a feedback discussion at the end of the practice session, when all participants have been through all stations and have practiced each skill as required. • Ask the following questions to initiate the discussion. • How did they feel about the practice session? • What challenges will they or do they face providing these skills in their settings?

• Present slide 12 to summarize and encourage participants to brainstorm solutions to these challenges.

Summary and conclusions

Key messages (10 minutes)

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 10

Slides 13 and 14: Key messages • Present slides 13 and 14 to summarize the key messages as follows: • When performing clinical skills, care providers should respect the rights of their clients and be accountable for their actions. • Care providers must explain procedures/tests (planned and/or performed) to the antenatal client using language and terms that the client can easily understand. • Recommended infection prevention practices must be applied when performing all skills.

• When performing procedures and tests (such as RDTs), the provider must adhere to the recommended test protocols and steps to ensure valid and accurate test results. • All tests/procedures and their outcomes must be recorded on the woman’s maternal health record book and clinic-based registers. • Regular and periodic analyses of clinic data must be conducted at the clinic to inform clinic performance and management decisions.

### section on “Documentation and data management” in Session 2. — phần 11

• Measuring the extent to which evidence-based interventions are used in routine clinical practice provides a detailed picture of the strengths and weaknesses of antenatal service delivery at the facility.

## 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Session 14: Course follow-up and support Implementing ANC for a positive pregnancy experience

Prepare the following materials for this session: • flip chart with paper and markers • training course materials: facilitator guide, participant manual • laptop • overhead projector

An essential component of this course is the provision of continuing support to participants to enable them to implement their new knowledge and skills towards creating a positive pregnancy experience for their clients. In this session, the facilitator will work with participants to plan for follow-up and support.

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Note to the facilitator: • Agree with the responsible Programme Manager beforehand on the follow-up schedule and procedures based on the prevailing situation in the country. • The course director or another supervisor should tell participants how and when the follow-up will be conducted. • Be prepared to tell participants what to expect in terms of follow-up. (Will a facilitator visit them in their facility, or will they come to a meeting to discuss their progress?)

• The rest of the session describes the work that they should do in the interim to prepare for follow-up.

Welcome and introduction

Slide 1: Title slide • Present slide 1 to welcome participants to this final session of the course. • Explain that this is not an academic session but a preparation for actual implementation of lessons learned at their respective facilities, and for related follow-up and support.

Aim and objectives

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slides 2–4: Aim and objectives Present slides 2, 3 and 4.

Aim To help participants plan how they will apply the newly acquired knowledge and skills to provide ANC when they return to their own workplaces and, during follow-up, discuss important questions and challenges with a facilitator or other experienced providers.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 167

Objectives On completion of this session, participants will be able to: 1. describe, in an action plan, the actions they will take to implement antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience in their own facilities; 2. describe the difficulties that they anticipate with ANC implementation and how they will overcome them; and 3. describe how they will prepare an antenatal case study for presentation at a follow-up visit or session several weeks after the training.

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

4. During follow-up, participants will: • describe how they have implemented the ANC action plan in their own facilities; • present a case study of a pregnant woman and how ANC was provided to her; • discuss the lessons learned from the case study, emphasizing the importance of ANC in achieving positive pregnancy outcomes; and explain the importance of reflecting on practices to evaluate and improve care.

Challenges in implementing quality ANC for a positive pregnancy experience

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Explain that planning how to implement the newly acquired knowledge and skills is essential for being

able to apply them once you return to your facility.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; As expected, there will be several challenges to overcome during implementation of the newly learned

skills.

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slide 5 – 7: Challenges in implementing ANC for a positive pregnancy experience • Several factors can influence the successful implementation of quality antenatal care. They include the availability of specific skills such as those just acquired by the participants. • Ask participants to list other challenges that they may encounter when trying to implement their new skills in their settings. Note their answers on the flip chart. • Present slide 5 to summarize and enrich the list developed.

• Using slides 6 and 7, discuss each of the challenges listed with some examples.

### 166 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

Job guideline/policy restrictions • National policies and guidelines may restrict the performance of certain procedures to a certain category of health care workers. For example, doctors may be the only persons allowed to administer intravenous medication in some areas; laboratory technicians may be the only persons allowed to conduct HIV testing. Enabling midwives to perform the relevant procedures, such as HIV tests, may require some national policy revisions.

Staff skills and roles • Health care workers may not feel confident to perform certain ANC tasks because they do not feel competent. This gap may be overcome by competency-based training. On the other hand, some health workers may be accustomed to a certain role and be reluctant to change – even if they have the necessary skills. In this case, a supervisor may need to revise job descriptions/roles and have a meeting to discuss the new roles and the rationale for the change.

## 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Space and infrastructure requirements Many traditional antenatal clinics lack the necessary space and infrastructure to accommodate all ANC components within the cubicle or ANC area. Infection prevention requires that rapid HIV tests and syphilis tests, for example, are performed on a surface other than the consulting table.

Time and workload constraints The comprehensive nature of ANC requires that many more services are offered to the client at each contact by one care provider: abdominal examination, rapid tests, dispensing medications, and counselling. These can add to the workload, particularly in very busy clinics with few staff.

Logistics management Resources are very limited in many countries of sub-Saharan Africa. Frequent stock-outs are a challenge. Procedures that require certain supplies such as test kits or reagents may not be performed when the resources are not available.

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Preparing an action plan for quality ANC implementation in your facility

Slide 8–9: Developing an action plan for ANC implementation • Discuss the key considerations in developing a simple action plan. List and include the following questions: • WHAT change is needed? o List the changes that are needed in the set-up of the facility, including in consulting cubicles, laboratory services, medicines, supplies, or record-keeping. o List the changes needed in the way staff members are assigned to tasks and/or to antenatal clients.

• Also list the changes needed in the way staff members provide ANC. o HOW can the change happen? o WHO will take responsibility for implementing the change? o WHO will help implement the change? o WHERE will the action take place? o WHEN will the action take place? o HOW and WHEN will it be evaluated? • Present the sample chart in slide 9 to describe the format for an implementation plan of action.

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Class activity

Slide 10: Preparing the action plan • Present the instructions for the class activity and develop action plans using slide 10. • Divide the class into groups based on facility or type of facility. • Task each group to develop an action plan using the key considerations presented earlier. • Give 20 minutes for the activity. • Remind the groups that they will have to provide a copy of their plans to the course organizers for use in follow-up.

Review of action plans • When participants present their action plans, it is an opportunity to see what they have learned about antenatal care and what they have realized about their own clinics and practices. • Read over the plans with them and make suggestions as you feel appropriate. • Ask them to discuss the difficulties they anticipate and how they will overcome them. • Give them support for their ideas and enthusiasm.

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 169

Preparing for follow-up after training

Slide 11: Preparing for follow up after training Present slide 11. • Inform participants that a follow-up visit by the facilitator will take place four to six weeks after completion of this course. • Facilitators may elect to visit participants at their respective places of work or may organize a special session bringing together a group of participants. • At these follow-up visits or sessions, each participant will present: o an update on progress made in implementing their ANC action plan at

their own facility; and o a case study of a pregnant woman and how she was provided ANC.

Note

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Participants must ensure that they are ready for the follow-up visit by doing the following during the next

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

four to six weeks: o Track implementation of their action plan o Prepare case studies: refer participants to the “Guidelines for presenting an antenatal case study” in their manual

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Remind participants that they should be prepared to discuss questions about case management,

outcome, process and lessons learned.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Refer them to the discussion guide (Summary of case management) in their manual and ask them to

individually read the information in the box.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Encourage them to ask questions and clarify any concerns.

Follow-up visit

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; The follow-up visit by a facilitator(s) should occur four to six weeks after completion of this course. Facilitators

may elect to visit participants at their respective places of work or may organize a special session bringing participants together.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Participant role and activities

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

o At the follow-up visit or session, participants will review the implementation of their action plans and present a case study to demonstrate how they have applied their newly acquired ANC skills and knowledge.

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Facilitator role and activities. The facilitator must:

o observe the organization and set-up of the ANC clinic; o engage with other key stakeholders in the facility, including management; o observe the participant in her role as care provider to a client. This step is important as it provides an opportunity for the participant to receive individualized guidance; and o review progress made in implementing the action plan, encourage and support the participant.

### 168 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 7

Slide 12: The follow-up visit • Present slide 12. • Describe the roles and responsibilities of participants during the site visit: what participants must do and how. • Task three participants to read out loud each segment of the slide and emphasize the key points.

## 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

Feedback discussions and way forward: • Feedback discussions on site during the follow-up visit provides an opportunity for participants to: o share their experiences and discuss their action plan reports and case studies; o identify commonalities in the problems identified and solutions offered; and o initiate discussions under the following headings:

Lessons learned from case studies A. In cases where the woman had a positive outcome: • Which actions helped make the woman’s pregnancy safer? • What made these actions possible?

- Dòng dữ liệu: B.; In cases where the woman had a negative outcome:

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; What actions should have been taken?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; Why were the actions not taken?

- Dòng dữ liệu: •; What can be done to make sure that the correct actions are taken in the future?

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 2

Lessons learned from action plan implementation • Which strategies or actions facilitate successful changes in ANC practices? • Which approaches make change more difficult? • Make a list of recommendations for improving ANC practices. • Identify one thing about your own practices that you intend to change to further contribute to making pregnancy safer.

End the discussions by congratulating participants on their achievements. Encourage them to continue to work to overcome challenges encountered and offer solutions if necessary.

Summary and conclusions

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 3

Slide 13: Key messages (1) • Present slides 13 and 14. Ask three participants to read out loud the key mes- sages to conclude and end the session. • Planning how you will implement your new skills and knowledge is essential for being able to apply them once you return to your facility. • Several factors can influence the successful implementation of ANC for a positive pregnancy experience. Challenges that may need to be addressed include: o job guideline/policy restrictions

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 4

o shifting staff roles and responsibilities o space and infrastructure requirements o transfer of knowledge and skills to the workplace o time and workload constraints o logistics management. • A follow-up visit is planned to occur 4–6 weeks after completion of this course. At this visit, participants will present reports on progress made in implementing their action plans and a case study indicating how well they were able to use the newly acquired knowledge and skills in client management.

- Dòng dữ liệu: Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024; 171

End of course: reflections and closure

Present these “End of course” slides after participants have completed end-of-course assessments and evaluations to close the course.

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 5

Slides 15 and 16: End-of-course reflections • Present the reflections on slides 15 and 16 to end the course, and to motivate and encourage participants. • Participants need to follow the steps below for follow-up after the training event: 1. Remember the key lessons that they learned from this course, which will influence their ANC practice. (It may be a small thing, but small things can be very important building blocks for change).

2. Prioritize five (5) lessons learned in this course that you plan to share with colleagues at the workplace and describe how this will be done. 3. Identify key things about the ANC clinic where you work, which you intend to change to improve the quality of antenatal care to ensure a positive preg- nancy experience for your clients. Implement the change! 4. List the key challenges that may hinder this change and describe how you plan to overcome them.

### 170 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 6

5. Note: The impact of this training course will also be evaluated by comparing your achievements to the intentions stated in your action plan.

care for a positive pregnancy experience.

## 178 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024

### 178 Antenatal care for a positive pregnancy experience | Health worker training | Facilitator’s manual | 2024 — phần 1

- Dòng dữ liệu: Objective; Observations made; Comments and lessons

learned 1. Clinic set-up and infrastructure • Space adequacy • Client ﬂow • Ventilation • Cleanliness • Available washroom • Linkage to laboratory, pharmacy, etc.

2. Client –provider interactions • Welcome/reception • Client comfort • Privacy/conﬁdentiality • Interpersonal skills • Partner/support person involvement

3. Care components • Quick check/RAM oﬀered • Client assessment: history, exam and labs • Preventive care: /IPTp-SP, TT, Fe/folic • HIV test/PMTCT • Counselling: birth preparedness planning 4. Documentation • Client clinical record • Clinic registers/logs • Clinic summary indicators • Information charts

5. Emergency care • Emergency drugs • Emergency protocols • Referral system
